PART I

INTRODUCTION THE ROSE IN THE GARDEN OF FLOWERS 

What a beautiful flower. Yes, a Universal beauty.

All other flowers may show their beauty and try to take the place of the rose, but they never will. All the human race loves the rose. Yes, there are more beautiful colors in some other flowers; there are rarer flowers; there may be other flowers that give off a more pleasing aroma but after all when we have looked upon them all and have drank in all their beauty; after we have inhaled of their pleasing aroma; it seems we turn back to the rose and say "Yes, they are beautiful, but you are the queen of them all. It is not only your sweet beauty that tells the story; it is not only that sweet pleasing aroma that makes you so dear to the children; but it is your likeness and your symbol to the new born flower of the birth of a new born child that makes you the favorite flower of the human race. It is the purity of your formation as a bud. It is the secret of your beauty that you seem to hold. It is the anticipation you give to your owner that makes you what you are and puts you into a place all to yourself. As we gaze upon you in your first coming forth, we look upon you and you look so sweet, so pure, so innocent. You hold us in awe as you seem to smile upon us and say, 'I will keep you interested day by day and as I unfold myself you will be always wondering what I am xi

xii 

INTRODUCTION 

going to reveal tomorrow; as I seem to unfurl my petals for your benefit so you can see life illustrated.

Some will see it and understand; some will see me and see nothing but my beauty and drink in of my pleasant aroma; some may not see any beauty in me at all; yet, I go on day after day unfurling my lesson in life that those who might see will understand and benefit by my life. First, I am a child in its purity and within me is a hidden secret-that is my Soul. 

In a few days, I leave my childhood and expand into youth. As you see me burst forth in my youth, I show a little more of my beauty and spread a little more of my aroma which represent the child advanced to know it lives and realizes it has someone to love and someone to return that love; someone to return that sweetness I have thrown out to others who have an interest in me and feel me and give me drink and fondle me kindly and carefully. How I glory in this part of my existence. It seems my infancy in the bud was all right; but I could not see as

I wanted to see the ones who cared for me. I could not feel their kindly touch for I was too compact; but even then I felt I was taken care of and it was this feeling that gave me the instinct to be worthy of such care. So here I am in my youth today; and as I look upon the world I feel its greatness. As I drink in the warmth of the sunshine, it makes me try to come forward and show more of my loveliness. As

I get thirsty and feel those cool refreshing little rain drops fall upon me, I seem to drink in of the life they carry with them. What could I say, but thank you? What could I do but advance and spread more of my beauty to repay the others who are so kind to me? In a few more days, I am in the stage of ma-

INTRODUCTION 

xiii turity. How good I feel when I am compressed no more, when I feel that I have given all my beauty the essence of my life to those who cared for me. 

How I glorified in the life of expansion that I might make bare the life of man and woman upon the 

Earth. Then comes the greatest part of my life. I showed beauty in its purity; I showed youth in all its effection; I showed maturity in all its unfolding and showed life in its reality. The fourth stage of my life comes into being. The sun rays seem to bother me. The rain seems to agitate me rather than make me feel glad. The admiration of my friends have lost their earnestness and instead of saying, is not this a beautiful rose, they say, this was a lovely rose. 

I wish you could have seen it before it became old and tired and weary of its journey through life. To hear these things seems to hurt me and make me wish for the day when I can pass from their sight and not hear their comments; yet, if they knew as much as I do they would not speak thus for I know I have lived my time in this one life. I have given all I could seemingly give to make the lives of those who see me more pleasant. Do you think, since I am old and not so good to look upon as I was in the passing through from my infancy to what I am today, this is the end of my life, of my beauty, my pleasing aroma, of my vision of the sun, of my feeling the coolness of the rain drops. You are mistaken, for you know we understand creation. We are not as men are, we are of Creation and know the laws of Creation, and, therefore, we do not feel as sad as you think we ought for we know why we are here and we know we have a never-ending mission; so as you see me shed my beautiful dress, my petals,

xiv 

INTRODUCTION 

they will fall to the soil which gave them life for a short season and in their death they will give back to the soil that gave them life all the things the soil gave unto them. The soil will take in the leaf as it seems to dissolve and the soil will also drink in the aroma that it gave the rose. So you see this is not death; it is going through a mystery to those of 

Creation who were given power to think and see or not see as they choose. The rose, as it sinks into the mother Earth that gave it to us last spring is only taking it back to care for it through the times of cutting winds, heavy snow storms and killing frost. She, the mother Earth, will spread its warmth of protection over it until next spring; then she will unfold her arms once more and say, my children you can go forth once more and live again and show your beauty and give your sweet aroma to the children of

Creation so they too might come to the same common knowledge as we know that the passing on from seemingly life to death is not death at all; but a new and ever-Circle of life after life. As they, the human race, gives its body to the soil just as we go back to the soil is only the life within us will never die; but will arise up in a new body just as the Soul or life of the rose comes forth in a new-born rose. You see it is a continued Circle even as all things of Creation live in Circles. 

If all things of Creation live in Circles why should man be deprived of this ever-coming forward that it too as time goes on becomes more beautiful. So the garden of the children may receive more beautiful bodies as the Soul-mind advances into higher, cleaner, purer and more manly thoughts; for as the 

Soul-mind is so shall the body be.

INTRODUCTION XV 

I would say to you, after you have read this little mental picture, "try to cultivate the Soul-mind pictures beautiful. As sure as you do, so will your life be beautiful. As you beautify the life, the body must come forth as it should to be a credit to the life within. There is no limit to the beauty you can put into pictures as your days go on if you have the 

Truth of Life even as the 'Little Rose." "

PART I CHAPTER I THE HUMAN MYSTERY 

If we could see within this Rose, It looks so pure and bright— 

And read its future as it lives; 

Then we could guide it right.

Yes, the picture of the Rose in the garden is beautiful; but the duration of its life is but a few days then it fades away until the time of its new life in a few short months. 

Let us look at the Rose of Humanity. As we gaze upon it, what do we see but a mystery, a picture, an interesting small piece of Humanity; yet, as we look upon it we seem to try to look into its future and try to see beyond the closed petals of its future life. 

Only through the Path of Wisdom can we see a child as it should be seen. As we gaze upon it, it seems to open up before us and reveal the true self -the Soul-mind. We see the possibilities of its future life-providing it is not contaminated or its Soul-mind subdued by circumstances. Of course, all children who are brought into the world are not of the advanced Soul-mind; but there are many ad- 17

vanced souls born and they should be taken care of as a priceless gift. The mother who is fortunate enough to have such a child should be enlightened by some man of Wisdom so they will not hold back the Soul-mind from further advancement; but will try to understand it and be always ready to answer the questions it may ask in such a way that it will not have to memorize-but never forget. The questions the child will ask will enlighten the parents as to the plane of the Soul-mind.

The Soul-mind is the Creative part of each human child born.

Let us see just what a child is and how many parts there are to a child:

First-there is the father and the mother. They eat of the foods of the Earth; and by so doing make it possible to replenish the Earth. If there were only the two elements of the child then, although, it would have all the molding of a perfect child it would only be of the material and would have eyes but see not-ears but hear not-arms and legs but could move them not for the mind would not or could not function, therefore, that is the part that comes from the Earth and goes back to the Earth again as the petals of the Rose.

The Creator's part that puts life and action into the child is the Soul-mind. As soon as the Soul-mind takes possession, life becomes active and movement begins.

When the Soul-mind takes possession of the material brain, the child can see and hear and know it can move its arms and legs.

As the material parts of the body begin to mature, the Soul-mind begins to formulate mental pictures

THE HUMAN MYSTERY 

19 and picture material and the mind begins to understand the things it sees and is told. 

Advancement begins.

A child is born with a free will and for a few months uses it. Soon the mother begins to teach the child right from wrong; but this does not take away the free will-it trains the right side of the mind to think right and do right and puts it into the Path of 

Higher Thought.

As the child advances, it feels a love for the mother and a respect for her. 

From the age of two years on the Soul-mind reveals itself. If it is a bright intelligent child it will ask questions that will startle the mother; and she will be inclined to think that a child so young should not ask such questions-but, I say to you, as one who understands life from its beginning to its end "do not discourage your child. If you cannot answer its questions, say nothing for the time will come when that child will-if left alone-answer the questions for itself. It is the Soul-mind asking through the young brain the questions it has answered in lives before, and in due time, will answer them again. If the child asks a question you can answer, be sure to answer it correctly and in child language so it will understand."

Questions that come from an advanced Soul-mind will be such as these and the child may not be more than three or four years of age. He may startle you with asking-Why do the trees grow? Why do the leaves fall? From where does the rain come? Why do we have to eat? What makes the clouds; and why do they go across the sky? What do you mean,

when you say, "the sun goes down?" What made the mountains? 

When a child under six years of age asks these questions, you should know he is of the highest Soul-mind and should look upon him as a wonderful man to be; also encourage him at every year of advancement.

The unfolding has begun, Let us watch and try to see- 

Just how a Soul-mind can 

Unfurl a mystery.

THE DECISION 

21 

CHAPTER II THE DECISION 

The time has come when you and I 

The parents of this child: 

Shall decide his Path of Life, Whether it be right or wrong.

Let us look into a home and see a child and its parents, and hear a conversation: "Well, mother, our boy will soon be able to go to school. I hope he will be just as bright and intelligent going through school as he has been since he was able to talk. I have great hopes for him and can hardly wait to see him go. If he asks the teacher the questions he has asked us she will be up against something."

The mother says, "I do not know, but I have been wondering and worrying about the very time to which you are looking forward. To me he is so advanced, even now, it makes me wonder if school will be a benefit to him or not. You see, he has such a strong will and will not be subdued very easily. 

You know, I do not see life as you do. I believe in the coming back of Great Minds, and I do believe our boy is some great Soul-mind. I seem to think he will never need an education. Look at him nowhe knows more than most children who have been in school two or three years. When he goes, they will put him in with the first grade children. It seems so unfair. Another reason, I feel to see him

have to go, he will be forced into discipline. You know, you think everyone must have an education. 

I do not agree with you. Some children are born to reach their Ideal Life without an education; and I believe our child is one of these children. It just seems a silent voice is telling me not to let him go to school and it also says, 'you have money enoughwhy not take your child where he can read the Book of Creation-where he can answer the questions he has been asking you and you could not answer?" " 

Then the father with surprise answers, "do you mean to tell me you do not want our boy to have an education? Do you want to bring him up so he will know nothing? He would not be able to do anything worthwhile. Is it possible you could mean this?" "No, I mean, if we send him to school-in my eyes, he will be a failure and I would be the cause of his failure. I could not see my child have his wonderful brain banded by the writings of other men. 

I could not see my child have to study day after day the works of men whose minds should not be more wonderful than the mind of our boy. Why should he be forced to go to school and come out at the end -a molded man. Yes, molded just as every other child. He would just be of the many; and ever since 

I have been his mother, I have seen him a most wonderful man among the few who are wonderful. I knew the time was coming and it is best that we understand each other better when our boy's life is to make or mar. I am going to ask you a question. 

You are his father. Does he have to go to school or not? I will leave you to decide; but before you do let us have a party of children. We will ask all the

THE DECISION 

23 brightest children we know who have been going to school-not less than two years. After the party you can decide." "Yes, but how is that going to bring the decision to a close." "All I can say is, 'wait, and you will see.'"

The children are gathered together and the proud mother gives each child a chance to show itself in its best form. She calls upon her child who is interested in all the other children and says to him, "all these little boys and girls have said pieces and sang their songs, what are you going to do for them?" "Oh, I do not know. I would rather not do anything, mother, if I just thank them, will that not be enough?" 

His mother says, "yes, dear, maybe that is enough." 

So he thanks them with these words: "I thank you all for the wonderful things you have done and said tonight.' 

The children all go home. 

Mother, father and son are alone once more.

She looks at her husband and he looks at the son then says, "why did you not recite for those boys and girls tonight?" 

He looks up at his father and says, "you see,

Daddy, the things they said and the way they sang those songs seemed so childish. I did not want to show off before them.' 99 

Then the father looks at the mother and says, "can he recite poetry and sing songs?" 

She says, "yes, lots of them; but he said he would never tell his Daddy or he might think he was showing off."

Then the father says, "Mother, you win. I see through the mist that has been put before my eyes by the world. I could not think of having our boy brought down to the level of the children we saw tonight. When do we start to break the law by not sending our boy to school?" 

The mother says, "Oh, I have had that part looked after. We can get a governess and she can take the place of the school teacher; so you see we will not have to break any law. I want to bring our child up in a life of want to not have to. 

I was thinking, if you would find a place on a mountain somewhere, we could build a nice little home with one large room in it for our boy. We will have a beautiful view of the mountain and the valley for him to look upon when he is outside. He will see the clouds below the mountain and see them roll up the mountain and ascend into the Heavens then he will be able to see thunderstorms both above and below. He will see the water coming out of the mountain side and the beginning of the rivers. He will see the trees that have lots of different shades of green, the wild flowers and the different kinds of birds and different insects. He will see the beautiful sunset and the rising of the moon; and have a clearer view of the stars. He will have lots of fresh air to breathe at all times. Then, in his room, we will put books from different authors and pictures of all things we know will interest him. If he is as other boys, by doing this, we will find out in a very short time just what he wants. We will put different instruments of music there; but he shall not be taught music. His governess shall only instruct him in the principle of numbers and the alphabet. He must

THE DECISION 

25 learn of himself how to read and to use the figures he may need. There will be no routine in his daily life. He will not be forced into doing anything for this is the path I wanted to follow when I was a child but could not; so as I 'wanted' so shall he 'have.' We will watch a life of 'want to' and not 'have to.'" 

What a mother!

She is leading, unconsciously, her boy into the 

Path of Wisdom. 

The Path of Wisdom for her boy 

Was this mother's vision clear. 

As time goes on, we shall see, If the voice she did really hear.

CHAPTER III THE MOUNTAIN HOME 

To look from a mountain 

Down to the plain below, Brings a mind into action; 

And Wisdom into it will flow.

The home is built. 

The governess, the mother and son move to their mountain home. As they ascend the mountain, the boy is all interested and the natural expansion of a 

Soul-mind begins.

When they arrive at their new home, he wants to know if this is where they are going to live or how long they are going to stay; how the mountains are made; how the trees grew into the mountain sides; will it ever get dark; can he play outside; when will he have to go to bed; when will he have to get up in the morning; will it get cold here and will he be able to play as long as he likes. When he sees his room, he looks around in amazement for everything he had at home in the city was here for him. 

The next morning his life begins. His governess is there to greet him; and she is just as much interested in this little 'Bud of Humanity' as the parents.

It is going to be a new experience for her too, as she says, "very odd, yet interesting. 99 She sits down and reads the instructions from the mother. She reads: "You must not force him to do anything-ask him if he wants to. When out walking with him,

THE MOUNTAIN HOME 

27 if he asks you a question that borders on the plan of 

Creation-say to him 'you will be able to answer that question yourself, just wait, and as you grow up, you will know it of yourself-then it will be yours."

Strange instructions; but they must be obeyed.

CHAPTER IV FATHER'S AWAKENING 

I wonder if this father will see,

The man in his little son to be. 

Two weeks later, the father comes to spend the week-end.

After the greetings were over, the boy who is now six years of age asks his father to go for a walk with him. 

As they walk the father asks, "Son, how do you like your new home?" "I think it is beautiful here. You should see the sunsets! They are wonderful; and the moon seems so big and shows such a nice light. Do you not notice the trees are different from the ones back home? My teacher counted sixteen shades of green for me yesterday. And, Daddy, you should see the clouds! They are away down there, sometimes. 

Why they do not stay there all the time, I do not know. I asked my teacher; but she said some day I can answer that question myself. She says I have to be a great man some day to please my mother and daddy. You should see those clouds come rolling up the side of the mountain and when they reach the top it makes everything wet. I have to go in when it comes to the top, mother says I might take a cold. 

You should see the insects, yes, and everything."

The father thinks-what a conversation for a child of six.

FATHER'S AWAKENING 

29 They return to the house. 

The boy says, "Father, come in and see my room.' 

As they enter the room, the father asks him what the different pictures represent; and he tells him the name and meaning of each picture. 

The father smiles and says to himself, "yes, she knows more than I do. This is surely a new way to bring up a child; but I think it is going to be all right." 

The father gets a glimpse 

Of the man his son may be;

If he leaves him to his mother- Even if it is a Path of Mystery.

CHAPTER V THE GENTLE SURPRISE 

Once more they are back to the city; 

Where other children live. 

Then let us watch these children; 

And compare the lives they live One has followed the life of 'have to,' While the other the life of 'free will.' 

After three years. Yes, but only a child of nine.

They decide to take him home for a month or two in the summer; and when they arrive home there is one natural boy in the city, at least. A boy without the mask of over-study or the mask of suppression by the law of discipline.

Let us compare him with the boys of his own age: 

He is clean, pure and strong. His eyes shine with a light of understanding and intelligence. He has no fear of anyone he meets. He is healthy and so natural that he stands out alone from the other boys. 

They in turn look upon him and wonder. When he talks, he uses only the simplest words and can converse with either boy or man. The father is proud of his son and takes him among his friends; but he sits quiet and listens to their conversations-never speaks unless he is asked a question then answers it to the point.

The father suggests a party once more. This time, not to decide a question; but to see the difference between his boy and the boys who have gone to school.

THE GENTLE SURPRISE 

31 They come to the party; and each one is given a test question and a prize is given to the one who shows the most intelligence. His boy will not enter the contest. When asked by his father, he tells him it would not be fair to the other boys as he could answer any question or spell any word put to him; and he would not deprive any of the others of the present. He did not need the present; so again he sits and listens. Yes, a good listener.

After the supper is over, all the boys go into a room alone to have games; but the room is soon quiet. One voice only can be heard.

The father looks into the room and sees all the boys sitting around his boy listening with their eyes and mouths open. His boy is telling them of the mountains, of the clouds, the sunsets, the beauties of the moon and the waters that come out of the mountain. He describes the trees and the different flowers and tells of the distance you can see and about the different birds and imitates their song almost to perfection. He tells of the noises the different insects make at night.

He paints mental pictures for these boys just as he has seen them in reality.

They all sit and listen to him until very late. 

The father walks into the room and says, "do you boys not think it is time to go home? It is past your bed time."

They get up to leave and as each passes the boy of the evening thank him for the wonderful story he has told them and ask when they can come again. 

He smiles upon them and says, "when you want to."

At these words his father looks at him and his

mind goes back to the night of his decision three years ago. The mother had said, "I want to bring my boy up 'to want to' not 'to have to.'" He could see now what she meant and how big and wonderful it is to be big because you 'want to.' 

He asks his boy why he used these words. 

His reply is: "I want only those who want to come of their own free will." 

His father says, "they will surely want to."

The Soul-mind that is advanced 

Does not try to show what it knows;

But when it speaks, it surely gives 

Its audience a Thrill.

CHAPTER VI THE GREATER REVELATION 

As time goes on from year to year, In this once tiny piece of mystery- It shows itself in Light and Truth; 

Just as a Soul-mind should. 

A few mornings after, at the breakfast table, the boy looks at his mother of whom he is so proud and says, "Mother, I want to go back to the mountain home as soon as you want to. The city seems to smother me. The people seem so strange to me. 

They seem to have nothing to talk about. They do nothing. I am lonesome, when I am with the people.

The things they do seem so small. To me, all they do is to be in a hurry to go-nowhere; and to read to know nothing. The boys are nice but they are down the line so far I cannot get down to them. 

I want to be where I have to go up to someone who knows more than I; someone who can tell me greater things than I know; where I can see not only human beings and houses but the beautiful true picture of 

Creation. I want to hear the voice of the winds. 

I want to sit and listen to my song birds-they can tell me so much more than the boys. I want to hear the insects singing their song in their own way. I want to hear the voice of the mountain and the music of the water as it leaves the spring. I want to smell the pleasant aroma of the pine trees and the wild flowers. I want to walk once more upon the branches under my feet and listen to their crackle. I am longing for the lovely sunsets, to be able to see the moon

once more, to watch the stars in the Heavens and listen to their stories for they do tell me stories and paint me beautiful pictures. I hear the mountain calling me. Mother, I do believe I am getting homesick for my real companionship. Whenever you 'want to,' mother, take me back home."

The father, after he had listened to his son in silence, said, "I do not like to see you go so soon; but if it is calling we must submit to the call, Mother, when will it be?" "Oh, I think we can be ready by the day after tomorrow. Can you not go with us and rest for a week or two?"

So once more they arrive at their mountain home. 

The son sheds tears of joy. 

As the father watches, his heart swells with joy and a new-born thought. He, too, sees the great 

Soul-mind in his boy. He is satisfied that no child. at the age of nine could see and know what this child knows. He wonders what voice his boy talks about. Is it possible for such things to happen? We will see, as years pass by, how this mystery is revealed unto him.

He spent three more years on the mountain. 

At the end of this time, his governess reports to his mother that there is nothing more she can teach her boy. He has excelled her in everything also every line of education. She knew and felt it her duty to tell the mother. She did not want to leave her pupil; but she felt guilty taking wages for something she was not doing. Since the pupil became her teacher-and he surely had taught her to see life in its true form and read the "Book of 

Creation" as she never dreamed could be possible.

"You should hear him talk, when we are alone. His thoughts get bigger and more beautiful. If you should hear him give a mental picture of the formation of the mountains, you too would realize what a wonderful boy you have and how little you know.

I am inclined to think your theory is true when you talk of Reincarnation. It seems impossible for a boy of twelve to accumulate so much-not educationno, it must be Wisdom-for the things he says have never been written in books. If you will let me stay with you, as a sort of companion without wages, I will be under a life's obligation to you. I do not want to leave neither you nor your boy. If you will only say 'stay,' how happy I will be." 

The mother said, "here he comes. Let us hear what he has to say about it. Son, your teacher has handed in her resignation. She says, you do not need her anymore as you have gone through everything she has to teach you; so she will be preparing to leave shortly." 

He looks at the teacher-"Do you want to go?" 

She looks at him and tears come into her eyes as she says, "no." "Then why talk about it? If you want to stay, why talk about leaving?"

She explained her position to him. 

He smiled and said, "what do you say, Mother? 

Do you want her to go?" "No." "Then we will mention it no more. It is a thought of the past, and, since it was an unpleasant thought, let us forget it. I need you to sit and listen to me, sometimes, when I get so full I have to have an audience. We will keep you here as a good listener."

Just then he sees his father's car coming up the mountain and he goes to meet him. 

The father has not seen his boy for three months and it is a real meeting between father and son. 

The rest of the day is spent talking over family affairs.

The mother notices her husband is not looking as healthy as he should and asks him if his health is good. "Yes," he says, "I am feeling all right," then sinks into silence once more.

She feels worried and invites him into another room to rest after the long drive. She asks him why he drove his own car so far-why did he not come on the train or bring his chauffeur with him. 

He has to tell her the truth. The bank closed on him and he is afraid they will lose all the money they have. He will have to cut expenses, somehow. 

She tells him about the governess and their agreement. Maybe they can keep on living here or they could go back with him to the city.

They go to the city, after the father had told the boy the reason he was anxious to return home. The boy said he wanted to. 

The governess goes with them so he would not be compelled to go to school by law.

The next few years pass. He helps his father in his business and surprises him with the ability he shows in a short time. 

At the age of sixteen, he becomes unsettled and is now free from the law of schooling. The governess is gone, and he is alone so much his mother is worried about him. He speaks to her one morning and when she looks at him there are tears in his eyes.

She asks him what the trouble is and he tells her he wants to go to the Home on the Mountain. He cannot sleep for the Silent Voice is constantly calling him. He must go. He can go alone and no harm would come to him for he is not afraid. They talk it over with his father; but the boy said he wanted to go and must go, so they had to let him go.

While on the train, who should he meet but his teacher. He sat down beside her and after a pleasant greeting she asked him where he was going.

He told her how the Silent Voice kept calling and he had to come. 

She smiled and said, "it must have been the Silent

Voice that has been calling me too and I had to come." 

He said "what do you mean? Are you, too, going up to the mountain?"

She said, "yes, that is the only place I know from where a Silent Voice can call, so I am going there." "We can live together. What a wonderful Creation this is that the Voice can call two at one time to go to one place and those two to be you and I. 

How wonderful! You know, when I get there, I feel as if I will find a new thought. The Voice seems to tell me so.' 99 "I know you will and I am, you know, a good listener."

Their joy at meeting one another puts them into silence until they arrive at the home on the mountain. As they arrive, the sun is just going down and they both stop and gaze at its beauty before they enter the cottage and a deep emotional feeling passes through both of them and they say the same words at the same time "how wonderful Creation is!"

They watch the sun until it passes from their vision then they enter into their home and they seem to have found their real place in life. 

After supper, they take a walk to their old resting place where they had spent many hours when she was the teacher and he the pupil; but they have changed places as he is the teacher and she the pupil or his audience as he used to call her the last few days they spent together before their forced departure.

As they sit side by side, neither speak a word for it seemed a long time; then she smiles and says to him, "are you glad to be back home again?"

He does not speak; but she sees him drying the tears from his eyes so she arises and says, "when you are ready, come home, I will be waiting for you and we can have a little music before we retire." 

She leaves him and he cries out as if his heart would burst with emotion-"Oh, thou Great Creator, will I ever understand your Creation? Will 

I ever know the principle of thy Creation? If it be possible to man, then I will for I 'want to.'"

As he says these words, there seems to be a feeling surrounding him such as he has never felt before and a Silent Voice says to him, "seek Wisdom and all things shall be revealed unto thee."

He then asks, "where is this Wisdom I have to seek?" 

The Voice says, "open thine eyes and thou shalt see the Truth in everything for thine eyes shall look beyond the conscious vision and thou shalt see the principle of all things-not as the masses see; but only as the eyes of Wisdom see. Listen and thy

hearing shall become more keen and above the noise and clamor of the world thou wilt hear the Silent

Voice of all Creation revealing their mysteries to thee. Thou shalt find a power of speech and will talk to the people of the things that are being revealed unto thee. As you listen, the mountains will tell you how they were formed, the trees will tell you their story, the heavens shall be a symbol of greatness to thee and the great secret to the Path of Wisdom is so easy all you have to do is listen. Do not go into silent communication-realize you cannot tell the Creator anything. Since He created all things, do not waste your time trying to tell Him. 

Since He is the Creator, He did write a book, and as each leaf of this book is turned you do not have to read-just listen and each picture speaks for itself. 

As you listen, all hidden secrets will come from their hiding place and say to you, 'I have kept my secret long waiting for someone of the Creator's Supreme Creation to come along who would understand my voice and manner of speech; and since you have found the Path of Wisdom, I am a secret no more.'

His friend had waited a long time, yet, he has not returned. The night is getting late so she goes out to see why he does not come. When she gets near him, she sees him sitting as she had left him. She puts her hand on his shoulder and says, "do you not feel tired? Do you know what time it is?"

He starts with surprise, at the touch of her hand and the sound of her voice. He arises and goes with her into the house. 

When she sees him under the light of the lamp she sees a new boy. Yes, a boy with a face shining with the smile that can only come from the awaken-

ing of a beautiful Soul-mind who has had its vision restored and understands the Path of Truth. 

She asks him, "why so happy?"

The very fire of truth sparkles from his eyes as he looks upon her and says, "I have been born again. 

My Soul-mind has come into its own. I am in touch with the Great Power of Creation. Its principle has again been revealed unto me. My hearing has become more keen and I can hear the Silent Voice at all times. My tongue has been loosened that I might speak of the truth of the Earth to the people. All 

I have to do is not to think or study. All I have to do is listen to the Book of Creation and it will tell me all things; and, too, it says "I will not have to memorize for I will never be able to forget anything 

I hear. As I travel, which I will do in the future,

I will not have to listen to other men's ideas and thoughts for I will get the true knowledge at first hand. From now on, the Creator is my teacher so what can man tell me. I must cease to read any man's book. From this time on, I will have to forget the books of yesterday and read only the book of tomorrow a book without an end for it was written in the beginning and will continue to be always 'The 

Book of Tomorrow.' Man's books are just man's thoughts; but the Creator's book is a book of Wisdom and Truth-what a revelation!" "You must get ready for bed. I could sit and listen to you all night; but we would be too tired and weary tomorrow so we better retire-if you 'want to." 999 

At these words, he smiles and says, "I want to." 

A Light of Life and Truth can only come

From the Voice that speaks so silently -That only I can hear,

CHAPTER VII LOOKING BACK 

In looking back just a few years, We see a tiny child; 

But as he lays and sleeps tonight, We see the youth appear.

As they sleep, let us look back to the beginning of this youth who is fast becoming a Master-mind. As we see him as he was at first-only a little piece of 

Humanity with a free will and sparkling eyes that seemed to say as they looked around, "this is not strange to me. I have been here before."

What a mystery he was to us then! We have watched the unfolding of the petals as they let loose and spread out. We can see the process now as we have watched his advancement.

Is it not wonderful of the mother to know the truth of this child and of the father to give his consent for the mother to lead her son into the Path of 

Wisdom? It is a beautiful awakening to see her son growing up just as she saw him as a Bud of the 

Garden of Humanity.

Here he is back to the home on the mountain once more; and even when he worked with his father in his business he excelled all the others who were in this line for years before he came. He soon saw loop-holes in his father's office and advised changes to a more simplified routine. He always gave his father credit for the changes. Yes, he fit in anywhere as he is 'Just Natural'-never changed-

but in his naturalness caused the masses to look upon him and say to themselves, "Yes, he seems so natural, so intelligent, his conversations are always wonderful and his calmness is almost unbelievable. His smile is true for you can see it comes from within. 

He answers any question you may ask him and when the answer is given you have to sit quiet for there is no comeback." "Yes, his parents should be proud of such a son.

You see, the masses are also watching the petals of time spread out; they too want to see what the secret is that was held within that one tiny piece of Humanity "The Human Rose Bud." As they compare him with their own, they wonder why there can be so much difference; yet, there he lays sound asleep, unconscious that the eyes of the masses are upon him. Even in his sleep, that smile is seen. Yes, the smile of Natural Self for he knows no "make believe"-he is a life of "want to" not "have to."

He is not a cog wheel of the industry of the world, he is the axle of a wheel of Wisdom that will, as it goes around, accumulate a power only known to the Master-mind. By his life, he shall give Light to the world by revealing the Truth of Creation. 

Morning has come. The sun is flooding his room with its sunbeams. He looks up and sees the brightness of the sun. He lays for a while and his mind recalls the night before. He smiles and gets up with a feeling born anew within him. Soon he is called by his Aid as he calls her.

Breakfast is ready and they meet at the breakfast table. She asks him how he slept. 

He says, "well, but I had a beautiful dream or vision; and I know by that vision the Voice that

LOOKING BACK 

43 called me here was my own Soul-mind calling me into the Path of Truth that I have to follow. I dreamed I was traveling and everywhere I went the people were asking me to feed them. I offered them bread; but they said, 'no, we have enough bread.'

It was truth they needed. They wanted a light so they might know where they were going. They said 'we are just stumbling through life in a mist and it is so dense we cannot see where we are going. We are told this and that and everyone is showing us a different path but no one seems to have the Light that surrounds thee. Give us of your truth. Put us into the Path of Light and feed us by thy tongue for we know you have the truth.' "I answered them thus: 'Yes, I will feed you. 

Yes, I will give you light. Yes, I will reveal all the mysteries of the Earth to you; even as they are being revealed unto me for as I receive so shall I give.' 

As I uttered these words, they all seemed to be satisfied. Just then I awoke and the sun was shining in my face in all its glory. What a dream! What an awakening! The Light of Life filling me to overflowing." "Well, when you feel you are going to have an overflow, remember I am your audience." "The first thing I will do this morning is write to my mother and will she not be surprised when she knows I met you on the train and you were coming back to our home at the call of the Silent Voicejust as I was? Will she not be pleased that I have you to take care of me and listen to me. I know she will be so happy. I have to write now, I will see you later."

I will be able to repay them in money for what they have done for me." "You will." "I want to' so much. I will see you later after 

I have finished my letter."

She also writes a letter to his parents and tells how she saw the advertisement in the papers offering their home on the mountain for sale; and how she felt about it and made up her mind she would buy it and keep it until they got back to prosperity again; then she would let them have it back. It held memories that she would never forget and the surprise she got when she saw their son on the train but she would let him tell the story of their meeting for he could tell it so much more effectually than she could. She hoped, by this time, her lawyer had been there and had made the necessary arrangements for the exchange of the property. She would stay there and keep their boy as long as he wanted to stay; but to be sure and not let him know of the sale or he would not stay. She hoped she had done the right thing-as she thought she had. 

To find a friend like this in life, Is to find a friend indeed;

When it comes, without a thought 

Of recompense or greed.

CHAPTER VIII 

A FRIEND INDEED

Let us see this friend, as time goes on- 

I wonder what she will find:

Why-Wisdom, Power and Love for all mankind.

Let us look into the home the day before the letters arrive.

We see the father and mother and a stranger in the house. He is asking a question about the sale of their property on the mountain. "Yes," the father said, "we have to sell." "What do you ask for it?" "$2,000.' 99 "Does that include the land and buildings on the land ?” "Yes, it cost us lots more; but you know it is hard to sell anything in this time of no money so we are anxious to sell." "All right," the stranger says, "my client will take it at your price. When can we have the deal put through ?" 99 "As soon as possible, Sir. We need the money.' "You can have a check for $1,900 today and a check for the other $100 when the deal is closed. 

These are my orders from my client so here it is. I will get busy and have the deed of sale recorded tomorrow."

He leaves. The mother looks at the father and

he at her, with the check in his hand and tears in his eyes. 

He says, "what about our boy? Yes, and he loves that mountain home. What will he say, when we write and tell him it is his home no more?"

The mother speaks. Her emotion is keen; but as she speaks you can see her seeing her boy as he isso natural. "He will pack, leave and come home. 

He will not complain."

As she understood him when a child so does she understand him in his youth. Yes, and she will always understand for she is of the higher plane just as he.

The next morning, as they were sitting at the breakfast table, two letters were handed to them by the mail man. Two, yes, and both from the mountain. They opened the boy's first and read it with interest; then opened the other and also read the contents of the letter from the girl who once they hired as a governess to their child. They both got up and put their arms around each other and wept tears of joy and understanding such as they had never done before.

The mother then says, "since I cannot be with my boy there is not another person I would rather have with him than the one whom I chose to look after him as a child so all is just as it should be. 

The boy does not know of the exchange. She cares for him and tries to be everything to him that he may need for she too sees the Light of Life and 

Truth. Her life as she says is entirely changed and that change is for the better."

CHAPTER IX A STEPPING STONE to the World 

As you take each step 

Into this world so cold; 

Be careful before you plant your feet 

Upon the stone ahead.

Let us take a look again into the house on the mountain. 

Yesterday, they did not go out for a walk. They had too much to talk about in common, telling of their days spent apart. They went to bed early; but next morning he got up early and said he wanted to sing with the birds. He took his morning walk through the pines and the song of the birds again greeted him. He began to sing with them; and the birds seemed to remember for the more he sang the more they sang and what a morning that was for him as he fed them for their song. When he sat, he could not help but express himself: "Creation is beautiful, and it seems to be getting more beautiful as time goes on."

Yes, because his eyes are being opened more and more; his understanding is getting more advanced; his hearing is getting more keen for the Voice is with him always and everything he sees shows itself in beautiful mental pictures. As each picture comes forth, he seems to see its beauty more and see the principle of Creation in them that tells of the Circle of Life in everything that is upon the Earth.

A STEPPING STONE TO THE WORLD 49 

As he sits and looks upon the trees once more, he wonders if the mystery of the tree can be revealed; and as he is wondering the tree seems to talk to him using that same small Silent Voice and it seems to whisper, "there is no mystery to me, listen, and I will tell you all. First, we came from a seed which had fallen from another tree of the same family. 

This seed fell upon the ground then was carried away by wind or rain until it found a place where it could bury itself or was buried by the moving soil. 

When left in peace, I began to decay. I had, within this shell of the Earth which was now going back to the Earth, a small speck called Life; and life in anything cannot be destroyed for it is the Creator's gift to all things that live-so when the decaying process was going on around me, I came forth to live once more. This time, a family tree, and not just one of the family. The season has lots to do with my life for life needs light and rain and warmth to encourage me to come forth and face the elements. Springtime comes and I take courage and the process begins to make me into a family tree. Since I am of nature and nature abhors a vacuum the sun becomes hotter and more powerful as it advances towards the summer time and as it draws my moisture from my mother, the Earth, she draws so hard that it pulls out the moisture at the top of every branch of me. I go after that vacuum and try to fill it up so, really, it is vacuum that makes me grow-just as surely as it takes vacuum to cause movement in everything. I go on and upward. I also spread out wherever I find a vacuum. The summer comes and

I spread myself with leaves to shade myself from the rays of the sun and also to catch the drops of

rain and drink them in so my thirst will be quenched. 

Thus I live. The summer is here and I am at full maturity for this one season; and my mission then is to show how beautiful I can become that the 

Creator's Supreme Creation, Humanity, can see my beauty. The sun seems to be trying to get away from me again and I notice, as the days pass on, instead of the warmth of the sun I feel the cold winds of the autumn; and soon I begin to know that my time is short in this life. Since I am of Creation and know the Truth of Creation, I know when we have lived this life we will live again next year; so to show we have no regret at the 'passing on' we change the color of our leaves so when the people look upon us as we are near the 'passing on' they seem to say that we are more beautiful in death than we were in life. Do you know why?—I will let my leaves speak for themselves, and they speak thus: 'yes, in the spring, we came forth in the bud but as the spring rains fell we drank of it and the heat of the sun gave us encouragement to advance and as we did we formed into a beautiful leaf to fulfill our mission. The wind blew on us, the rain poured upon us, the sun beat upon us, sometimes great storms tried to pull us from our duty; but we held on until we felt the sun's warmth was leaving us, the cold winds blew upon us and our mother, the Earth, began to draw from us the life we had our mission was coming to a close. It is then we change our color with a smile for we know we will soon have our liberty and freedom and as we shake ourselves loose, we will feel that feeling of falling, falling until we lay upon our mother, the Earth, once more. 

She will draw us within herself and take the life

A STEPPING STONE TO THE WORLD 51 

part of us into her keeping for she knows the winter is at hand and she wants us to hide from the storms until the spring comes once more. She will then send the life part of us up to her branches, yes, to the very top to show our life once more. Yes, the same life but not the same leaf-the leaf of last year went back from whence it came. It was of the Earth and back to the Earth it must go; but the life goes on forever. So you see we live in a Circle just as does all other life-no matter whether it be man, beast or vegetation, the life goes on and on returning, yes, ever returning until the end of time.' " "The tree knows so much, why does not man know this also?"

As he asks that question within himself, the Silent Voice answers him thus: "After all things of Creation has told you its story and there is no other Creation to speak that speaks truth, only then will the Voice of Wisdom reveal life, death and life after what we call death-and the truth shall be spoken unto thee for the Voice of Wisdom speaketh truth only. As you are now in touch with the Voice of Creation, listen, for many things that seemed deep and mysterious will open up for you and will see there is no mystery. No, not even in the 

Earth anywhere nor in the Heavens. The storehouse of the Earth will show you how everything man needs on the Earth was put there by Evolution and time to fulfill the Creator's Creation. What a wonderful Creation!" 

Then he, the boy, burst forth in a thankful prayer: "Thanks, Oh, thou Great Creator. Thou who

didst create the Universe. I thank thee for thy wonderful Creation. "I thank thee for bringing man forth; and giving him dominion over the Earth which is part of thy Creation. "I thank thee for the free will thou gavest man. "I thank thee for putting good and evil upon the

Earth that man might of his own free will be good or evil. "I thank thee that thou meant man when thou said 'man;' and not sheep as man appears in the eyes of men of the truth. "I thank thee for putting greatness and smallness upon the Earth so man may choose whether he shall be great or small, of his own free will. "I thank thee for filling the storehouse for man; and if this is not evenly distributed, it is not thy will, as thou gave the free will to man so man makes the unequality—not thee. "I thank thee for giving man a mind without limit as far as the knowledge of this Earth goes and the things his eyes can see. For thou gavest man a 'Path of Wisdom' to follow if he wants to and a promise that if he seeks wisdom all things shall be revealed unto him. "I thank thee that thou put love and hate, sorrow and gladness, sickness and health, advancement and failure, trust and mistrust, sunshine and rain, moonlight and darkness; I thank thee for all the things thou put in thy Creation that man may have the will of himself to choose the right or wrong, the lesser or the greater. "I thank thee that thou art great and good; and I

A STEPPING STONE TO THE WORLD 53 

know if I am punished this punishment is inflicted by myself and not thou. "Since you gave me the gift of manhood or free will, I will do all I can to fulfill thy trust and will find the 'Path of Wisdom' and will advance and advance until I have reached the goal thou Great 

Creator put there for man. I know thou wouldst not put a goal beyond the limit of thy Supreme 

Creation man. "Since thou made me man, I should not have to ask for help. "Since thou made me man, if I fail, I should not say 'it was you or the environment in which I am placed.' 

I should be true to thee and myself and say, 'thou made me man, since I failed to be a man, it is my fault-not thine-not anyone elses-but mine alone.' "Would I, as a man, want to lay my burdens, my miseries, my shortcomings, my mistakes, my troubles and trials upon someone else; would I want to have someone else fight my battles for me? No, not me. 

Thou made me man and man I will be. Yes, even unto the point of despair. I will not ask for help.

Rather than ask or solicit help even from my Great Creator or the Ideal which He gave to us to look up to by the Path of Wisdom; if I fail to keep my eyes upon Him and fall by my own neglect-then it is I-not Him-nor you-but me. "I want to be great in Wisdom not Position."

Just as he starts his Modern Prayer, his friend comes up behind him with pencil and pad in her hand. As he speaks the prayer aloud, not knowing that anyone is near, she takes it down in shorthand.

As he finishes she marvels at such a prayer from so young a boy; then she lets him know she is near.

He turns toward her and says "such a morning, first, with the birds with their song; then the tree tells me its story." He goes on and relates to her all the story of the tree and how the Voice answered his question and what a promise it had in store for him when he becomes big enough to hear and understand.

She takes everything down he talks of and he brings in thought after thought and each one more beautiful than the other. He seems to be filled to overflowing; and she notices he talks of his Creator and uses the word Creation and couples it with all Truth and tells her he will soon have the secrets of the entire Earth laid bare to him. He will write it for the benefit of the people. He tells her the Soulmind of man is limitless for he sees mountain after mountain of Wisdom and as he grows older he will know more and more of life and life after death. 

He tells her he can see no death-only the portion that belongs to the Earth. "Let the Earth have its own," he declares, "when it gets too old or weak to sustain the Soul-mind." He sees if the tree dies then lives again why should not the great Creator's Supreme Creation live even as the tree or the Rose in the Garden. "Do they not make it easy to understand? I am beginning to see it in its Truth and I am sure the Path of Wisdom will lead me to this 

Light of Truth."

As he talks, she keeps on writing and soon he has told her many things that she never had heard or read before. She had come to tell him lunch was ready; but that had gone from her memory. As she took her watch out, she saw it was five o'clock. He

A STEPPING STONE TO THE WORLD 55 

had been talking five hours; and the food he had been receiving and giving to her had taken the place of the food for natural causes. 

She smiles as she says to him, "I came to call you to lunch at twelve, now it is five so we will have to eat two meals in one." "I do not care if I do not eat for days, if I can be fed by the Silent Voice of Wisdom. I do not want to live to eat, I want to eat to live. How I want to live! Life never meant so much to me as it does at this moment. How wonderful! How great! How inspiring! Why can we not have words big enough to express ourselves?" "Would you want to be able to express your feelings?"

He stands and looks at her, then says, "no, I am satisfied to feel the way I do. It is beyond words. 

All I can say is 'What a Creation!'"

As they go home to supper, he speaks not a word; and she being a person of common sense does not speak to him.

They eat their supper in silence. After supper, he takes his violin down for the first time since he came back and plays. Yes, plays as he never had played before. He seems to be telling the story in song of the tree, of the wonderful things he had seen, of the wonderful things he felt even to the prayer and the swelling up in emotion. Such music! It seemed to come from the Soul for only the Soul could play as he plays tonight.

As his friend looks at him, she too, feels as he feels. Her Soul goes out to him. When he finishes, she put her arms around him and says "you beautiful Soul. I wonder who you are and who you were

in other lives. You must have lived before, yes, many times for no one of your age could accomplish so much in the few years I have known you. You talk as no man ever talked. You show emotion such as man never showed before. You play your violin such as man never played before. Yes, you are different. I wonder what your mission can be. What a life! What a life you have given me! How will I ever repay you?" 

He smiles upon her and says, "do you want to repay me?" 

She says, "yes." "Then listen to me because you 'want to,' and I will be well repaid for without you my talk would be only to the trees and the birds. You are the only person in the world who understands me. So you see you are really necessary in my life." He stands a while; and as he does he seems to be looking back into the past. He looks at her and says, "yes, I know you now, even as I knew you then. You are the same to me as you were then only in different position. You were not any nearer or dearer then than you are now."

She asks what he means. He smiles and says, "in my last life, you were my mother-both in flesh and Soul-mind; but you were taken from me and I promised I would find you again. 99 She thought, "so this is why I love him so and him so young. Now I am proud of my love for him and not ashamed as I thought I should be. It is not the love I thought it was, it is more wonderful, more beautiful. It is real true Soul-mind love from mother to son." Then she puts her arms around him and gives him a kiss of love.

A STEPPING STONE TO THE WORLD 57 

He accepts it and returns it even as she had given it. What a revelation!

He retires for the night so he might get up early to sing again his song with the birds; but she sits and wonders and tries to look into the past as he had done. The vision would not come, so she gets out her writing and prepares his conversation of the afternoon for a publishing company. With it she sends a letter and says "this book must be published -no matter what they think of it. It would not be necessary to have it corrected-just print it as it is written.' 99 In a few days she gets an answer and a contract to sign and another to fill in to promise to give them all the writings of the author. They were more than satisfied and would deem it a favor if they could secure the privilege of all publication of such an author; but why did not the author want his name put to it? The work was of the highest standard they had ever had. Would she state the price of the contract; but try not to make it too high for the first two books. They would have it published in every country possible for it is food for the world. 

In a few days she hands him a letter and in it was a check. When he looks at it he turns to her and says, "what is the meaning of this?" 

She says, "read the letter, then you will see you are going to be a busy boy for a while." "Yes, but this check. Is this all mine?" 

She smiles and says, "yes, that is all yours. The first fruit of your labor." "May I endorse it and send it to my father?

Poor fellow, he is, I know, disappointed in his son

since I left him. Maybe this will help him over his disappointment." "Yes, but do you not think you should send him half and keep the other half for yourself? We can, if we 'want to,' travel; and you know, as you travel, the more you will see and the more of Creation will have a chance to talk to you. Do you not think that a good suggestion? One-half will keep your mother and dad until you receive your next check. You see every month you will have checks coming in; and in time there will be a great deal more than that one." "I do not understand what I have said or done to receive money like this." "No, you cannot; but I can, and every conversation we have from now on means money without limit." "So my life is worthwhile after all-both from a material standpoint and the standpoint of Truth and Wisdom. Since I just have to listen and repeat to you what I hear, it seems the money should go to 

He who tells me these things." 

She smiles once more upon him and says, "well, some day, you will meet him in person; then you can give it to him.” 

He asks what she means.

She tells him he will meet many who are poor and many who are in need of help. Maybe many crippled children and many other cases who will need his help and if he helps those people he is giving the money to the persons who talk to him through the Silent Voice for they are the Creator's suffering children. "Then will you help me find them as you found the means for me?"

A STEPPING STONE TO THE WORLD 59 

"It will be both a pleasure and a dream fulfilled, if I have that privilege. You know you have not seen much of the world and the masses; but soon you will see and whatever the Silent Voice tells you to do-do-and whatever it tells you to say-say -without fear. You will not only give money to the few, but you will give Wisdom to the many. 

The world needs Wisdom today more than it ever did. There I have lectured you for once-how do you like it?" "Oh, it sounds reasonable." "We will go to the city today and have a bank to take care of your financial interest as we will be traveling soon. All money can be sent to your bank and put to your credit then we will not have to worry. We will then travel." "Am I going to see the sea and the deserts of the 

West and the orange groves?" "Yes, you are going to see everything you should -everything interesting or worthwhile in this wonderful country of ours. We will then travel to other countries and as you travel and see the things that have been mysteries since the time of man, you will reveal those mysteries so the people shall know our Creator put no mysteries here—they are man made mysteries; and you are the man to solve those mysteries. What a Light you are going to be! How proud your parents are going to be of you! Do you know your mother saw all this in you when you were just a little bundle of mystery. It was she who understood and saved you from being another cog in the wheel of this industrious world. What a mother! But, what a son! What a Soul-mind son!" "Yes, but what about my Soul-mind mother?"

"Yes, and I thank the Creator for the trust He put in me. I will never betray His trust. It seems when we get started to talk, we forget everything else. We must cease to talk and get ready for our trip to the city. You get ready, and I will soon be ready."

As they were descending the mountain, it seemed to fill him with a new thought. He asked her this question "since a mountain is a symbol of Wisdom, and when you are upon it you can see the clearness of Light and I have really never lived on a level with the masses, will it be possible for my vision to be limited as theirs? I feel sort of afraid. Will I have enough air to breathe? Will I be left alone with my thoughts, as I have been here? Will I be able to sit and feel my smallness in the Universe; and yet be filled with that same thing that seems to grip you and hold you in its influence until you feel tears streaming down your cheeks and realize how wonderful life is and how beautiful the world is? 

Will I be able to retain the power to talk of Wisdom, no matter where I am or how many people may be there? Will I be able to hear that quiet Silent

Voice which means all to me-that has made me what I am today? Are you sure, I will be able to hear the things I have been used to above the clamour and noises of the world? Are you sure the people I am going to meet will welcome and not be afraid of me? You say I am different from the others. If I am, will the world accept me and my writing? I feel just a little afraid. I will be lonesome when I have to mix with the people. I seem to have such wonderful company, when I am alone. I am anxious to travel; yet, Oh, well, I must be big

A STEPPING STONE TO THE WORLD 61 

enough to meet anyone-no matter how big or how small. I will be equal to the situation." "I am pleased to hear you say that; but remember, I will be with you, no matter where you go or who you may meet. I am your housekeeper, your stenographer, your business manager and that puts me with you at all times.' "I am surely glad." "Well, here we are in the city. First, we will find a bank. I want you to do your own business. I will go with you but you must conduct your own affairs. 

Before you go; I want to tell you what to do as this is a new experience for you. You should put all this in checking so you can pay your bills in check. You can fill in one and send it to your parents. Won't they get a surprise? Then hold so much cash. Give instructions for the bank to endorse all checks made payable to you and have them added to your account. 

After a certain amount in your checking, have the surplus put into a private account." "I will follow your instructions to the letter."

They enter into a bank and go to the new account clerk. He hands him the check and says, "I want to open a checking account with your bank." "Yes, Sir. How much of this do you want to put in checking?" "All but so and so." 

The clerk says, "do you not think it would be better to divide this into two accounts? Say so much in checking and so much in private account?" 

She waits for him to reply, to see just how he would receive this suggestion. 

When, without looking at her, he says to the clerk, "I thought, I told you what I wanted."

"Well you did; but I thought." "It is not what you think, it is what I want; so you just do as I tell you." "Yes, Sir. Yes, Sir."

He does not give any more advice but follows his instructions.

She had to touch his hand to let him know he had not failed her.

He gave his final instructions then they left. 

How proud she was of him! 

They went shopping and he bought what he wanted-still living in the life I want or I don't want, I know or I don't know. What a molding of a Master-mind that would not be told by anyone what he wanted!

They arrive home again and start to prepare for their journey ahead.

First, they will go to his home and live with his people for a week.

In every step of life he shows, unconsciously, in his natural way, his greatness. 

A man of Wisdom can be known, By everything he does;

And when he speaks, it is what he means 

For he knows just what he says.

CHAPTER X FIRST NIGHT AT HOME 

A meeting of a family, Yes, a really and truly home; 

Where each one loves the other They just seem to be "Three in One.' 

The next day they arrive at his home. His mother shows her love for him in a mother's way. His father shakes his hand and comments on how good he looks. Then they settle down. The father and son in one room, the mother and the woman she chose to take care of her boy in the other room. 

He asks his father how business is and hears the same story of loss of money and the expenses of the upkeep of the house and other things. He conveys to his son that he still thinks he should have stayed home to help him in his business to save expenses. 

The boy smiles at this for he knows his father is one of the cogs of the wheels of industry; and money is his aim in life. He expresses himself by saying, "Yes, it is hard to have to pay someone else when you have a son of your own; but your son did not seem to fit into the wheel smoothly. There always seemed to be an undue friction. It may not have been felt by the wheel but the cog felt it and could not be satisfied."

At these words, the father looks up at his son and says, “you speak strangely; yet it sounds like common sense."

"Well, father, I always speak common sense or I hold my peace. That is the motto of my life. I never talk to be heard. I do not like useless noises. 

Do you?" "Well, son, that is well spoken; and has a great lesson in it, but I am of a different world than you. 

You seem to dwell in a world all your own. I am in the world of cold matter-of-fact material. Maybe some day you will be understood; but up to now you are a mystery and always have been-to me." "No, not a mystery-just misunderstood; but I hope, some day you will understand me and also the people of the world. I hope, some day, to repay you for bringing me into the world. I know, so far, I have been an added expense to you; but my time will come.' 

Just then, the mother enters the room and takes her boy by the arm and says, "dinner is served;" and the light in her eyes was beautiful. You could see the fountain of a mother's love overflowing for she had been listening to the story of her boy's life, and what a beautiful life it must be.

As they pass around the table, he puts an envelope at his father's place and one at his mother's place. 

At this action, his friend saw and understood the principle. He was making them both alike. How wonderful! On one was written, "To My Father, with all the respects of a Son." On his mother's was, "To My Mother, she who saw and understood, From a Son of Love."

After they finish their meal, each opens their envelope and on a piece of paper was written, "A part of the first fruits of my labor."

When they look at the checks and see the

amounts, the mother's eyes fill with tears and the father looks at his son and says, "will you ever forgive me for the thoughts I had of you, Son?"

He replies, "I have nothing to forgive. I was just misunderstood. I want to make you both happy and make me worthy of such parents. To you, I give all the credit for what I am today-not forgetting my right hand supporter," as he takes the hand of the woman who had brought him before the public. As he takes her hand she, too, sheds a few tears of gladness; and I think I can hear her say "how much 

I love you, you will never know."

As he notices the tears in all their eyes, he smiles upon them and says, "why tears; when we should have smiles of joy?"

They dry their eyes and leave the table. The father and son go into the room alone once more while the women clear the table. What a homecoming! What a Mother! What a Father! But, what a Son! 

When they settle down, the father asks him where he has gotten the money. He tells him of the sale of his first book; and as he talks of his book, his father stops him and says, "then you, my

Son, are this unknown author the country is on fire about? The book, just published, is really yours? 

All you can hear is 'That Book' and the people trying to guess the name of the author. You who I thought I would have to keep as long as I live; and worried who would keep you after I was gone. Can this be possible?" "Yes, Dad, it is possible; and before you began to rave I was going to suggest a few things to you. I will have a monthly income that will run into the

thousands. My second book will be off the press in a week or two and they are begging for more. So since my income is assured for years to come, I would like you to give up your struggle of life and take mother on a tour around the world. You have not seen much, and it would be a wonderful thing for you to withdraw yourself from the wheel of industry; and see how beautiful this world is when you get out of the grind of the masses. You can live for quite a while yet, if you take life easy—and you can if you 'want to.' Do you 'want to?' Make up your mind and as you want so shall it be." "To think, you are the author of this wonderful book and the book to be named 'Creation in Truth.' 

My Son, how do you see these things?" "Well, my eyes have been opened and I see beyond the masses. I am looking for Wisdom-not prosperity. My ears have been given a keener reception; so I can hear the Small Silent Voice that brings my Soul-mind into the highest thoughts possible. My tongue has been loosened, and when I speak, I speak as the Silent Voice speaks to me. As 

I sit alone, I hear the most beautiful things, and as 

I look, I see the true beauty of the Earth. As I listen, the Voice of Creation speaks to me. The mountains always talked to me—that is why I loved them so and because they loved me and liked to talk to me is why they kept calling me. I was lonesome for them and they were lonesome for me. 

Now I am going to travel, and as I travel, I will be constantly reading the Book of Creation. As I read it, I will be able to understand it; then I will tell the people what the Book of Creation tells me. You know, Dad, Creation cannot lie. I hope to hear, as

I travel, the voice of the seas, the rivers, the hills, the sands on the seashore, the climate of different countries and the different races of people; from where they get their color. The different deposits of the earth will tell me from where they came. 

The storehouse for man will open its doors and say, 'yes, look into my larder and watch and you will see how time and evolution put me here. Yes, and after I was here, it took a long time before man came forth to claim me; but I am satisfied for the waiting for I see man's advanced thought. My victuals, I give to him freely for I have enough and to spare.' I will sail upon the different seas, and, I know, each one will have a beautiful story to tell of both the joys and the sorrows they bring to man. 

I will listen to them and when I give it to the world —what a picture it will be! Yes, Dad, Creation is vast; Wisdom is unlimited; there is a power beyond all human understanding; a general love for humanity and when I get big enough to find these three great principles and know how to use them-how happy I will be! All I can say when I talk thus I could go on saying forever, there is no end to it.

The Book of Creation is full and every page is of the most interesting reading; and the more you read the smaller you get in your own estimation, for you see so far beyond you; and the beyond is so full of treasures you would like to have that you feel as though you would never get there. The grade is very steep and your progress very slow; yet as soon as you try to get a step ahead of yourself there seems to be a hand there and it is laying upon you and the voice says 'take your time only one step at a time and be sure your foot is firmly set or you may

slip back; then you will have to climb longer and the second time it will be harder for you have slipped and when you slipped your feet made it more slippery. So I would say go careful, go easy and take care; but be sure and make your own path-do not try to step in the footsteps of others. They may lead you from your path and it might just look as though it were the same path as you were on but be not led by such marks. Nor should you turn right or left as your goal is straight ahead and only by going straight can you reach the goal put before you.

You will find, the further you ascend the mountain, the further you will see; and the more you see the more you will understand; the more you understand the more eager you will be to proceed further for the heights of the life of a Soul-mind is even unto Creation itself—the goal is worth the struggle-the struggle only makes the victory greater; and the greater the victory the more Wisdom you receive.

Do not let the glaring lights of Life lead you from your path.'"

At this time, the mother maternal and the mother Soul-mind enter the room and take their seats. The father is looking seriously at his son; as he has not heard him talk this way before. He says to the mother, "Mother, we have a priceless gift in this son of ours. How glad I am that you saw and understood at the beginning of his life. What a mistake I might have made! How wrong my thoughts have been! I am ashamed of myself for the things 

I have thought and said to you.'

The son speaks again, "Father, let the past be the past, let us start all over again. Keep the worthwhile memories of the past; but let us cast the un-

pleasant thoughts from us. Let us live today that we might know more tomorrow. Let us not imitate the lives of those in the past; but rather let us try to be what the men will be tomorrow. Let us keep the wheels of evolution going, there are enough people today who retard the progress of the evolution of the Soul-mind of the people. Let us live upon the mountain in all its glory. Why should we grabble in the fog of life when we can get out of it; and when we do get out of it let us send messages to those who live in the fog and tell them, of the beautiful sunshine awaiting them, if they will make up their minds. Also let them know if we can reach the light-they can also for the path is still there—all they have to do is find the path and keep upon it. 

They cannot fail, once they have gotten to the top of the mountain and felt the light of the sun and breathed in the pure air, they will never want to go back to the land of fog again. Once they taste of the truth and their eyes have been opened so they can penetrate ahead and see the higher life awaiting them, their motto will be, 'I will press onward and upward for I can understand what life means.' 

How I sometimes wish I could let the people see the things I see and hear the things I hear. 'How I 

wish I could show them the real true light after death.' What an incentive to live! What a field to live in! How wide this life is cannot be told. How deep is this sea of Wisdom is beyond my comprehension. How large the fountain of the love for humanity is beyond me; but I know I will do all I can to take of all three powers that man can carry. 

As the days go on, it seems there is no end to the beautiful thoughts we receive from the Soul-mind of

this Creation. Only through a continued life could we accumulate so much of the Creator's Greatest Treasure; and as we partake of them life after life reveals unto us the vastness of this treasure storehouse. I hope, as the years go on, and I grow in body into manhood, then into middle-age, then press on to old age, I will keep accumulating without ceasing until I have reached the goal of my desire in this one life; then when I return, I will be able to start where I left off." 

Here his father feels he must ask a question for the boy's talk is too deep for him and he asks, “is it possible to, as you say, Reincarnate? You seem to make it clear to understand; yet to me it is against all the teaching we have been taught."
