Instead of cursing as you have been doing, it will open the flood-gates of higher thoughts, and show the truth of life and death and life after death. 

It will show you your life is continuous and not ended by one short life. It will make you crave for more years to live so you can make the one short life a stepping-stone to a bigger and nobler life in the next life. It will show you a power, if used wisely, maybe you could save someone else from suffering the agonies of Hell-as you have:-for if you find the Truth of Creation, it will reveal its principle unto you; and with the knowledge of this wonderful principle and the love for Humanity, you will, as years go on, be a man among men. Do you not know, if you had known a Creative Healer, one who knows truly the Principle of Creation, and he had seen such love as you had, he would have given her back to you-in health and strength; for Creation is the greatest healing Power known to the world? You can have all these things, if you would seek the Truth and know that the Creator does not show partiality to anyone-He never did, and He never will. In the beginning, He gave man a mind, yes, all men a mind; and with these words he left him: 'As thou thinketh so thou shall be.' All I can ask you-have you fulfilled that Trust, have you

lived that others might live, have you advanced the world of thought to the higher life, what plane of life have you reached, how wide is your vision, what do you hear from the Voice of Creation, do you or have you tried to give the Light He expected you to give or have you just lived in the fog of life without ever looking up to see if there was a Light above the fog? My Son, have you done your duty to He who gave you life? Have you ever tried to paint a picture of life each day, and as each day goes on add a little more touch of beauty to that picture; and make up your mind that you will continue to paint it day by day, even if circumstances of life may cause a blot to appear upon it-be big enough to paint over the blot with a smile and say, 'I will not let blots interfere with my picture, I have always enough paint to cover them for the more I have to paint over again the more beautiful my picture becomes-because it means an extreme effort? No, this is not impossible; you see life is all time and time means nothing so you have a long time to paint." "You speak well and wisely, Sir; but I am afraid

I have thrown away my easel, canvas, paint and brush. I have not a thought big enough to put on any canvas. My picture, I am afraid, would be a failure." "If it is, it is only you-not anyone else, it is only our smallness and our loss of manhood that makes us speak thus. I am sorry for you; since you have of your own free will given up or thrown away the manhood that the Creator gave you as a sacred trust. 

What a betrayal, what a failure, how small a man can be! It is well we have a Just Creator who is

GLIMPSE INTO DARKNESS OF LIFE 123 

willing to look upon such a failure as you and say, 'Well, I gave him a trust and he betrayed it; but since I love him, I will give him another opportunity for his Soul-mind shall surely live on and maybe he will see manhood in its true sense in the next life and go higher. Yes, I will give him many chances for he must be at the end as I saw him at the beginning—a man.' ” "Sir, you hit hard-spare me." "So again you are showing your unmanliness? I am speaking truth and truth spares not. Answer me, have you played the man? As you answer, so shall 

I believe you?" "Have I been a man, you ask? No, I see it all now, I was not big enough to seek the truth for myself. I let others lead me through life. They taught me a faith, they told me of its power, they told me to put my trust in God, they told me not to carry my own burdens of life but to lay them on someone else—and I believed them. Why was I not big enough to see? Why was I not a man? Yet, all the time, I thought I was-I could not see that I was being led. How small I must be in the eyes of my

Creator, when He saw me having someone else to lead me, think for me, carry my trials of life for me! 

Why, Sir, I see the light, I see it all now and I understand the words He spoke and how true they are, 'As thou thinketh so thou shall be.' What a revelation! What a life I have led! How small I have been! But from now on, if there is anything left of me worthwhile, I am going to start out, yes for the first time of my life, and be a man." "Do you want to be?" "Yes, and I am determined to be."

"Then you will be.”

Just at this time, the young man had never spoken a word, but at this point he says, "If you are as true to the true life as you were to the life you used to have; then you will be a man indeed, Sir." "You also speak Wisdom."

The mysterious man says, "Yes, he is one of the advanced Soul-minds returned that the world might know more of the Truth. He speaks well." 

What a night! Yes, for the three of them; but let us look at the face of the man who sat down a while ago, with an expression without a hope. What a change, now! As he looks up with a smile, his gaze is met by someone who he did not know was there before; and as he looks into her face in the moonlight, he feels a thrill go through him. He arises and says, "A night well spent; but the final surprise makes it more so.' 99 She smiles, as he takes her hand and then names are mentioned. He bids them "Good night," saying it is time for his bedtime drink that he might sleep. 

As he goes into the Salon, he orders his first drink; but when the waiter brings it, the Silent Voice whispers, "I thought you were going to be a man." He stops the drink half-way to his lips and speaks aloud, "Yes, I am, and I mean it." He puts the glass down, leaves, walks around the deck; and for the first time since the darkness came into his life, sees the beauty of the sea, stars and moon. 

Our friends see him walk to the rail. They pass him but do not speak. There is a fight to fight; and it is a one man's fight.

The mysterious man goes to his stateroom, the boy to bed, but the friend of the boy does not go

GLIMPSE INTO DARKNESS OF LIFE 125 

for she stands against the rail also. Something has come into her life, she does not understand, as she has never felt that way before. She is lost in thought, when she feels someone near her, she turns, and as she does, sees the man of sadness—as she used to call him. He is looking at her with a smile. 

She speaks to him thus: "Well did you win?" "Yes, I did, I have often heard people say they had been born again; but as I stand here tonight, I know I have just been born; I was not born the last time, I just was carried through life. What a revelation! Are you, too, of this Path of Truth?" "I am the audience to the young man of the Life of Truth." "Who is the old gentleman?" "As far as I can figure, he is a Master-mind, a man who has found the True Principle of all Creation. They say, he has lived many lives and has always been speaking Truth; yet always seeking more Wisdom-as he says the Path of Wisdom has no end and is without limit; and he is going to be as near the limit as any one. Stories told of him say that he does and can do wonderful things for the sick. He seems to be just a father to everyone; yet he does not approach anyone but seems to draw people unto him when they have grief or trouble." "Well, he surely told me what I was tonight, and let me see myself in my true light. He surely knows life. I am thankful for the change." "Yes," she says, "and as you go on in the Path of 

Truth, you will know that he has given you a treasure beyond price. It must be late." "One-thirty only, such a night as this could not grow late! I could swear that he did not talk more

than thirty minutes; yet my watch says four hours. 

How interesting!" "And even how interesting to even stand here and talk about it; but I will have to go. Good Night.' "9 He once more is left alone-a new feeling has come over him, he does not understand; so he goes to his state-room-not to sleep, he thinks, but to review his life alone. He lays down. Does he sleep? 

We will find out tomorrow. 

The next morning, the sun is shining beautifully.

People begin to come on deck for their morning walk before breakfast. Our young Master-mind and his friend are walking, when they see the man of the night before coming toward them-sad as yesterday? No; but with a cheery "Good Morning" smile, he greets them. They ask him if he slept. "Sleep is not the word for it, I just drifted into slumber land, and how peaceful, how restful yet what a dream!" "So you dreamed too?" "Why, yes, did you? I hope your dream was as pleasing to you as mine was to me; but there is the call for breakfast and I am so hungry, I think I will never be satisfied this morning. Do you know, that was my first night's sleep with a sober mind for over two years; and this is the first time I have had an appetite for breakfast. I am just curious to see how much I will eat this morning. Born Again? Yes, how much those words mean to me, only I can know!" "You are mistaken, everyone on the ship will know, everyone you meet who have known you before will know and let you know that they know, and your people will know as soon as you arrive

GLIMPSE INTO DARKNESS OF LIFE 127 

home-you will not have to tell them for they will tell you just you see if they don't." Then with a smile she says to him, "I hope you do justice to your stomach this morning."

The Path of Wisdom again laid bare, Before a troubled Soul.

The light of Life shone through the haze, To a life just born anew.

CHAPTER XV THE REVELATION 

Open thine eyes and thou shalt hear,

The things that are almost beyond compare. 

The Truth in the mysteries of a quiet life That are in this world so full of strife. 

As they came again on deck, the man of sadness yesterday, but as he says 'a man born again today,' is anxious to sit with the young man; and as soon as they are seated, he approaches them and says, "May I sit down?" 

The young man says, "Yes, if you want to." 

He looks at him, smiles and says, "I want to.

That seems to be a word you use so much; but when its meaning, in its true sense, is understood, it is a wonderful word and means so much in life. I can see it now for the first time in my life-another lesson. It is so much more wonderful to do something you want to than it is to do something you have to. It surely does show the free will part of man; yet there are so many things we do that we have to that makes a common routine and we fall into this routine, unconsciously, do you not think?” "Yes, I can see your view, we are apt to do so many things we just think we have to, to please someone else that we forget that anything done because we have to do it is not well done. Only the things we want to do and take the pleasure in doing, do we enjoy doing. In your life, you were called upon by a have to do that you were so loath to do

THE REVELATION 

129 that the doing was hard, indeed. You wanted your wife, yet you had to give her up." "I know, how true and how hard that was; but since I see the truth of it all, I feel as though I would, if she lived today and was suffering as she was then I would want to see her relieved of her suffering. You see, I have benefited by the lesson of last night, already; and I had a dream which showed me the truth more clearly."

The man of mystery, who is passing by, does not stop but says, "I see the seed fell on fertile soilcare for the soil earnestly and the harvest will be worth the reaping." 

The boy says, "How wisely he speaks, and how wonderful are his words!" "Only Wisdom could give the words that this man speaks," says the man of yesterday. 

And the lady, "As I listen to his words, they make me wonder how much he knows. He meets everything and every issue in the most natural state. He is a man of mystery, indeed. As I look upon him, all I can say is 'He is different.'" "What do you mean, when you say 'different?'" "I do not know-only different in every way to the ordinary man.' 

The morning is pleasantly spent, talking of the night before. As they part for lunch, the new man of the Light says, "This is almost unbelievable. Can it be possible for such a change to come over night?

There is no more fog, no more darkness, no more pressure on my chest and the tightness of my head. is gone. Can it be true or am I dreaming? Can there be such a life? I feel so good, I feel more a man than I ever felt before. Young man, I thank

you for that smile. How I hated that smile for several days; but how I am going to love it for the rest of my days! I am grateful that you smiled upon me. I cannot tell you what I think, words are not big enough; I can only feel it and I feel it is true. I thank you (turning to the lady) for your presence." 

As he leaves them, she smiles and says, "What do you think of him?" to the young Master-mind. "I have nothing to say; but I see two paths, I see a person on each path. As I look, I see the two paths come together and as they do I see the two persons fall in side by side so easily and naturally -it seems to be a single path for two, and it leads to happiness." He looks at her and smiles. "I thought you said you had nothing to say?" "Well, can a person not see a picture if he wants to, and describe it without the other person guessing or coming to conclusions? I could mean so many persons that it does not have to be you. You know, I need you for a long time yet, so you see I hope the double path leads far before it becomes one-in your case." "That is enough of that, let us go and eat."

The afternoon is spent in walking and enjoying the beautiful day with its heat and sunshine; and soon the day passes.

This is the night of the Ball on the ship. The people are all dressed and parading the deck. There seem to be so many more on the ship than any time before.

As our young Master-mind takes his seat with his audience, as he calls her, he says, "This is a beautiful night. It seems so calm, so peaceful, so pleas-

THE REVELATION 

131 ant in this warmth of the evening, and so still-the 

Voice will be heard, if it will speak tonight.' 99 "Yes," she says, "but do you not know there is a 

Grand Ball tonight and the sight will be worth the seeing? Do you not think you would like to see the people dancing and hear the music?" "No, I do not think I would like it; but if you want to see, by all means go. I can sit here alone, no, not alone, I am never alone for the Small Voice is constantly with me.' 99 "Oh! I do not want to go, I thought maybe you would want to see-that is the reason I told you. I would rather be with you than anywhere else in the world. You see, the dress, the Ball and the dancing would be pleasing while it lasted and tomorrow I would forget; but the things you might say will stay with me for life. Why waste time for a passing memory when you can hear things that will be a life's memory?" "You also are reaching the place where you speak wisely. What a true friend I have found in you!

When I look upon you and hear you speak thus, it puts me in direct touch with the Book of Creation; and as it begins to unfold its leaves, I know it is a new story or vision for they come always in mental pictures. The pictures begin, in turn, to unfold; and as you look into it, you see the Path of Wisdom more clearly. The greater things of life come floating in, and as they do the Voice says 'Look and I will let you see how far the Path will lead you. First, I will show you the picture of the Earth on which you live and how far a man has to go before he reaches the height of Wisdom attainable in this life of advancement toward man's highest point in Soul-mind.”—

Would that the Light become brighter that I might find the highest life possible."

At this, the Silent Voice speaks to him thus: "To attain the highest height, you must be natural by being truthful to yourself, by analyzing yourself, by watching that no organ has too much put upon it nor too little, by coming to the knowledge that there is nothing big enough to worry about, by taking life in its true meaning, by setting a goal in life so far beyond you that it will take years to reach, by seeking ever seeking the truth, by accumulating Wisdom, by seeking a Power without limit that can be used for the benefit of others, by acquiring a Love so vast that the fountain of love can never run dry and by not letting the smallness of others overcome you. You must have confidence in all humanity, although they do prove their smallness-do not forsake them nor look down upon them; but give them your sympathy and in time they will repent and ask you to forgive them which you will do readily for you should see no smallness in anyone, but look for the little spark of greatness that is in all men; and then try to kindle that spark into a burning flame. Do not take anything from anyone, if you cannot give them something greater in return.

These are the keys to the lock that opens the door of Life and Wisdom, unto those who would become what the Creator saw man should be. He made man to finish His Creation, just as He saw it finished when He looked at the beginning, 'I would see man, yes, all men amass such Wisdom, Power and Love that harmony will prevail throughout the Earth.'" "You know, as I sit and hear the Voice of Wisdom talking to me, I feel: if I could only go on ever

THE REVELATION 

133 hearing this Voice and the beautiful thoughts it brings to me; if I could go on accumulating this vast 

Wisdom and receiving Power and more Power and the flood-gates of love were flowing for me continuously, I would be satisfied to just sit and drink until the end of time." 

At this, the Voice speaks again and very strongly thus: "Now, if you traveled all your life like this, and as each day brought the Truth of Life, and you became a Master of Creation and gained all the Wisdom and was shown all the secrets of the Power of Creation and this great Principle became revealed to you how to use this Power, and you drank from the fountain of Love until you felt the world belonged to you in your Wisdom; and yet you traveled on until the end of time and saw the end of time come-would not your life be a failure?" "Oh! Thou Silent Voice, spare me, I see myself as thou would have me see. After all, how small 

I would be, how selfish I am! No, My Creator, it was a weak moment of the flesh, not my Soul-mind for I see myself clearly at this moment." "My Son of the Light, I will forgive you for I see as you travel further into the Light and receive more Wisdom and thy Power is increased and your 

Love for Humanity is multiplied; then you will not want to retain for you will find that the more you give to others the more you will receive."

As the young Master-mind came out of his receptive mood, he said: "Creation, how beautiful-how wonderful-how vast-how majestic-how thrilling -how life-inspiring! Oh, thou Great Creator, give me Wisdom that I might understand these things more. Give me Power that I might use it for the

benefit of Humanity. Give me Love that I may be able to give it to others even as thou hast given it to me. Make me live that the world might be better for my living. Nay, My Creator, I would have all

I can receive of thee that I might pass it on to others. 

I do not ask this for myself, but for the good only that I might give to others. How can I thank, thee, Oh, thou Great Creator? Words are so small to try to express myself. I cannot keep silent-I must find a way to thank thee-I must, yes, I must."

At this, our young Master-mind arises in a hurry. 

His friend does not understand; but in a few minutes he is back upon the deck again, his violin in his hand. He walks toward the rail. His friend knows then that his Soul-mind is so full he has to give vent to it by the music of the Soul-mind instrument. 

The deck is set for the Ball; and they are ready to start the music-when this boy begins to play his violin. He is unconscious of anyone or where he is, as he plays. You can feel the vibration filling the air. The orchestra does not start. As the sweet strains of the violin come from the young man standing facing the Heavens, pouring out his Soul's thanks in music, a strange feeling comes over his listeners.

Everyone drinks it in and knows it is something they have never witnessed before; yet he plays on, yes on and on, sending thrills through his audience. He does not realize he has an audience; yet they keep coming nearer and nearer until you would think almost everyone on the ship is standing around him. 

The Heavens seem to lighten up, the waters cease to make a noise against the sides of the ship, it seems everything and everyone is listening for fear they will miss one note of this wonderful music; yet

THE REVELATION 

135 there he stands sending out sound waves, yes sound waves, that will go on forever. Do you think the 

Creator hears him? If He does not, His Creation does; and they wonder how such strains of music can be produced from a violin. It is not the violin, but the Soul-mind tuned up to perfection; and as the Soul-mind is so is the music. He seems, as he stands there with all eyes upon him, that he will never come to the end and the people do not want an end; but he again bursts forth with such a thrilling outburst that the people can hardly keep quiet any longer. Suddenly, he comes to an abrupt end and says aloud, "Yes, I tried in words but they failed to give expression of my thanks, I tried to tell you through my violin and how it tried but it, too, has failed."

Just then the Man of Mystery comes up to the boy, and as he stands and looks upon him a light shines from his eyes, and he lays his hand upon the boy and says, "You have tried to give thanks with words and song, now try Silence, My Son of the Light." 

How beautiful! Yet it is not enough for the boy.

He is filled to over-flowing; but he goes back to his chair and as the Man of Mystery leaves him he says, "My Son, I am proud of you this night; and surely thou art fulfilling thy trust."

As he sits down and looks at his parents, the tears are glistening in their eyes-but they are tears of joy. His friend takes his hand and he understands and says, "I think I shall go to my room, silence only can tell Him how I love Him." 99 The music starts for the Ball; but the people feel a contrast in the music and can not dance as they

had anticipated. There are so few on the dance floor, it is a failure. So many of the people stand in groups and talk of the beautiful music and the picture of our boy as he stood alone against the rail with the moon shining upon him.

What a picture, what music, what a memory to those who heard and saw this tonight! Yes, a night to be remembered as long as their lives continuea Master-mind. 

Yes, all these things surely will come true, When all mankind are born anew; 

And the Truth of Life as this boy knew it, Is given to all who want to find it.

A THOUGHT A thought came floating in to me, From a source I cannot see; 'Tis enough that I know they are beautiful 

And makes life more pleasant for me.

There is only one source they can come from; 

For they seem so much higher than me. 

That tells me that I am in touch 

With something Truthful and Real; 

And that something is greater than me. 

Thoughts like these I know could not come,

From a lower Soul-mind than I; 

So they must come from a higher plane, Yes, a plane that is ever so high.

They could never come from the Earth below, For there is nothing there to see; 

Only solidity of the crust of the Earth, And the soil that gives us something to eat.

That is just to feed the material man; 

But the inner man, you see

Needs the food in thoughts of the higher kind; 

And they can only come from above.

CHAPTER XVI HIS FIRST TEMPTATION 

The world will try, yes every time, To stay advancement of the mind;

And use it for a worldly gain By painting a picture showing fame. 

Again night is here and the father, mother, friend and boy are having their evening chat; when the new born man comes and asks to join the party. They tell him, he may if he wants to. This from the young 

Master-mind to be.

As the conversation begins there comes towards them, another person. They invite him to join them.

He thanks them and draws up another chair, and says, "I heard you play your violin last night; and as I am one of the musical world, a thought came to me-What a violinist! Such playing, I have never heard before. I did not sleep last night for I have become interested in you. If you had someone to look after you, in the proper way, you could become the Greatest Musician of today; and just think what that would mean to you. You would meet all the greatest people of the civilized world, you would be invited to play before Kings and Royalty. If I had you under my care, what a life you would have, what wealth you could amass! Why-money would roll into you so fast, you could ask any price for your musical talent. If you played before Royalty -and I know I could get you there, what an honor this would be to you! What a son your parents

would have! Yes, the world would be at your feet if you played before them as you played last night.' "But you see, I do not know music, I was never taught music-only the scale." "Oh, we could soon overcome that. The way you play, tells me that you could learn, if you had a good teacher, all the music you need in a few short months. 

I can arrange that; and I would be willing to pay the cost, if you would sign a contract to play for me -for five years-at a salary I would be willing to give, that can easily be arranged. 

Just at the finish of this part of the conversation, the Man of Mystery appears, and they are all pleased to see him. Each of the five, who have met him before, offer him their chair.

He says, "No, but you (looking at the young man) may get me one, if you want to." As the chair is brought and he sits down, he says, "Did I hear something about some kind of a contract ?"

The man of business speaks up, "Yes, Sir, I have given the young man the greatest opportunity he will ever have in his lifetime," and he repeats, what he has said, to the man of mystery, who listens with a smile until the person finishes by asking, "What do you think of the proposition?" "It sounds good, or it would to the ordinary person; but you see this boy is a Soul-mind violinist.

As he told you, he knows no music and he means it; but as I listened to you, I felt I was in the right place at the right time. As I see it, you would turn this musical soul into a money proposition, you would deprive him of his naturalness and in its place you would put the artificial. He plays only when he wants to-you would force him to play because he

had to, you would take him away from the life he has and give that life to the masses for pay, you would take his free will and make him a victim to the very thing he has so much pleasure in, you would make him the reproducer of some other man's music and he would then become an imitation instead of his originality, you would take him to pay homage to Royalty when they should show homage to him, you would pay him a set salary and force him to be a cog in the wheel of industry-for as you know whatever is a trade so is the wheel of industry seen. 

He would no longer be able to think for himself for as you thought so would he have to think. He would not be able to go where he would want to, only where you ordered him would he be allowed to go. You would take him around the world and show him to the people, just as someone else would take a Freak of Nature so he would arouse the curiosity of the people and draw people to see and hear him. 

Yes, you have given him a proposition; but, when you see it as I see it, it would not be a proposition but a calamity. You would pull him down to the level of all music pedlers. No, it would be better to have a millstone around his neck and thrown into the sea than accept the proposition you have put before him. He is too great to play for Royalty; and since he is too great for them, he is also too great to play for the masses. He plays to one only and that is his Creator. Did you not see, as you looked at him last night as he stood there in the moonlight, the smile on his face, his very Soul pouring out his thanks to his Creator? Was he conscious of the people that stole silently towards him? Did he know he prolonged the great Ball of the evening and made

it a failure? Did he, as he finished, turn around to bow before the people who were listening to himto ask for an encore? No, the words he uttered, as he turned, were: 'Even this does not do justice to my thoughts of my Creator.' Ask the young man if he could play now, and see what his answer will be." "He need not ask, for without the emotion of the Soul-mind, I could not do justice to my violin, myself nor my Creator; it is only when words fail me that I bring forth my violin to help me express my feelings. It is only my Creator, my violin and I." 

The stranger: "You speak wisely, Sir, and you surely put a true picture before me such as I have never had before. Although, it is a dream gone amiss; yet I enjoy hearing you talk. I will mention business no more, if I may sit and listen to you." "I shall be glad to have you listen, since it is to you I owe the beginning of our conversation. As one thought leads to another and the Path of Wisdom leads us to solve one mystery after another and show life in its truthfulness-so does the man of 

Wisdom see the Principle behind every issue, as I saw the Principle behind your proposition tonight.

You see the Principle of all things are revealed as we live and seek to find those Principles. Man, if he will, can find the Principle of Creation and be able to use it." "You talk deep, Sir." "Oh, no, just on the surface. Would a man bury something deep in the ground, if it could be kept on the surface where he can pick it up at any time, or would it be common sense to have to dig it up when-

ever he needed it? The Creator laid everything man needed on the surface; but man in his blindness trampled it down until it was lost to view then it became a mystery. So you see only man made mysteries.' 

"Do you mean to tell me, Sir, that to you there are no mysteries in the world?" "Yes, and no, the things the Creator put upon the Earth for the use of man were not hidden in mystery; but how man had to fall over them before he saw them is a mystery. How a man could be so blind, when he had eyes to see is a mystery." "Very interesting; but I would like to ask you a question; and it is this: 'Does everything move in Circles?"" "Everything, even the Earth on which we live.

Since the Earth does, why should not all things of the Earth move in Circles also? For instance, the trees live and die and live again; the seas give up their waters to the power of the sun but after a journey across the skies it falls to the Earth and goes back to the sea again; the sun to us goes down in the west only to rise in the east again; the moon does the same thing; the mountains form and in due season will return to whence they came and new mountains will be formed once more; we are born, live and die, only to be born again. Oh! I see that brings you forward in your chair; but wait a minute and you will see life as it should be seen. The body is of the soil; when it dies it goes back to the soilto stay?—No, it crumbles back into dust of the

Earth again and by its decomposition becomes a nutrition to the soil and feeds the vegetation of the Earth once more; and then man eats of the vege-

tation and the body is back in its same form as before-of the soil-So even the body of man is in this Circle. On the other hand, you will wonder what becomes of the part of the body we call Life -What and who gave life to the body?—Our

Great Creator; so you see, we are of the body of our parents, we are of our Creator-really three-inone. Since the material part goes on and on so does the Creator's part go on in Circles. The Soul-mind was a gift to man by his Creator, and without this we would not be man: For this is the seat of the free will, the part of man that makes us man, or otherwise, we can progress steadily onward to the highest man or we can stand still or even deteriorate just as we use our free will with the Soul-mind. The giver of this Soul-mind gave us a Trust and that Trust is to bring this world to as near perfection of man as can be; but with this Trust, he also gave us a free will and with these words 'As thou thinkest so thou shall be' left us alone with our Trust." "Then how does the Soul-mind, as you name it, reach the height of which you speak?" "By accumulating more Wisdom every time it takes possession of a new born body." "Why! That brings in reincarnation." "Decidedly, it does; and why should not the Soul live over and over again until the end of time. You know, to a man of Wisdom, this is the only reasonable thought. I know, it brings into the minds of people new thoughts; and if they could realize the truth of this, they would make this world a better place in which to live-also it would be a great incentive to lead people to think higher thoughts. They would become more independent

and would realize that each man should know what he should be, how he should live and how he could advance if he stepped out for himself and not be led by the thoughts of others. How can a man tell another man how to live, if the other man were as big a man as he-if he is not, whose fault is it, the other man's fault or his? We let the other man think too much and we sit still and are content to abide by his thoughts-which means we seek the line of least resistance and by doing so retard the progress of our own manliness. If any man knows more than I, whose fault is it-mine only. He used what the Creator gave him, I failed to fulfill my Trust." “Then if man fails to fulfill his Trust, does he go to Hell; and if a man does fulfill his Trust, does he go to Heaven?" "That is a big question; and you know it has been taught to the human race for thousands of years. 

If anyone condemns the theory, the people look upon him with distrust; but I shall give you a mental picture and when you go home you make a sketch of it and then think for yourself-for after all, it is the individual that counts:-we will make a Circle to represent the world, then an outer circle to represent the Universe. Now you have our world in the

Universe or space. Sketch man on top, one looking down, the other looking up; on the side put two more men; on the bottom put two more men; on the other side put two more men, in each case, one looking up, the other down. The ones looking up represent looking up to Heaven, the ones looking down represent looking down to hell. The ones looking up see the beautiful Universe as far as their eyes can

reach; and as you look up the thoughts are naturally upwards. The ones looking down, if they have eyes to see, will see just this: First, the soil that gives us food; then the strata that are filled as the storehouse for man, yes everything that we need to live and advance in the line of evolution, then below that the molten heat of the Earth and without that molten heat we could not live for it is that heat that keeps the soil warm for the benefit of vegetation, it is that heat that draws the rays of the sun, it is that heat that gives us our climates, it is that heat that gives us warmth both summer and winter; so since it serves man so faithfully, how could it symbolize the place we are taught is down below? 

What do they mean, when they use the expression 'Down to Hell and Up to Heaven?' We get life both from the heavens and the interior of the Earth, then where can this place of punishment be but only in the Soul-mind, yes, the mind that goes with the Soul at all stages of life?" "You surely give me something to think about," said the stranger. "No, I would not say think; but listen to your

Soul-mind and as it answers so you believe: for as your Soul-mind is so shall your thoughts be. If your Soul-mind is advanced so shall your life be advanced in the Path of Wisdom. If it is not advanced, then how can your thoughts be?-How wonderful life is if you know how to live!—But otherwise it is a failure. So my Friend, as I leave you, I would say 'make life worthwhile, then you will not have lived in vain,' Good Night." "I wonder who he can be, I wonder how much he knows?" says the young lady.

Then the new born man says, "I, too, am wondering.'

The young Master-mind then speaks, "A night like this, with a Master-mind, carries me away into a higher plane of life and light that fills my heart with joy that I am permitted to live once more the 

Path of Wisdom to trod; for I will know more and more as life goes on in the Circle of life without end." "You also speak wisely," says the stranger. "Oh, yes, when I have something to say. A

Master-mind knows how small he is; yet a smaller mind seems to know how big he is. I wonder why? -Why the smaller mind thinks he knows it all, but the Master-mind realizes he knows nothing to what he ought to know?"

As they all arise to retire for the night, each one expresses themselves thus: "A night to be remembered. Yes, a night I shall never forget. I have only one fault to find, and that is the swiftness of time, the other a new Light to Life shown me." "As I listen, I agree, it was a night well spent; and now it is half-past three."

The picture failed completely; 

And as you read this chapter 

You shall see:- 

Great Minds sometimes in weakness fall;

But this one did not waver at all, Thanks to the Man of Mystery.

CHAPTER XVII THE STORM 

As the storm rages on,

A story it will tell- 

To the Young Master-mind; 

Then a story he can tell.

The next morning as the people came on deck, the weather is changed, the sky is black, a breeze is blowing and the sailors seem to be getting ready for something, the people know not what. All hands on the ship are at their posts as if expecting to do something. As a sailor passes our people, they ask him, "Does this sea ever get stormy?" "Yes, Madam, and when it does, we know it; for it shows no mercy. We have had a radio message that there is a storm traveling our way; and by the feel of that wind and the way it is gaining speed, we are sure we are going into it." 

As they hear this, our young Master-mind says, "I thought we were sailing too smoothly all these days. I do not think it would be a voyage complete without a storm."

As the time goes on, the wind increases and by this time the heavens turn black and the sea begins to show its first commotion. The ship begins to heave with the waves, the waves increase in size and the ship rolls accordingly. Soon the lightning flashes, the winds increase their mad rush to somewhere and the waters seem to be getting angrier. At each increasing velocity of the wind, you can see how the

THE STORM 

147 ocean is resenting the action of the wind. The more the wind blows, the more maddening the sea becomes and the more the ship is tossed upon the waves. Sure enough, they are in a great storm for the ship seems to be helpless to fight its way, it has to submit to the rolling of those huge waves. All this time, our young Master-mind has been sitting upon deck watching and understanding the Principle of the storm. Soon orders are given that all people must leave the deck, as the waves are increasing to such a volume that they expect them to reach and break upon the deck of the ship. The few who are still upon deck, leave to go inside. Our young man asks one of the officers if he must go inside. "That is the order of the Captain, so it must be obeyed. Would you want to stay here and be washed overboard?"

Just as he says this, a great wave breaks over the ship and this decides it for the young man; so, although, he can see the true picture of this storm, he loses his desire to stay on deck. He goes inside. 

A storm, yes, a storm indeed; and how that ship fights with the sea. You would think the sea is using every tactic possible to claim ship and people for its victim. First, it hits the ship one way then another until the ship groans as if every bolt is giving way to the fight; but no, she fights bravely until the afternoon, between five and six o'clock; when it seems to settle down. The commotion among the passengers is sad; yet you cannot help but smile. Why is it that everyone has such a horror of death on the ocean? There are weak-minded people who are sure the ship will never pull through; there are some whose faces are fraught with worry

as they think of their people at home waiting for them; there are some who are indifferent and say to themselves, "Well, if this is going to be the end, it has to come sometime." But there is one on the ship who lays upon his lounge and does not worry. You can see he is seasick; yet there is no expression of fear. Why? Because, he is a man of

Wisdom. He is asked by one of the passengers if he is afraid. Although sick, he answers, "No, He 

Who Creates sees the end of all things Mortal; and

I cannot see the end of this ship-so we have no fear, she will reach her destination safely."

The person who asked this question, at these words, dries his eyes for he has been thinking of his wife and family waiting for him and he knows how they need him. But the man of Wisdom brings comfort to him; and soon he leaves him to tell others, who are afraid, what he had said. Soon after this, the ship sails out of the storm, yes, and out of fear, into gladness. The people soon forget what fear they have had and talk and smile about the fear they have experienced. Soon all are on deck again. 

Evening is drawing near and the sunset is beautiful to see. After the blackness of the afternoon, the sun seems to throw out more light than ever, as it is disappearing into the sea. The sea is settling down again to its quiet peaceful state as only the Pacific Ocean knows how. Dinner is served in the dining room. Other meals of the day have been served, but there were not many people present at the serving, the sea has had its joke and robs them of their appetite.

This being the last night on board, a Grand Ball is prepared in the First Class part of the ship. Our

THE STORM 

149 young Master-mind is sitting, yes, and hearing the Voice of the wind and water telling what caused the storm. He is approached by one of the ship's messenger boys with a card in his hand. The messenger hands him the card which reads: "The Captain has been requested to ask you to play for the people on the First Class Deck tonight at 9:30. I hope you will accept this invitation for we have some very distinguished guests. It will be a great honor to you to play before these people. 

Signed-The Captain of the Ship."

Our young man reads the card. He has had the privilege of a sight-seeing tour through this part of the ship. He thought it was wonderful; but as he looked around and saw the people who sailed in this part, they seemed somehow not to be human-not advanced-but like pieces of statue work he had seen in museums somewhere. They gave him the impression that if they moved they would spoil their beauty or maybe if they would happen to stand up they were so stiff they could not sit down again. As these thoughts come to him, he smiles, takes his pencil and writes: "No, I do not accept your invitation. I deem it no honor to play before those people. I play only for my Creator." "Take this to your Captain, and tell him I thank him."

Just then his audience, as he named her, comes to him. As she sits down, he says, "I had an honor offered me." "What was it?" "The people in First Class requested that I play

for them and their pleasure at 9:30 tonight. They even set the time for me." 

She smiles for she knows what he meant. "Well, what did you do? Accept the invitation and have your head swell for the first time as you sit and anticipate the great coming event? You should be proud of yourself to be requested by the mighty of the Earth that you play for them and give them a few moments pleasure. They might even clap their hands, if they heard you; and think what an honor that would be to put them to that trouble. You should go just for the fun you would have. Did you ever see these kind of people try to show their appreciation? Some put their hands together very gently, some just hit the palms of their hands with the tips of their fingers of the other hand very gently for fear they make a noise. What a night of laughter you are missing! What a picture of camouflage you are depriving yourself! I deem it an honor for you that you have eyes and see and understand the hidden principle of life. If you could mix with them and see through them, it would be food for thought." "No, do not tempt me. I said no, because I did not want to; but as you talk, I can see a great opportunity. But it will come again. I would like to see this picture you describe, and see its two sidesthe serious and the funny blended into one. Some day I shall take the advantage of my violin and see life as it should be." "We could have traveled in this class; but I wanted you to see and meet the Natural first-then some time we will travel with the Unnatural class. 

It will be a wonderful revelation to you and a great

THE STORM 

151 study; also show you how far man is from the goal of man as the Creator saw him." "Maybe it will be good for a book some day. I would rather sit with you alone in the evening than be anywhere else. You know that storm gave me a wonderful picture, a wonderful revelation and the principle; but we will have to be alone before I can tell you." "If that be true, where can we be alone? Not on deck, for as sure as we sit here, you will draw an audience. You know, since you stirred up the curiosity of the people, everyone wants to meet you.

The only way I know is to go to my state-room." "Yes, but I would have to deprive you of this invigorating pure air; and we were compelled to stay in so much today." "What do you want me to do?-Sit here and listen to others when I would rather hear you? I would rather listen to you and gasp for my breath than breathe all the purest air in the Universe; and miss the story the storm told you today. At the time of the storm, I must admit I was afraid; but since it is over, I would not have had it otherwise." "You speak my thoughts, are you reaching a point that you can read my thoughts before I express them?" "Oh, no, I am just speaking for myself—To the state-room we go.' 

Since they want to be alone, let us leave them and go on deck. People are all settling down for the evening. As we walk on deck, we hear people talking of the storm and how each one felt.

When we come to the place where our young Master-mind has his chairs, we find four people sit-

The gold fields in the west were nothing to the nugget I had found. I would be-what?- 

Well-a gold mine without the labor. I would have nothing to do but keep this boy under my supervision day and night. I would have to watch his diet, watch his hours of sleep, put him into a routine so strict that he would not want to do anything of himself; for the moment the contract was signed he would be mine. ""Yes, it sounds good the way you put your proposition; but here is what I see: He would have to play when he did not want to, he would have to go where he did not want to and would not be able to go if he wanted to go, he would have to sit when he would rather stand, he would have to stand when he would rather sit, he would have to go to bed when he felt like staying up, he would have to stay up when he felt like being in bed, he would have to meet people whom he did not want to meet and he would be deprived of the will to meet people whom he wanted to meet, he would have to travel the seas when he would rather be on dry land, he would have to stay on the land when he would rather be sailing the seas, he would have to talk to people whom he Idid not like to talk to and not be able to talk to those whom he wanted to, he would not be able to stay where he wanted to-and all these things he would have to submit to to be able to make someone rich and maybe become rich himself—yes, in money; but no longer in principle. If he did accumulate wealth, it would only be to lay in some bank or banks for he would not want any more than he needed and any surplus is a drag; so you see the picture. What do you think of it?' "When I read this, it meant another night of sleep lost; but this time not building myself up through the channels of another but looking into life as it should be. The truth of this man's state-

ments staggered me. He leaves no room for argument, he leaves you no crevice to crawl through, he hits you right between the eyes and so hard it blinds you for a few minutes; then when you open your eyes to see, if you can still see, believe me you see a different picture to the one you saw before he hit you. I have had pride in myself for years to be able to drive any proposition home and win but this time I lost-no, not lost; but found a view of life such as I thought could never be. It is so easy to understand. He put everything in line and showed how unreal we are, how unnatural we are, how small we are, just imagine how he sees what we call big people.

How can a man bring such a train of thoughts forward in a mind like mine; and open up these things we think great and turn them into such smallness that he makes you dwindle down in your own estimation? The greatest joke is, we know it is true."

Door of Conversation and as the question is so shall the conversation be-ask for much get much, ask for little get little. Go on."

157 "Is it possible for a man to withdraw from the wheels of industry and still make a living?" "Some men, yes, and some men no. If have you gone through the routine of school and college with one special aim of being a cog in this special wheel of industry and you fit into the wheel easily, then you are in your right place; but if you feel discontented and feel a friction by your being a cog in the wrong groove then it is possible to leave that wheel and find your right place in life. But as long as you have a master over you, you are still a cog in the wheel, no matter how smoothly you run.

When you reach a place and then realize that no man should know more than you and prove this to anyone you meet; then you can say you do not belong to the cog wheel class anymore-you become the whole wheel yourself, when you reach this point. 

Then you can make a living by giving to others what no one else can give; and they give you in return only so they are not under an obligation. Even as they give, they will say to you, 'I can never pay you for what you have done for me.' To reach this point, you must not imitate anyone, you must be your natural self even unto refusing an Education. Education is the greatest destroyer of the natural we know of today. I did not want to go so deep into this, but one thought brings in another and as they come so must I speak them. For example: We will take three children. No. I is extremely bright and is of an intelligent mother. She has taught her child how to read and write, and the numbers and read stories to him and he being a bright Soul-mind drinks in everything. 

He can even carry on a conversation almost unbelievable; but the time comes when he has to go to

school. No. 2 is a medium bright child who knows quite a portion of letters and numbers; and it is time for him to go to school. No. 3 is a boy who is dull and has been taught nothing by his parents. 

These three children go to the same school on the same day. They are all six years of age, all put in the same grade and taught by Law. The child who is bright has to stay with the other two and go through the same routine. Since he knows it all already, he just sits and listens and answers the questions put to him. You can see my question answered,

Is it fair to either the child or the parents to keep that child in the same line as the dull child and retard the progress of the one by the slowness of the other?' No, it is when the parents realize that they have had a gift of a bright Soul-mind given to them and know how to withdraw the child from the line unto which he has been forced and bring him up as common sense says-and not the law that takes all three kind of children into the routine and sends them out all on a level. Have you ever watched the young men and girls come out of school with honors and great anticipation with what they thought they had an Education? If so, just travel with me for a while. It is a year now since you saw these young men and girls step into the world, each had the same education, each knew as much as the other for they had all to answer the same questions, read the same books, did the same problems. They went in as different minds; but they have been molded into a mass of people with only one mind and that mind had not been trained to think-but to memorize that is all. Let us see them when they find employment; we go into a drug-store and see one of

159 these bright young men or girls serving at the counter. Does it take a high school to teach this girl or boy how to make a drink at a Soda Fountain? Yes, as we travel on, we will see them in the mines, behind the plough in the fields, in the work shops as part of a machine watching one special part of the machine and that is all they will have to do for as they enter the wheel of industry they think that is good enough for them and in a very short time they have forgotten they ever had an education. The next morning, the young man and his audience, as he named her, came upon deck and she carries some papers in her hand.

So they sit down. She starts to read the picture. "As I sat upon the deck, the morning of the storm I noticed a slight breeze and as it played upon the water, the water seemed to enjoy it; but the breeze was in a hilarious mood and it kept increasing and increasing until I saw it was changing the tem-

per of the water. First, they enjoyed it then it began to enjoy it more, and was more in earnest; but the wind did not know when to stop. Because it saw the water loosing its temper it did also; then the fight began. It started by a gentle caressing; but finished in a raging storm. Since they are blinded by the rage, they wage war and destroy or try to destroy anything that may come in their path of destruction. They care not what they do, how many lives they take, how many fathers they take from their families, how many mothers they take from their children, how many brothers and sisters they take from each other, how many sweethearts they separate after the vows have been made between them that nothing could part them, how much material substance they destroy, they are seeking revenge and in their seeking they take the things that do not belong to them; and then as I see once more the anger being slowly abated and then coming slowly back to themselves and see them lying so still-I want to ask the sea the question: 'Why do you get into such a raging temper, why do you not consider that you hold upon your bosom, the lives of the 

Creator's Supreme Creation, why do you wish so much destruction?' The sea answers thus: 'You seem to blame me for all. It is not fair to me. I know, the things that rode upon me suffered by my anger; but you should ask the wind for it was the wind that made me mad, it was the wind that made me lose my temper and made me reckless in the things I have done. Ask the wind, maybe it has a story to tell too.' 'All right, will you, the wind, tell me why you insisted in working this quiet peaceful water into the state I saw it in today?' 'Why yes,

163 

I will tell you. You see, I, too, have a duty to perform and there was a great vacuum. First, the vacuum was small, and I thought I could fill it in by a gentle breeze; but, no, that did not meet the demand. I saw it was going to be a battle for that vacuum must be filled or it would cause great destruction somewhere.-So

You will have to question the vacuum.' Nothing can move without a vacuum unless it is pushed by artificial Power. Seemingly, it was I that was responsible for the destruction; yet not me, alone, for I could not become a vacuum of myself. 

Oh, no, it takes the Sun to create me-so if you want to find the principle of the vacuum, you will have to ask the Sun what part it played in the storm at sea.' 

Then the Sun says, 'Yes, I shall tell you how I create

a storm. I play all the heat or some part of my direct rays, intensely, on some particular spot; and as 

I do, I keep drawing the moisture until I get it sort of swirling around and by this means, the outer shell of the moisture becomes non-porous, and the air is drawn up and up leaving an intense vacuum-until there is a tremendous space without air. Then the walls of this vacuum begin to weaken and the moisture becomes porous again; then the air rushes in through these small apertures that cause the gentle breeze, but soon the pores become larger and the wind increases in volume to meet the demands of the larger pores; then friction becomes predominant and the great vacuum explodes and then there is a great rent in the outer shell of the vacuum. It is then that the wind rushes in madly ahead. It heeds neither man, beast nor anything that may be in its path until it has done its duty and the vacuum is no more.' I said, 'Then it is really you, the Sun, who caused destruction on the sea?' 'Yes, to a certain extent; but I am not prepared to take all the blame for I, too, have a mission to fulfill, not only one, but many. And this is one of the many. I have to do this sometimes to draw enough moisture from near the surface of the Earth and draw it up high into the Heavens so I can keep it high enough to reach the top of the highest mountain. It is then this vacuum is so often created. When I have gotten enough to serve my purpose in the part of my life, I open the cloud which I have kept so long and carried so far and let it fall upon the mountain tops to feed the vegetation, also down the mountain sides and into the valleys that the children of Creation might live. This is beautiful, but it seems we have to

THE STORM 

165 destroy the few to save the many. Even at that it sounds human.' "Then who does really appear to be the one to blame?"

He says, "If the Earth in the high mountains did not need water, the Sun would not need to create a vacuum; if there were no vacuum, there would be no wind; if there were no wind, there would be no storm on the sea; if there were no storm on the sea, there would be no destruction of ships built; then if all the ships that have been built were still upon the water, what an ocean it would be-so I think taking all in all, all we can say is that Creation is wonderful, the principle is beautiful and it brings out the two words cause and effect. If we could leave the cause for everything good, and remove the cause of everything evil, would this life be worthwhile?—No, for without a fight there is no victory. If a man is born of rich parents and spends all his life in full and plenty, did that man realize what he had?—No, he did not any more than the man who was born poor and lived poor all his life for he never knew what it was to be poor. Each one is equal to the other, and each values life the same; but if a man is born rich and loses his riches through life, he does realize what riches were. The same applies to the man born poor who amasses riches for he knows what it is to be rich even as he can realize just how poor he used to be. Life is just as you make it, it can be a life of greatness or a life so small that you would have to look for it with a light in your hand. My Son of the 

Light, some day, I want you to come and sit by the seaside; and as you watch the waves come rolling toward you, ask them this question: 'Why do you

roll on and on, do you not get tired rolling?' Then listen. This will be the last time I will see you for a long while; but we shall meet again. As you mix with the masses, do not let them drown out of your life, the Silent Voice. You will see and meet many people so I would say 'watch your step, and remember if you feel yourself slipping, carry a small box of sand with you and if you get afraid just drop a little under your feet.' You will find this a great help to you. You are going to enter into the fleshpots of the world." Then turning to the young 

Master-mind's friend, he says, "Watch over him, talk to him for his good; but do not go against his free will. Leave him have his want to; and we shall see what we shall see. I say again, go with him when he wants you; but when he does not want you then leave him go alone. A man must be a man, and he only can make him a man. I will say 'Good Bye' as we will be leaving the ship today." "Will we not see you anymore today?" "Yes, you will see me, no doubt; but I will not have the privilege to talk to you again. I will see you both again." Then turning to the lady, he said, "Do not let the two paths become one too soon."

She opens her eys and looks at him with a smile, "So you, too, saw it?" "He Who Creates sees the end of all things mortal. It will be a happy ending; but do not enter into it too soon. Stay with he who has been put under thy wing of love. A love that has come down the ages." 

To his young Soul-mind, he says, "If ever you need me, you will not have to ask me for I will be there."

The Man of Mystery does not seem to be anywhere on the ship in the landing; yet he is first off the ship and on his way before the others have. He waves to them and is gone.-Where?-I wonder.

After two days of rest in the hotel, our young Master-mind says he would like to go down to the seashore and watch a sunset. So they plan to go the next day. As they sit and see the Sun setting, a sunset that can only be seen in the West, the boy watches it without taking his eyes from the ever changing beauty of the sky as it slips down behind

the clouds. What a picture for a young mind of

Creation, how beautiful, when the Sun has gone down! He then looks at the waves rolling in, ever rolling, he asks his friend: "Do they always roll in like this?"
