He wants to find the beginning of it so he goes further up the mountain where he comes to the beginning of the stream. He finds a most wonderful bubbling spring. He sits down and the Voice says to him, "The spring can and will, if

A WHEEL 207 you ask it, tell you a beautiful and truthful story in mental picture." He smiles, yes the smile of yesterday, and is ready to receive once more-the spring starts its story:

THE BUBBLING SPRING 

As I wandered up a mountain 

To be alone for awhile- 

To sit and watch the Creator 

That seems so much to me. 

And as I wandered upward,

In the beautiful light of the Sun, It set my mind in action, Yes, reception had just begun. 

When I spied a stream of water 

Come rolling down to me, I knew I was getting thirsty 

So I drank from this cooling stream.

I wondered where it could come from So far up the mountain, you know. 

I thought I would follow it upward To see where it came from, And how it found its Path to Daylight 

The water of that little stream.

Soon I found the beginning 

Of that pure and cooling stream.

It was just a little fissure In the side of the mountain, you see. 

The water came bubbling out, And it seemed so happy and free. 

---

To give it a name worth while, I thought I would name it a "Spring." 

As I watched that bubbling water 

Come flowing out of the rift, I thought I would ask it a question; 

Whether it could answer it or not.

It seemed to smile at this question, And said, "Why are you so interested in me: 

I have given drink to thousands; 

Yet you are the first to question me,

To tell of my life from its beginning, I will have to take you back to the sea: 

For really it is my mother, And I one of her children, you see.

It must be thousands of years, Since my mother spoke to me. 

These are the words she said: 'I am sending you on a mission, And the mission you must fulfill. 

It may take you thousands of years; 

But what does that mean in our time? 

You will have to be kept in darkness, And I will press hard upon you Until you climb to the top of some mountain; 

Then once more day light you will see. 

But the Sun will seem so much brighter, Since out of the darkness you came. 

You will sing your song of gladness 

As you feel your usefulness to be. 

Yes, you will help some thirsty traveler,

As he wends his way through life. 

You will give to all His Creation

A WHEEL 209 A something that nothing else could give:- 

For without you, naught could live. 

Creation without thee would fail, To bring this world to be such

As we look upon it today. 

It would be a barren world, If you had not been put here to stay.

But that is not all the mission 

You will be called upon to fulfill. 

Although, you started so small, Yes, just a tiny little spring.

You soon began to expand 

And then you became a small stream.' 

But as the stream flows downward 

To its journey once more to the sea;

As it goes, we will watch its journey As through the land it flows. 

Yes, ever becoming wider 

And even deeper, you see;

Until it becomes a river- 

And that is stage number three.

By this time it is not for drinking, For it has become polluted, With all kinds of decomposition 

From everything living, you see.

But still it serves a great purpose, And an industrious river can be. 

You will see the material life 

Of that bubbling little spring. 999

So you see, it, too, has a circle

As all other things of the Earth. 

I left the mother, the sea, Yes, thousands of years ago; 

And as I see the light once more, I am just a baby again. 

But when I become a stream 

That brings my youth once more to me; 

Then I become a river- 

And once more go back to the sea.

As we watch and see by the Path of Truth- 

How all things are revealed unto us.

It makes our journey through life, A lesson in life, worthwhile.

As he finishes his reception of the story of the 

Spring, he for the first time, sees he is not alone but his friend is there with pencil and paper. He smiles upon her as he says, "Then you got it?" "Yes, and it was beautiful. I felt you needed me once more. How happy I feel at this moment! It is so long since I have had such an opportunity. 

I was afraid the end had come. You see you have been too busy with your new found life to receive from the Voice. The glare and glamour has drowned the Silent Voice out; but this shows me one thing I am thankful for and that is: It is not dead only sleeping. This gives me hope that some day you will wake up again and see with the eyes you used to see with and hear with the same ears you used to hear with and your tongue will be loosened and used to speak as of old."

A WHEEL 211 "Do you think I am so far down that I am not in touch with the Silent Voice? Do you think my hearing has been affected by the noise of the masses? 

Can it be possible you see me different to what I used to be? Can it be that you believe I have lost my Master-mind; if so why is there so great a demand for my company? Why do the people see nothing but bigness in me and like to put me in a place of honor among them in everything I take an interest in? Why is it I am sought after by every 

Circle of the masses? I am invited to the most 

Elect and down to the organization who work for the poor. Can it be possible that all these people think me so wonderful and yet you say you were worried at the change in me? How do you account for it? Where do I show the weakness? I still have the life I want to."

She smiles upon him as he says these words; then she says: "You may think you do but do you? Before you answer me, wait, you know I heard you talking over the telephone the other night. Some one was inviting you somewhere, at first you answered thus: 'No, I cannot come tonight, I have some work to do.' When I heard you say these words how proud I was of you; but the conversation continued, you kept repeating your first answer; then I do not know who was on the other end of the line but I felt you weakening. I would like to have taken the receiver from you and told the party, whoever it may have been, what I thought of them. It is you and only you. Soon you said, 'all right then I will be there.' Can you imagine what I felt like? Can you blame me for seeing as I do? No, I have not lost confidence in you for

I know the world and its tactics. You are young and think flattery is real; but some day your eyes will be opened and then again you will see. It is you only you and I must not lecture you any more." "You are my friend, indeed. What would I do without you?" 

She says, "How often I want you when I cannot have you, you will never know! How I miss those wonderful nights we spent together on the mountain! Also the first year we lived here, but the last two years have not been the same. I should realize that soon you will be a man in years as you are a 

Master-mind reborn." "I shall promise, from this day on, to stay with you more and get back into the Light of Life again.' "Please promise nothing. You see, you may break it then what?" "All right, then by my actions will you judge me." "We will see what we will see, and as we see so will we know."

By this time they reach their friend and her lover who is waiting for them. He is anxious to know what they had found to interest them so much. She hands him the story as she has written it. His eyes open as he reads and says, "To think I have been on this mountain so many times, drank from that stream, watched the bubbling spring; yet it never spoke to me this way." "Oh, yes it did," says the young man, “but the vibrations were not in harmony. To have reception, harmony must be mutual on both sides." 

They get ready to leave the mountain with a vow that they will come again-soon. They have spent

A WHEEL 213 a day of great pleasure to each of them-each one a pleasure of their own.

They arrive home, settle down with a night of pleasantness ahead of them; but the door bell rings and when the door is opened three persons enter, and, of course, invite them to take off their hats and stay awhile. One of them, yes the one who has made up her mind that if this young man has a Master-mind she is going to be the Master of that mind, says that they will not stay but will he, the young man go with them. They are going to visit some other people and she has promised to take him along. She turns to him and says, "Come, put your hat on, you must go, I promised I would take you." As she says these words, he gets up, gets his hat and coat, bids his friends of the day, 'Good-bye' and is gone.

He does not get home until late in the morning and he is talking to himself and making more noise than usual. She gets up and goes toward him when she notices he is not as he has always been; she sees and understands, and leaves him alone, his first night of wine. She does not sleep any more for she is troubled; but his life is want to not have to. 

I wonder how far he will go in the life of have to before he sees the Light through the fog that has enveloped around him?

The next morning he does not feel well, he has a headache—the first one in his life. She being wise says nothing. The breakfast is on the table; but he does not touch it. Then she says, "Not hungry this morning?" "No, I ate late."

The masses are taking their toll. I wonder who will win?—The greater mind or the smaller minds.

She sees him drifting from her day by day. He writes no more. He seems to be into everything, going out nights, and staying in bed late into the day. 

All she can do is look on and wonder if after all the Man of Mystery knew what he was talking about when he said to her, "Let him follow the Path of want to whether it be upward or downward."

The Path of Truth is not smooth by any means. 

Yes, the Wheel of Life goes slowly around 

And seems to draw him in- 

To mix with the masses in their giddy whirl. 

I wonder when it will end.

CHAPTER XXI THE RESTORATION OF THE MASTER-MIND 

Sometimes life looks dark and drear, Yes, to a point almost of despair; 

But once the Path of Life's Wisdom is trod They may get down but never out. 

This goes on for three years.

He has not written neither has he played his violin in her presence. He makes up his mind he will leave and travel alone; since he has seen the ways of the world and has met so many people-So he tells her one morning as she listens to him, her heart sore. Then she asks him if he was sure he is going to travel alone.

He says, "Not exactly alone-Miss So and So and her people are going also. She has been preparing to be of use to me as we travel." "Oh, I see, then you have found someone to take my place, I am sorry, yet glad as my friend, the new born man, and I are going to be married soon.' "9 He sits up straight in his chair, "Then I am not leaving you alone. How pleased I am to hear this! 

When will your wedding be?" "I only have to name the day." "Then get things ready and let us have a party, let us have a big wedding, let us invite everyone here." "If you want me too, we will because this will be the last day of my position with you-so we will celebrate it as we should. We will invite your people

and as they live so will we all live-for one day. 

Even I will join in in this life of make-believe for one day more before I take up a life of true homemaking in earnest."

He seems not to be sorry to hear this, but to feel as if he had been freed from something he knows not what. 

That evening she tells her lover of her decision. 

He takes her in his arms and says, "How wonderful you are! How sweet you are and how noble you have been! If you stick to your duties as my wife as you have to that boy-what a treasure you will be to me!" "The two paths have come together, let us keep close so that no one can come in between. When I was with the young Master-mind I was all for him, when I am your wife I will be all yours. I loved you ever since the first time I saw you. I wondered why you looked so sad, now I know if you were taken from me I too would be as sad as you were. 

How big and strong and wonderful you are, My 

Love! I do not ask for all your love—I will share it only for she whom you loved and who loved you; and she still loves you. So we will both love her; and in this love, we will be a three-in-one love." "You speak with such Wisdom, I wonder if I ever will be worthy of you?" "As the years go on you will be able to answer that question yourself. It is too big for me to even try to answer, at the present time; but I shall try to be worthy of you." "And I will try to be worthy of you. We will live each day to be more happy tomorrow. We will begin a new picture. Heretofore, we have had a pic-

ture all our own, but we will have one this time for two; and if I make a mistake you will paint it over as soon as possible—and if you do I will lose no time doing my part. Let us, in our new life, live to live so both of us might say truthfully when we near our journey's end, 'We did live.'" "Well, when does this great day come? What is the date? I know the month and the year and the other should be easy for you." "Give me until tomorrow, and I will tell you." "All right, set the date and whenever you say, so it shall be."

She chooses her wedding day and the time draws near and preparations are being made. They have a busy time. The idea of finding a man who wants her helps to fill in the regrets of the departure from her life with the young Master-mind.

Not much is said of his future travels. The day before the wedding she is doing her last minute shopping and giving final orders in a store for the things she wants delivered-she is so interested in her business that she does not notice someone standing beside her. When she turns to leave, she looks up into the face of the Mysterious Man. She notices his smile. The first thing he says to her is, "Well, I see the two paths will soon become one and that path will be a happy one.' 99 "Yes, but how do you know?" "Do you not think I can read the headlines in the paper? I did, even if it was over the shoulder of a man sitting in the seat ahead of me in the train. You see, I just got in this morning." "Will you come to our wedding?" "Yes," he says, "but it may be late before I get

there. But I will be there to see you leave on your wedding trip." He does not mention the young man; but as he leaves he says "I will see you again tomorrow after you have become a wife. You are going to be a fortunate wife. Good-bye."

On the wedding day the people are starting to arrive. All things are going wonderfully; but before the crowd gathers she asks the young Master-mind if he will play his violin for her tonight. He promises he will but says, "It is almost three years since I touched it." He has a feeling of regret; but soon forgets it. 

The young lady of his love dream enters the room and comes toward him and takes him away from his friend of the past-as though she has the right. She glories in showing her strength before the true friend of the past. At first he looks at his friend then again at what he thinks is his love, then leaves his friend. As they part from her she says to him, "Remember the story of the flower."

He looks back to her and she can see she has hurt him. When the two young people are alone, she says, "Do you not think this would be a wonderful opportunity to announce our engagement ?" "Why, yes, if we were going to be engaged, but I have not thought of that. No one said anything that 

I can remember." "Maybe you cannot remember, but you did ask my parents for their consent and they were satisfied. Since we are going to travel together, why should we not be married before we go?" 

He being a man says, "If I asked you to marry me and got the consent of your parents, although I

cannot remember, I am man enough to go through with it—but it is all a mystery to me." "Well, if you get too much wine and say what you do not intend to, the only way I can see is to make you pay for your folly. I could have been married long ago; but I have been waiting for you. You will be a big man some day and with my help to get you into the right capacity, you will make a man of yourself. You can talk as no other man talks, you can play your violin as no one else can, although I have not heard you play for a long time. You know you are mine, yes since the first time I saw you. I chose you as mine and how I have kept after you and when you proposed to me I knew I had won a victory over all the other girls-also this friend of yours I never liked her for keeping company with you when I wanted you so bad; but here I am and as the saying is 'if you go after it you can have anything you want.' Will you announce our engagement at the dance before the midnight lunch? Let us have all the fun out of the party that can be gotten out of it. Yes, we will eat, drink and be merry. Come on, step out of your gloom. To look at your face you would think I was talking sorrow and you ought to know there is nothing serious-not even in married life." 

At these words, he said, "Well, as you say so it shall be." So it is settled to give it at the last hour -as the surprise of the evening. What a party, everyone enjoying themselves to the limit! Only three and these are the three of most importance, the new husband is full of contentment for himself but he has to look upon the picture of the masses for the first time through the eyes of Wisdom-and

he feels a sympathy come over him for these people as he sees them. His wife is happy in her new found position and she too looks at him then at all other men and wonders. As she gazes around the people, her eyes met the eyes of her friend and Master of yesterday; but her Master no more. As she does she sees a sadness in his eyes she has never seen before. She smiles upon him but he does not smile back. She is worried and wonders what the mysterious man will say when he sees this young Mastermind subdued.

The evening speeds on and soon everyone is beginning to feel the effects of the wine. The party is becoming just a conglomeration of human voices, no one saying anything worth while. All at once a young lady stands up and claps her hands for quiet; then announces that her young friend will give them a surprise in his music-and another surprise after the music. She calls upon him to come forward.

This is a critical moment and it worries the bride for she sees the condition of her young Master. She has never seen him as he is tonight-he looks sad and so unnatural. It seems he has drank more wine than anyone present; as if he is trying to forget something that troubles him. Surely it could not be that she now belongs to someone else for he has told her he is leaving her. If only the Man of Mystery will appear. Her mind goes back and she lives over the past; and he always did appear at every critical moment-surely he will not fail tonight. As these thoughts flit through her mind the young 

Master is rising unsteadily to his feet with his violin in his hand. He makes an effort to steady himself, puts the violin to his chin, draws the bow across the

violin and as he does the sound that it produces seems to agitate him. He tries it again, but the sounds still do not suit him. He straightens up, starts to play and as he does-did music come? No, only moans, only troubled sounds. The people are anticipating something great and instead the violin seems to be as intoxicated as its master. He tries and tries, but the more he tries the more he fails. 

Passion, rage and hatred show in his face as he stands there facing the people. What a fool he has made of himself!

As these thoughts fill his mind he gives a hilarious shout and says, "You will not play for me. You just send mocking sounds to torture me. You who was once my friend. How I hate you! You are not fit to play upon.' 

The people sit transfixed as if they are statues afraid to move or say anything. As they look at this young man before them they think he has lost his mind. What a sight! What a picture of distress!

He raises the violin above his head to dash it upon the floor when a hand grasps it from behind and stays the destruction of the violin, his greatest friend, the only thing that could tell his Creator his feelings toward him. As his hands are stayed in carrying out the destruction, he turns in rage to see who dared try to stop him; and his eyes met the eyes of the Mysterious Man. He stands and looks at him and what a feeling comes over him. The man of Mystery changes his expression of sympathy to a smile and the Light of Truth comes back to the young man. He puts his violin down turns to the people who are still sitting wondering what is the meaning of all this. He gazes upon them, his eyes

becoming brighter, his brain becoming clearer, then he sees again from the eyes of wisdom for the first time for over three years. He gazes long at these people who a few minutes ago seemed real, but the longer he looks the more he sees. His mental vision is drawing a true picture. He once more turns to face the Man of Wisdom, then again to the people. 

They all seem to shed their outward garments of camouflage and he can see them for just what they were. He again turns to the Man of Wisdom and says, "Why, they are just clay, yes clay, of a color

I would not like to say; but clay-only they are covered with the beauties of man's Creation. What a hideous picture! Oh, what can I do? How can I ever take from my mental vision this true picture laid bare before me? Can it be possible that I have been one of them? No, it cannot be." 

The new bride stepped forward and said to the man of Wisdom, "I knew you would not fail us."

He turned to her with a smile, "Then why did you worry so much tonight?" "Oh, you know everything." 

To the people she said, "The party is over. You may all go home."

The young lady who says she is the promised wife of the young man stands up. He looks at her with a new light in his eyes and sees her for the first time in her true self. She says she is sorry the music has been a failure, but there had come through it a drama worth seeing. "But there is another surprise for you." She then turns to her supposed husband and says to him, "Now tell the news to these people and I hope you will make it more effectual than you did your music."

He stands up and looks at her and smiles, yes the old self once more. He then speaks, "My dear friends of tonight and of the past, I thank you for what you have taught me a lesson that I will appreciate as long as I live. You have entertained me in the past, and I did enjoy your company; but tonight the climax came-I made a fool of myself; 

I tried to give you music; but my Soul-mind, having been subdued to the point that it had nothing to give, refused to give anything and as you saw so it was. This young lady who has been my constant companion for the past four years, I must say, has been good to me and has shown me many lessons in life for which I wish to thank her. A few days ago she enlightened me on a subject of which I could not remember. She told me I had asked her to be my wife; and also I had asked her parents' consent which they had given." Turning to the parents, he says, "Is this true?" 

They answer, "Yes." "She made me promise to announce the engagement at this party, as a surprise; but since I cannot remember of saying any of these things—and I have only her word for it and marriage was always my last consideration, I will announce our engagement just as she wished me to do. But I will claim the right to name the date of our wedding. You are all here to witness this statement; I shall marry this lady; but the date will be published later. It will not be for many years-how many, I, at this moment, cannot tell. If she holds me, I will abide by her decision-that is all. I thank the hostess and new bride for this party. I thank all you people for being here and I thank my Master-mind (turning to

the Man of Mystery) for coming at the critical moment." He then sits down. 

The engaged lady stands up and says, "You think you will get away with this; but will you? You may, but you will pay for it-and dearly." "As you say so it shall be. Punish me as thou seest, I will not say one word against anything you may do. I see, I hear once more and the price can not be too big for what you have done for me tonight."

The people leave; but with a true mental picture such as they have never seen before. 

When the last one is gone and the four are together, the new married man says, “All we need is the ship, the moonlight and the swish of the water against the sides of the ship to make the night complete. What a day this has been for me! After I took my part this afternoon, I sat back and saw a play of the life I used to take part in. Can it be possible these things used to fill in my life? What a picture and the thrills! What a life! How interesting to the observer! What a consolation to see the finish of such a play! But as long as the hero comes out victorious, after you can see him as a lost hope, how wonderful! What a lesson! You said truthfully, when you said, 'You must watch your step.' How diligent you have to be, how easy it is to be led by the cloak of camouflage, how cunnning and deceitful they are! I think I once heard someone say 'Do not judge the flower for its beauty on the outside, but penetrate into its inner self and see the center.' 999 As his new partner of life smiles, the young 

Master-mind looks at her and says, "Yes, if we

would take advantage of the lessons that are given to us, we might profit by it; yet there is some satisfaction of being able to look for yourself and see as you should see."

The Mysterious Man says, "As I see it, there are no two persons who can see the same. This world seems to be for the individual. The more I see the 

Truth of Life, the more this seems to be common sense. To help others to start to see, we can sometimes put them on the true line of thought-That is the reason the bigger or more trained minds try to convey to others thoughts through their conversations or writings. The man of Wisdom knows that no one can see as he sees; but by putting the things as he sees them before others they grasp an Idea which will in future expand into a mental picture of their own-And even if the picture is not as you put it, they too will see and understand in their way just as you do in yours; and it will be just as real to them as yours is to you. You know, as we see so do we understand. We should never force our views upon others. If we are drawn into a conversation and feel as though we should take part in it let us only enter in at the right moment and it will fit in but if you force it before its time or hold it back too long; then it is a waste of time to say anything. We, of the Path of Wisdom, never talk to take up time-time is too short for us and we have so much to accumulate in this short life we live that we should not waste one moment of this precious time given to us by the Laws of Creation. 

As you two start on the single path together, you are both of the Life of Truth and you will start upon a journey tomorrow. Be sure to care for one

another, but both of you keep your eyes open and do not miss one page of the Book of Creation. 

Where you are going there is enough food for thought, and you will see many things together and each one of you will see the same picture at the same time; but there will be times when you will both be reading from practically the same page yet you will not get the same picture. It will be interesting to tell your picture, as you see it, to each other. You will have the advantage of a double vision. Time is getting late. I must go to my hotel. I will see you tomorrow again and will see you leave for your wonderful life in its new Path; then I will take my 

Young Soul-mind with me after you leave. He will not take the journey he had planned for him. I have many things to say to him in the next few days. 

Good night." "Why did we not ask him to stay here all night?" says the young man. "There is one mind no one can change so why waste time asking him?"

Back to the Light of Life once more, He will excel as never before For only a struggle of Life 

Will make another victory won.

CHAPTER XXII THE LAST CHAPTER 

The blending of two Souls begins, Yes, it is the Supreme gift to woman and man- 

Just as He saw in the beginning—

They come together in life once more. 

Let us watch the two Souls blended into one by the unseen power of the beautiful Circle of Love, start out on their new life, on new path, each one seeing only greatness in the other, each one thanking the other for the peace and joy that has entered into their lives. 

Love Is there such a thing? Yes, there is, and when found and understood, it is the most wonderful gift given to man and woman by their Creator. 

A Soul-mind love is something that cannot be spoken —only felt and the feeling is unfathomable. Is it low? No, it is so high that when you have reached high enough to find it there is nothing else in life matters for this love means everything, yes everything. All other things become so insignificant that you can hardly see them. Seek true love, it is waiting for you somewhere. Do not give up the search until you find it and when you do hold on to it-no matter what comes or goes; as it is a continuous 

Love. Yes, a Circle made by the Creator, a Love without an end. As I look upon these two, these are the thoughts which flit through my mind. What a beautiful picture!

Soon the Good-bye's were said and they left. 

May the Silent Voice go with them. "So we are left alone," as he turns to the Young Master-mind. 

There are tears in his eyes, as he says, "She was good to me, so kind and considerate; and the last few years I did not treat her as she deserves. What a failure I have been!" "No, not a failure but a success. She was with you through it all. She saw and understood and do you not think she managed the party well yesterday? Can you not see how she brought you to light and life once more, and how she showed what you thought you saw for yourself, the clay you had been intermingling with? She, too, is of the Path of 

Wisdom, and the wise always waits until the right moment before they act in the play we call life. But we will go to the Hotel."

They arrive at the Hotel and settle down for the day, the Master-mind and the young Master-mind to be. The younger was eager to unload his mind so the other sat in silence as the younger started to talk he says, "How can I thank you for what you have done for me in the past?" "Make your future worthwhile." "I have been a failure and I feel my position keenly. How blinded I was, how easily I became the victim of flattery, how I misunderstood; but what a lesson! When I first went to the masses, I thought by talking to them I might let them see life as I saw it; and they listened attentively when I gave my mental picture of the things I had seen. My intentions were good, but they surely got me. Yes, I can see it now. They soon deprived me of my life of

want to. They would make me talk, they would suggest going here and going there and bring it in in such a way that I would fall in line with them. 

Then as they did so would I do-soon my eyes were blinded to the Path of Truth and I was in a fog; yet it suited me at the time. I was satisfied to go with them wherever they suggested. How easy it was so simple to fall in line from one thing to another until I became one of them. Nay, I became wise, they knew nothing else, I knew and submitted to be one of them. I talked as they did, I listened and laughed at the things they did and even drank wine because I wanted to be a sport as they called me. Each day I would make a resolution to become myself again, but when night came a Voice over the phone or a knock at the door or a toot of a horn and my resolution vanished in the air. Each night they seemed to know just how much money I had with me and they saw that I always left with only enough to get home. How they used to tell me what a man I was!—And I was the victim myself.

I am pleased that I never thought it was the fault of the others. What a picture to look back to, what a lesson in life! Do you think I will get back to the life of a man again?" "You are still a man and anyone who heard you talk at the party will say, 'was he not manly the way he spoke and the truth he told? It is well he saw in time for we all saw and wondered why he could not, but we are pleased at the outcome for that young man is too big for us.' 9 99 "Do you think the girl I promised to marry and for whom I announced the engagement will put me to any trouble? For the life of me I cannot remem-

ber anything I said in regards to marriage. I never thought of such a thing.' 99 "No, she will not cause you any trouble. As soon as she learns that you are not going to set the date, the suspense will be too much for her and after what you told, she will not come near you for a while. 

Just wait and see, you can forget that part of your life. I will say you came through it like a man." "I will tell you why I never thought of marriage.

My first year in the city, we went to a certain place to eat and I saw a young girl there. It was the day after the first party I had been to when I looked upon the young lady who has been in my life ever since. It was a place where they feed the hungry and poor. As we entered into the dining-room, as they called it, we were met by a young girl-she looked at me and I at her. I felt something happen to me. I then looked at her dress, it was of a deep grey and of a cheap material. Her eyes spoke to me and said, 'I know you;' but as they did a picture of the beautifully dressed girl of the night before came before me and we passed into the dining room. I have been there many times since but never saw her again. But that face and those eyes follow me; and sometimes they smile and then again they look sad, not just sad but with a deep sympathy as if to say 'Did I just find you to lose you again?' Even the night of the party she came before me; but I saw her not in her dark grey dress but a dress to suit those eyes and beautiful face. That is why I told the truth. She gave me courage. Was that a dream or a drunken vision or can these things be?" "These things can be. She must be of a higher 

Soul-mind plane than you are-for the Soul-mind

can carry a clear mental vision to anyone who may be receptive and as she sits, wherever she is, and sees you as she saw you that day so does she convey her vision to you. You shall meet her again some day and, as sure as you do, you will both understand. 

I hope she has been big enough to wait for you." "Do you mean I will see her and we will get together?" "As sure as the Circle has no end-so shall you meet and travel years of this life together. You make me happy. No, not me, but the pure love you have for this true Soul-mate." "Can you tell me how long? I feel I need a companion who is greater than I.” "You will not have long to wait."

The night speeds on and when the time comes to retire, the one goes to sleep and the other lives over his mistakes of the past. Was it a mistake of the past? I say, 'No,' only a little delay from his advancement on the Path of Truth. But a lesson and a victory worthwhile also a glimpse of the lives of the masses. I know he will not look down upon them, he will only sympathize with them in his great mind. 

The next morning as they start to the mountain, he, the young master, drove the car. Not much is said. After a day's driving they arrive at his private place of abode. When they get there the Sun is just sinking down out of sight in all its glorious sunset, in all its panoramic scenery. As they enter the cottage there were tears in the eyes of the young 

Master, yes but tears of gladness. He said, "To be away from all the make-believe and to be back to the real once more, how wonderful!" 

The next morning they go out for breakfast to a

place not far from the mountain home. The young man is full of life and vigor but he does not say much-so there isn't any conversation. They eat breakfast and return to the cottage. 

The mysterious man says, "You will spend this day alone. Take a walk and hear the Voice of Creation in its purity and truth. Leave the things of yesterday behind. They are of the past-live today that you may know more tomorrow. Make memories today that you will be proud of tomorrow and then you will live in only the pleasantness of life. 

Remember the trust the Creator put in you.-Go, then and fulfill that Trust." 

As he sets forth he says, "If it be possible, I will." "You can, if you want to."

He spends the day alone; yet not alone, for he is once more in touch with the Silent Voice. He wanders to one point of the mountain after another, he begins to see as he used to see, he feels his strength of mind coming back to him. The birds begin to sing. He wonders if he could sing with them. He tries, yes he has not lost that either. How he sings with those birds again! Their song seems sweeter than ever before. The trees have also a new beauty, even the branches as he trod upon them were sweet music to his ears. "How glad I am to be alone once more with 

Truth, yes unbelievable truth. How great is the

Voice of nature compared to the babble of the masses. He has had an expensive lesson; but a priceless gift of knowledge. He looks back upon the three years of his life of have to when he became the servant of circumstances. "How easy I, unconsciously, slipped down to the level of the

masses. The slipping is made so easy by others when you are slipping and all the time you are sliding down you can make yourself believe you are lifting the others up until you find yourself just as far down as they. Even then you do not realize how far you have gone down until some shot brings you to your feet and causes you to open your eyes. Then and only then can you see how far down you are and at the same time realize you were not bringing them up but you had been going down to them."

As the day goes on he keeps alone so his mind will see himself as he has been before he went to the masses, the change that came over him while he was one of them and the feeling of freedom that surrounds him at the present time. "It was worth it. 

It was all me. I should have been big enough to stand firm but I fell and will not condemn anyone else for it." up As he said these words to himself he straightened and said, "I want to be as my Creator would have me of my own free will. So again it is I, and, I will not leave the Path of Truth again."

He makes this resolution as he is nearing the cottage and is met by the man of Mystery who says, "Well, how have you spent the day?" "Just as I would have it, I have gone over the past three years and although I have no regrets, yet once in a life time is enough for me." "I will say again as I have said before-Be sure your foot ahead is secure before you attempt to bring the other one forward." "I will." "We will eat supper then we will watch the sunset and the moon rise. It will shine upon the lake of

this mountain; then we will be alone with Creation as the most beautiful picture and as the picture is so will our thoughts be."

After supper they once more walk up the side of the mountain until they reach a place where a bench stands. They sit down in silence for just then the 

Sun begins to paint its picture. Such a sunset! Each one drinks in the beauty of Creation, yes the young

Master-mind is filling up to capacity and as this feeling became greater and greater he wants to speak his thoughts but they will not come. Tears come into his eyes and as the Master-mind sees he speaks thus: "Life, yes Life! Creation. Oh, thou great 

Creator. How good thou art to man but man can be so indifferent to thee; and thy beautiful manifestation it is beyond me! Time-What is time? There is no Time. Only Eternity. I thank thee for this word and for giving me a mind to understand the meaning of it as thou would have me. My son of the Truth, tonight as I sit with thee, it carries my memories back when I too was growing in the Path of Truth. I remember once I was down and sad because of my position, and could not see the advancement I was making. I felt lonesome among the masses so I left the world below and climbed up a mountain-Just as we did tonight, and sat as I thought alone; but was I, no the Silent Voice was there. The thoughts flowed in. I often sit now and go over that night." "Will you tell me of it?" "As I got there, the Sun was going toward the horizon. It was beautiful to behold. The Sun looked so big and it was the color of crimson. The sky was every shade of color imaginable and as it dipped into

the lake the slight ripple of the waves on the surface were more beautiful than man can describe. It looked as though there were two suns, one going into the water and the other coming out. It was the most beautiful sight I ever dreamed of seeing. The inspiration that came from it will go with me as long as I live. I often sit in my quiet moments and recall that scene to my mental vision and say, "What wonderful things there are in the world to see, but how few see them or look for them! How many have lived and died and could not say they lived.' It is seeing the panorama of the beauties of the Heavens that surpass all other beauties of nature. We see the lovely flowers, the wonderful animals, the glitter of the sands of the desert, the splendor of the rivers as they flow on ever on, we see all these things and many others but it is just the sunset in the Heavens that gives us that ever changing picture of beauty The fantastic shape and form of the clouds as the Sun goes down, the changing color of the waters as the Sun settles more and more upon its surface-It is these things that make man's mind crave for elevation of mind, life and thought-It is things such as these that make you determined to go ahead and get all out of life possible. It must be a joy to the 

Creator if he can see man, one of His supreme Creation, enjoying and understanding and acknowledging His greatness. I was so full of thanks to

Him who Created these wonderful things in life; and that He gave me a brain with a Soul-mind that I might see and understand how wonderful life is if I only seek to live. These were the thoughts that came to me as the Sun sank down out of sight. All 

I could say was, "Surely this is a gift of the highest

treasure for the Creator to create so much beauty and give man the mind and eyes to behold the 

Heavens in all their glory.' Man's language could never express what he feels; but what a consolation it is to know he can understand the Pathway to a future life if he seeks the Path of Wisdom. You see,

I, too, have been down many times; but I struggled on. It is a man's fight against almost the world-at least my world. I have brought you here to let you see the path you will have to travel. You are out of the first mistake, but that was only a small fight to the fight you have before you. As you grow in Wisdom, and the truth of life becomes more and more understood by you, you will see the principle behind everything. You will have a true picture and know by whom and how that picture was painted. Some will, at the first glance, appear beautiful but as you gaze upon it you will see the true picture for the camouflage will disappear before your vision. The people will listen and then become afraid of you for your tongue will speak truth and truth only—and they have not been brought up in truth so they do not understand truth. You will make many enemies, but only for a time. When they see the truth they will come to you for more truth. Your Wisdom will put you in such a position that you can meet anyone in any stage of life. You will meet many men of great learning at least what the people call learning, at the present time. They will converse with you upon all stages of what we call education; but you, as they talk, will be able to judge whether it is a master-mind or a mind with a cultivated memory. 

If, in their conversation, they keep quoting men of the past and the wonderful thoughts they wrote you

will know that you are listening to-not a conversation but a recitation. It is a pity we cannot have men today who know as much as the men of the past or can even tell us more of the earth today than they did thousands of years ago. If there are not any men today who know more than the men of yesterday where is our advancement? Is it possible that these years of Evolution have been of no advantage in the line of thought-Yet everything material has gone forward in leaps and bounds? Have we used up all the brain power to this direction material? No, I am thankful that there is one once in a while born who refuses to enter the Wheel of the masses and be molded as the masses are into one part in the machine of industry. Do keep yourself away from the material, if possible. I do not mean for you to be afraid, but to stand up and say, 'I was born to be a man. I have a Soul-mind still, the gift of my Creator in the beginning and I will not betray my Creator's trust. I will live to accumulate knowledge of everything that men know; but I will not be satisfied with men's knowledge. I will find the Book of 

Creation and will read it diligently and faithfully. 

I will ever keep my eyes open so they shall hear the words of Wisdom that will be spoken to me. I will find the principle of Creation and use it for the benefit of the masses.' If you keep your goal in mind at all times and keep your naturalness you will some day be a credit to all who know you-for they will be the better for the friendship and kindness and truthfulness you will give them. My Son of 

Wisdom, there is nothing you may not know. If any man knows anything about anything, remember, your brain is as big as his; so keep accumulating.

There is no limit to man's mind. This is a big statement; but as time goes on it will be proven to the people who listen and read the Book of the Path of 

Wisdom and Creation. You too will be able to say 'He who Creates sees the end of all things Mortal; but the continuation of the Soul-mind.' My Son of 

Wisdom, I have another life I have been watching even as I have watched you-only he has been brought up in the life of have to. He, too, is an advanced Soul-mind; but so far has been subdued by the material parents' views of life. Soon I want you to meet him. It is getting late and we must rest our bodies as well as the mind, so we will wend our way home. When we meet again, I will have this other young man with me. I will be gone before you arise in the morning-so I will say 'Good-bye' until I see you again. You may stay here until my return." 

Not the end only the beginning of a Circle. 

Look for my book-The Human Rose Bud No. 2.

One is the Path of Wisdom, The other the Path of Education.

PART II

PART II CHAPTER I THE MATERIAL 

Again we look into a home where another child is born. Yes, another little Piece of Mystery. What is this child? He is bright, his eyes are opened. 

Anyone who looks at him would say he is looking around as if to say, "Yes, I am here, and I will let you know I am here; for I came with a free will and I am going to use it." The parents have to submit to the will of this little piece of sweetness, this little piece of innocence, this little piece of purity, this little piece of something that is beyond man's comprehension.-A mystery, indeed! A master of the home, for how long, I wonder?

A few weeks pass, and we happen to pass the house; and another picture meets our gaze: We see the mother with a mother's love showing out of every expression of her face; yet there is a deep, penetrating, far-seeing expression that only the mother can have as she looks at her child. As we read her thoughts, we will understand whether she understands life in its true picture or not. She sees something great for the future of her child; but 241

she is looking through the material eyes. She, being of the material, cannot see that she has been given the gift of a beautifully advanced Soul-mind. He does look bright and intelligent, at least, that is what everyone says who looks at him; and she feels proud of what they say. But if she could understand, how much more beautiful her child would become. She sees him brighter than other children. but does she know, the truth, why?

As the days go on, she loves him more and more. 

He becomes more masterful, as time goes on. She submits to his every whim.

When he reaches the age of three, he becomes more interesting and the people express their opinion more and more of his brightness. He shows his higher plane thoughts by asking questions of his mother and father. He becomes more interesting to his father, as he grows up and asks questions which startle him. The parents try to understand but cannot; for this child is of a higher plane and lives in an advanced Soul-mind world. The parents are strangers to the world of Light.

Before he reaches the age of six, he shows a keen interest in many things and learns quickly everything that comes to him.

The time is drawing near when he will have to enter the material world; but can these parents see the true picture of what they are going to do to this young life? No, they do not. Then let us see the picture as a man of Wisdom sees it: The parents are earnestly conversing about their child, the mother says, "Well, in another month or two he will have to go to school. I can hardly wait for the time to come. I know he will be very bright and

THE MATERIAL 

243 intelligent. He knows more now than most children. 

I will be proud of him." “Yes,” the father says, "I, too, am longing for the time to come when he shall show his brightness before the people. I know he will carry everything through for he surely has an independent spirit. He surely has a will of his own. I am afraid his teacher will have a problem to bring him into the line of discipline. He has been master over us ever since he came. We will see just how he carries on in school. This is going to be an interesting part of his life for me." 

His mother says, "He told me today he did not want to go to school. When I asked him why, he said, 'well, I wouldn't let the teacher tell me what to do, and I will come home if I want to.' I wonder if he remembers the man I told you of whom we met one day? As he looked at our child, he said, 'you have a wonderful child, if you bring him up correctly, he will be a credit to you and a gift to the world; but if you bring him up wrongly, you will still have a big man but only a gift to the few for he will become great no matter which way you bring him up. You could only subdue him as long as he is under your power. Do not let him go to school.

He has an advanced mind which would only make him a man in a material way and would force him into the Wheel of Industry. If he is left alone, he will become the wheel of an industry, not of labor but of advancement of the masses. You may stay his progress, but he will establish himself, I have no fear.' I can never forget how this man talked; and you know our boy was not more than two years old when this man talked to me. Can it be possible

that our child heard this man and understood when he said, 'do not send your child to school.' He says these words so much: 'I do not want to go to school.'" 

The father says, "He is a bright boy and far beyond his years, but this Soul-mind advancement you say that man talked of, I cannot understand, yet the questions he asks and the things he says make us wonder. How can he be educated without going to school? That seems to be unreasonable. What can anyone know without an education? What business can he get into, what can he do for a living, how could he talk, read or write? It is these things I cannot see so all I can say is 'to school he must go.' 999 The day comes when he enters the march of the masses. He has become one of them so now he is master no more, his master-mind is his own no longer.

Let us watch him as he enters school with eyes of intelligence that speak to all who see him and say, "Yes, I know and I will know so here I am."

He is put into the care of a teacher. As she puts him through the preliminary questions, he answers her firmly and to the point; then he turns to her and says, "So you are my teacher ?" "Yes," and shows him to his seat then starts him on the Path of "have to" for the first time. 

Let us see what she does with this child.

He is put in a row with other boys. Some are bright as he, some are not so bright and some seem to be just dull, yet the three are placed upon a level. 

Up to this time he has excelled all other children his age; but today he has been brought to the level of the others. The teacher goes around among all her

THE MATERIAL 

245 new children and places a band of discipline around each of their heads. She sees the strength of this boy's mind so she has to turn the tension screw a little tighter on his band. This is the beginning of the life of 'have to.' He goes from one grade to another, ever kept back by the law of the schools, until the other boys catch up enough that they, too, can go to a higher grade. Our little bright, intelligent boy has to submit to drag along with the others. After he has gone to school for a few years, do we still see that strong manly-minded child who entered school?-No, we see a submissive boy and this submission tells its story only too true to the practiced eye of a man of Wisdom. He just submits and sits and says nothing, he has to put no effort in his work so he has no interest; and the parents wonder why their child who was so bright in his childhood could be so different after a few years of school. They had thought school was the only thing.

As the years pass, he seems to pass into indifference and have a very little interest in the things that interest other boys. The boys who are not nearly so bright as he seem to excell him; but do they?—

We will see as the petals open of what was once and still is a mysterious Human Rose Bud: For even now at the age of nine as you look at him you see his great Soul-mind within him; and the reason he does not talk as other boys do he thinks, "I have nothing to say then why say anything? The things 

I have to say are so easy and mean so little, why should I waste my time trying to tell people what I know when I could be seeking to know more of the things I want to know." So he says very little but

when he has to go through the tests he excells all the ones who have so much to say. 

Time goes on and as he reaches the time when he has to choose a vocation in life, for the first time, the Silent Voice speaks to him and says, "You should be a friend to Suffering Humanity." He looks around to see how this can be done but his parents have made up their minds he is going to be a Lawyer as his father was, so when he grows up and the father grows old he can keep the name going just as he has kept it going. He is forced to take up the profession as a Lawyer; yet he cannot get away from the voice that says, "You should be a Doctor."

He does not follow his own course but as his parents would have him. He submits to this, and as time goes on he goes through this routine of life just the same as he went through the previous years with no interest in anything; yet going through it with everything which is a credit to his parents but, seemingly, so small to him. The constant studying, thinking, concentrating, and memorizing is beginning to tell its story. He becomes quieter, more submissive to his parents' will and as time goes on he stays more and more in the house. He cannot mix with other boys, he is going to be a big man some day-so his parents tell him he should not do as other boys do, he must keep himself aloof from them. He becomes a lonesome boy. He feels he is getting nervous so he takes to smoking cigarettes to soothe his nerves; but do they help?—No, he becomes more nervous and his parents fail to see this for all they can see is the seeming advancement of their boy's education and are anxious to see him

THE MATERIAL 

247 excell other boys. The Soul-mind he has makes this easy to a certain degree but his brain is being usurped by the material, yes, nothing but material. 

He soon feels this for his head hurts often; but he does not tell his parents. He has many sleepless nights but they cannot see what they, unconsciously, are doing to their boy.

The great coming-out is near at hand. He is at the top in everything; but he is getting nervous, weaker of body and sleepless nights are more frequent. The constant studying, thinking, concentrating, and memorizing make him feel as if his brain would give way. He smokes more and more. 

The day of the examination comes. He has everything, he cannot fail. It is over. He went through it, he knew not how, for his head was in a whirl, his brain worked mechanically but he made the highest marks in the school. He was given all honors. 

When the day was over and he got home, he sank down and could not think any more. Next day he is ill. They, his parents, send for a Doctor. After the 

Doctor examines him, he says, "It is just nerves, the school has been too hard on him. Give him a rest and he will soon be well again." 

What a penalty to satisfy his parents! How much he had to give, they will never know! How hard it was for him to be submissive, they will never understand! That was over and he is sent away for a-what they called "a well-earned rest."

They are satisfied with their boy and by next year they would send him to college, and it would be one of the best in the country. How proud of their son even if he had gone to the limit of his material brain to satisfy them!

He left home for his vacation-alone, as his parents could not go with him.

He settled down in his new place of rest, and the master-mind soon reasserted itself and he did not read. Had he not read enough because he "had to"?

No, he would just relax his mind and come back to health again. How little his parents knew how near he was to the snapping point; but to sit alone and forget the routine of life, to just eat when he felt like it, to go to bed when he wanted to and get up when he felt like it-what a wonderful change to the dragging of life in school. 

He is taking a walk; but he cannot walk for his body was still weak-so he sits down to rest and as he does a stranger walks up to him and says, "Good Morning."

He turns to see who is speaking to him. As he does, his mind seems to tell him he has seen this stranger before, so he says, "Good Morning." "May I sit with you for awhile," asks the strange man? "I was just taking a walk when I saw you. Are you sick or just trying to carry a load that is too heavy for you and had to give way under the strain ?" "Sir, you ask questions in a very peculiar way but 

I think I can answer you. No, the load was not too heavy, but the monotony was too much for me, it got on my nerves. You see, I have just finished school and this thinking, studying, concentrating and memorizing became more than I could stand. I came through with high honors, but how, I do not know. It was just like a dream, it was so mechanical, but it nearly got me down." "I see, these things were too small for you and

249 you had to fill your brain to over-flowing with what someone else wanted. Your Soul-mind rebelled against it." "How do you know? How can you tell me what 

I thought no one could know but myself? I know

I am not as other young men are but I thought it was my own secret." "No, my young man, it is not your secret. If your mother had taken my advice when you were but a child, you would not be as you are today. But there is one thing that pleases me and that is as I look upon you, although you have been submissive to your parents, you have retained that independence to yourself. Even as I came towards you, I saw that your Soul-mind was coming forward to take possession of you again. As I walked toward you, you seemed to be saying, 'routine, how I hate you, why should I have to submit even for the sake and the pleasure of my parents?' How good to be alone; yet here I am to spoil your wish. But I must talk to you.' 

"You bring a faint memory back of a few words that come forward in my mind and they are these: 'this child will be a great man some day, if left alone to choose for himself; but even if he does not, he will still be a great man to the few.' It seems as 

I look upon you, those words must have come from you many years ago and they still come before me." "Well, my young man, since your parents have counted so much on your being a Lawyer, I say, 'go through with it but as you read the words of Law do not memorize them as you have been doing in the past. Read them through, but as you do, know that the books were just written by man and should

not be copied or memorized by you.' If any man can write a book on the Law so can you; if any man can see through a case, no matter how complicated, so can you. No man has a greater mind than you. 

If you are going to be a Lawyer, be a Lawyer and not a copier. You can, if you want to, know as much 

Law as any man, nay, all men, if you seek to find the principle behind every Law. Do not waste time reading the Law, see the principle then when you are called upon, the principle will not fail you. If you do become a Lawyer, be a man also and take cases only on their merit and not for the money. 

Before you start to produce, I would say, go to the offices of Lawyers, meet them, try to listen to the cases put to them and after the client has gone continue to watch your Lawyer friend and hear what he has to say. When you have heard the Lawyer's opinion of the case he has just heard, watch him to some book upon his shelf and look up a similar case and read it; then ask yourself the question, 'is this what you would want to do?' Some day I will meet you again and you will answer the question for me. A little more advice before I go. Let that inner mind of yours have a chance and you may be a Lawyer some day; but there is something more you could do, a something that you will be able to do that will be a wonderful help to Humanity. By being a Lawyer you may help the few but by following the Path that your mind lays before you, you will be a friend, indeed, to the many-how many, you will never know. Accept the material to please your parents; but cling to the Path of your Soulmind to reach the goal in life that you saw before you so many times in your younger days. Can you go

251 remember what brought tears to your eyes when you were a child? If so, then try to reach up to what you saw as the greatest calling in life. Can you remember what it was?" "Yes Sir, how could I ever forget? This morning I saw a child crippled and the tears flowed as freely as they did years ago.' 99 "My boy, some day you will find your true path of life once more. As the stranger leaves, he watches him out of sight. When he arises to go, he feels some new strength come over him. His head has lost its tightness, he feels like he has been kept down by some heavy weight and this has been removed. "Who can this man be? What can he know about me. He talked as if he had known me all through my life. 

I did not tell him I was going to be a Lawyer. I did not tell him I had a hidden secret which I was going to seek some day. How does he know so much of my life? Why did those words I have heard so much in my life come up so strangely when 

I first looked at him? Can it be possible that he can tell me more about my own life than I know?

He said not to study but seek the principle then all things will be easy. What does he mean that I can go through the hours of being manufactured into a 

Lawyer? A new thought already-manufactured into a Lawyer and they call it an Education. Now 

I see just what he means. How clear it comes to me.

wondering what he will talk to me about the next time he sees me. I hope he will come again tonight just that I may spend a night with a man such as he. I believe I can eat lunch so I will go back at noon today and see if this be possible. To meet a stranger, to learn so much in such a short time, to feel stronger in mind and body, to feel as if I could eat, to feel the band taken from my head, I wonder, yes, I wonder who he can be? Food for thought.

Once more his mind is set in motion but this time it is not forced mind action but natural mind action. 

Let us watch the outcome. As he wanders back to the hotel, his mind is busy reviewing the past; but he feels so much better in this short time, that his mind action does not tighten up his head. He finds a relaxation of the mind that is almost unbelievable yet true. After lunch, he sits down and writes a letter to his parents and tells them of his wonderful experience. He also teels them about the things the stranger told him and describes him to them. 

When the mother reads the letter, she, too, sees the man of the past and by the description knows that it is the same man who talked of her son when he was but a child. They become anxious about their son and write to tell him that they will come for a visit over the week-end. After he had left home, they consulted a Doctor about his condition and he had said that it would be a long time before he would get out of the condition he was in. He told them it took sometimes months before they could study again. He would not be surprised if he would not be able to go to college next season. He would have to rest and not try to study for as sure as he did

The last few miles of the parents' ride in the train is an anxious time for them. Any mother and father who has been in the same position can readily understand. I am going to live as I want to live from now on. I am so interested in my new life. Here we are at the Hotel, before I could realize it."

As they enter the Hotel, the strange man meets them and as he smiles upon their son and says,

"Good day," the mother looks from the man to her son-and understands.

He looks at her son with a smile and at her with a look of sympathy as if to say, "Yes, you would have given your boy's life for fame; but he is too big to be sacrificed for the smallness of life." 

As these words seem to float into her mind he is gone and they go to the room the boy has prepared for them. The father seems to have nothing to say for he, being a Lawyer, is a man all material. He likes his son and only sees in him a man who will enter the Wheel of Industry he has been in all his life. He thinks it is the only calling in life worthwhile. I wonder if his dream of his son will come true. We will watch the Wheel of Time as it slowly goes around picking up and dropping off Humanity as it goes, dropping some where they fit in life truly and again some who become an uneven cog in the wheel we call "Life." Where will it drop our young 

Master-mind subject to his parent's will? As time goes on, we will meet this stranger who has, it seems, taken a hand in the life of the young man, not to mold him but to let him see who he is and what he can do and what he will do for the benefit of the masses if he can lead him into the path from whence he came which will predominate, the material or the natural? For as "man thinketh so he is."

As his mother talks to him, the father takes from his pocket a special menu he has had written by a specialist. He hands it to the boy and says, "You must follow these instructions to the letter. CHAPTER II THE NATURAL 

Next morning, as they meet, the mother, a little anxiously, asks her son: "How are you this morning?" "Mother, I feel all right. I fell asleep as soon as my head was upon the pillow-and what a sleep, and how I dreamed, and what a dream!" The clients of the past floated before me and a voice said to me, 'did you do right in all your client's cases?' 

I was glad when morning came.'

When the father hears this, a mental picture comes back to him from the past, yes, when he was a boy. He used to go to see a Doctor who was very much interested in him; so one day he sat in the

267 tor was coming through. Something jumped right in before him with a tremendous rattle. When he looked to see what it was, a skeleton stood in front of him. Then he said the lesson for today is: "Remember, as you go through life, you will meet many, seemingly, great men of all walks of life; but as you look upon them, you can smile and say, 'yes, you are a great man in the eyes of the world but I wonder what your skeleton is like-for you know, every man has a skeleton in the closet which means secrets he dare not tell to anyone or people would know just how small he is."

As these thoughts run through the mind of the great Lawyer, he smiles and says, "Son, we are as great as we know we are and the only way we can estimate our greatness is to know truly how small we are. Here is where Wisdom comes in. It does not take you back to other men's writings, it takes you to the Principle of everything and when you have found the Principle you form the conclusion as to how you will handle the case. You forced him, although unconsciously, into filling his brain beyond its natural limit. You were proud of him for his brightness and when you heard people say how bright he was, it made you more anxious to put him ahead. How wonderful you thought your boy was! At every corner of the road of education, he asked himself the question: 

Is it worthwhile, if I have to lose my mind through it all? You could not see his position. You could not see him getting whiter and thinner and getting more nervous and restless. No, you had not time to look at your boy to see what he was giving so you could have the congratulation of the masses. 

Did he want this?—No, he did not; but you did, and how nearly you did undo the great Soul-mind that had been given to you as one of the greatest gifts that can come to a mother-if she could understand. You know the results of this thing we of the 

Path of Wisdom call 'life of memorization.' The material mind can only hold so much material thought; but a mind kept in harmony with the Soulmind advancement never can be filled to capacity.

A man of Wisdom does not think nor memorize, neither does he study. He trains his mind to receive, he trains his ears to listen and he soon can speaknot things that are repeated by thousands of others, but original thought such as I have given you tonight. 

There is no limit to the Path of Wisdom. When your boy goes back to school or college, he will go through it without the same routine that he has gone through. I am going to teach him how to find the 

Principle of Law; then he will see the Principle behind everything that is written in law books. He will not have to read and memorize; but by the time he has to go through his examination he will know more than his examiners. He will know the 

Principle and they will only know what you know -the memorizing of book after book until you get your license then you forget; for you keep your office full of books so you can read from time to time. Your son, if he ever has an office, will need few books; and he will be a Lawyer, indeed." "You make me feel both ashamed of myself and proud of myself—ashamed to think I do not know more and proud that my boy has found such a friend as you." "I may need a Lawyer, someday; and then I will know where to find a good one."

The young man now speaks for the first time and says, "I did not want to be a Lawyer; but since you say you may need one, I will not disappoint you when you call upon me. I have an inward voice always calling me to be a Doctor and how can I be both?" "A good Doctor who understands the Law will be both a Doctor and a Lawyer in one. They can go

THE NATURAL 

275 hand in hand and since there is no limit to a Soulmind such as you have, I would say young man, 'go the limit.' Since you will not have to memorize any more, there is no danger of your mind going back on you." "I want to ask you another question. How do you know so much about every line of life? It seems you can go into the inner circle of all lives and describe them so truthfully." "All I can say is: 'seek Wisdom and ALL things shall be revealed unto you.' If you borrow not your neighbor's thought then you will be obliged to use your own brain to think for yourself. Why should you not know as much as your neighbor, and why should not your neighbor know as much as you; if you both seek then you shall both find. Do not find fault with the other man if he knows more than you. 

Blame yourself for not knowing how to know as much as he. You see, through life, it is always you.

If you know much, it is you, if you know little, it is still you." "You make it very clear and very interesting.

There is another question I would like to ask and if this one brings such an answer as the other, I am going home with a lot more than I came with. Can a man be honest with himself if he is honest with the masses?" "A man cannot serve two masters, honestly. In the first place, the masses would not let you be honest to them for they do not know the meaning of honesty. To try to be honest to them, you would be dishonest to yourself. I would say to that question, 'do not take a case to defend unless a person is not guilty; and never try to prove a man guilty

when you know he is innocent.' You will then be honest to yourself and honest to the masses of whom you are the servant; for after all you know a

Lawyer is just a servant to the people, without a case he could not be a Lawyer-only in name, and what is in a name? What is a profession, if no one needs our services? What does Education or even

Wisdom mean to us, if we cannot give to the people the fruits of our labor?" "You talk as I never dreamed man could talk. 

We have another hour to stay with you; but I am so glad you have taken so much interest in our son. 

We shall go home with a new light in life. You have surely shown the Principle of life as it should be; but how are you going to let the masses see as you see? How will they ever understand this life as you understand it? You know it is beyond the comprehension of even an Educated mind.-The true picture you can give of the things we think no one can know but ourselves. Someday, I would like to hear you talk of the Laws-what Laws we should retain and what Laws we should cast aside. I know by your conversation you understand the Principle. 

No one can dispute that; but how can you know so much?" "My life is Wisdom. Nothing but Wisdom can satisfy me. I have a mind as big as any man; so you see that makes my mind as big as all minds. I can give a true picture of any life no matter what that life is from the position of the, what we call, 'biggest' man to the lowest man. Their lives, to me, are an open book." "Is it possible that you can see every issue before our country today?"

THE NATURAL 

277 "Yes, and yesterday, today and tomorrow. I live to know not to think. I live to see and seeing understand not to see as the masses see. I live to listen-not to talk and I am a good listener. My life is all accumulation-not of the material things of life for they mean nothing to me. Let men seek fame, let men aspire to become great before men, let them seek position in any way they want, let them become great in the eyes of the masses. What do I care for fame? What do I care for anything but 

Wisdom? I would not exchange one speck of my

Wisdom for all the position in the world. I would not accept the riches of the richest man for the Wisdom I have. Money means nothing to me, I give as fast as I make. I do not want any of the material things of this world. I want to live to leave a memory worthwhile. To live and be forgotten in a few days is a life of failure not victory. I am going to make my life a victory. All the world is after money; so that leaves all the Wisdom for me. I have no competition. I have the vastness of the 

Universe to draw from. Let the masses have the material; but let me live in Wisdom. Let me hear the Silent Voice of Creation. Let me read the book of Creation, yes, the Voice of Creation is wonderful. 

When all others are talking, let me listen, then the principle of all things are opened unto me. How vast is the field! Do you know if I would write all

I know to the world, it would take me years, hundreds of years to tell all I know at the present time; and too, if I live for a few years more and keep accumulating as I am doing, how long will it take me to tell it all to the masses?" "Do you intend to write and reveal all the

knowledge of all seemingly great men and organizations?" "Yes, I will start first with one then another and as I do I will give a true picture of the principle behind each. I will take in everything that is worthwhile and lay before the people the inner principle of all things. The sooner the masses know the truth of all things, the sooner will the masses begin to advance. They have been led like sheep and oppressed by fear and told how to live by others who know not how to live themselves. They will see and understand the truth as it should be understood." "You have a big man's work ahead of you; but as I look at you and hear you talk, you seem to change completely my view of life. I wonder if I will be the same lawyer when I go back home that I was when I left." “All I can say, as it draws near to your time of departure: 'as you think from now on so you shall be.' ' 999 "You have surely given me food for thought. I hope the soil is fertile."

The son then speaks and says, "I feel a new strength, a new awakening, a new life taking possession of me; but Dad your train leaves in fifteen minutes. You have no time to waste. Get ready,

I see Mother is ready and anxiously waiting for you. 

"I never felt the parting from anyone in my life as I feel at this moment, but I must go. Good-bye. 

You will never know what a burden you have taken from us. I feel differently.' 99 When they leave for the train, the parting is real

THE NATURAL 

279 -a new friendship, such as this man has never known before.

When the young man arrives at the hotel, the strange man was still sitting in the lobby. He walks toward him and says, "Once more I am alone yet not alone; for I have you. I am thankful to you for the things you told my Dad. He, I know, will look deeply into this for he is a man of thought.' 99 "I hope so," said the strange man; "but it is time you retire to rest. I will see you again tomorrow." 

They part for the day; but what a day. The young man prepares to retire. His head is full but not of the things of the past but of the anticipations of the future. Will he be able to sleep with his head full of the wonderful things he heard that day?

He sleeps and as he sleeps let us take a look at him: It is almost eighteen years since we looked upon this little piece of mysterious humanity; but as we watch the petals being unfolded, we see a hand molding them into the shape they would have them not as nature meant them to be. We see the 

Human Rose Bud submitting to the molding only through the respect he has for the one who is, unconsciously, molding him and doing away or depriving him of the natural beauty. He comes forth to give to the world; but as we watch him and gaze into his inner life, still a mystery, we see a light, a life not yet known to those to whom he has comea part that cannot be molded, a part that has the seed of greatness taken care of until it can come forth in all its beauty.
