The outer petals are molded by the material; but the inner are kept compact until they can come forth and show in their advanced beauty. It has to submit to almost destruction to

be able to come to its own without hurting the feelings of one who thought he was giving something worthwhile. How easy to make a mistake, an unconscious but sad mistake!

We look upon him, as he lies quietly, his mind at rest as well as his body. What a picture! Someday this light will shine and bring forth thoughts that have never been brought forth before. He will see and understand the principle of the great power that is felt and can be used-but not explained. 

Only by the results of he who would use it can it be seen by the masses. A great Soul-mind dwells within the body of the young man sleeping there. What a picture! What a life! What a future is waiting for him-only a man of Wisdom sees! Let us leave him in peaceful slumber. Our thoughts of him may make him restless; so I would say unto thee, "My Son, a Master-mind, peace be with you. Soon enough, the day will dawn for thee and as the dawn so will the day become the awakening."

THE AWAKENING 

281 

CHAPTER III THE AWAKENING 

Morning is here again.

The young man opens his eyes and is surprised at himself. "Sleep? Yes, and what a sleep! How good I feel. Can it be possible that after the excitement of yesterday, I feel so good today? Are the days of miracles over? No, they are still with us and this is one of them. Three days ago, I was afraid of my mind, today I am sure of it—what a revelation! Can this be? I must get up." 

As he does he feels like singing to the morning

Sun that is streaming in through the room; but he just stands in front of the window and lets the Sun throw its stream of light upon him. These words come to him: "Life, beautiful life! I will live to appreciate thee to my heart's content, you glorious 

Sun. I love thee for are you not the Light of Life?

I understand what life is for the first time in the years that I have lived. Have I lived? No, not until today. I will continue to live and make life worthwhile by doing all I can to fulfill the mission I am here to fulfill. I wonder who the strange man is, who has brought me to life? How can I pay him for this? To offer him money as my father suggested would be an insult. I will ask him today how I can repay him and as the price so shall I give." These are the thoughts that are flowing through his mind

as he takes his bath and prepares for another day with the strange man.

Full of anticipation, he enters the dining room and sees his friend and joins him at the table. They eat and the strange man suggests they take a ride into some quiet spot away from the masses. They go to find the place they desire. They sit down and the strange man inquires how he slept. He tells him he slept well with no uneasiness, no rambling thoughts, no dreams as he used to have; and says he feels so light and healthy that it is beyond his comprehension. "Yes, now it is beyond your comprehension; but when you advance in years you will understand all these things which to you and the world are a mystery. You are the continuation of a great Soul-mind but that Soul-mind is great enough to wait until your material mind matures enough before it starts to reveal your past knowledge to you. It has kept you up so far but only to keep your strength of mind. 

By silence, you know, you have seen and understood many things of life that you have not spoken of. 

As you looked at the masses and placed yourself among them, you were a misfit, were you not?" "That is true, Sir; but I did not understand and I just always thought it was I who was not big enough or I had a selfish feeling that kept me from among them." "That was not selfishness, it was your Silent 

Voice of your Soul-mind preparing you for your future life. It was telling you in its own way that you could not be with the masses, be as the masses, do as they do and rise to your true place in life; for your life is not of the masses but to be a guiding

THE AWAKENING 

283 light to them. You will show the world in your future life that there is at least one man who lives by the true principle of manhood. You will cause such a stir in the world that your name will be spread to the whole of the civilized world. This is your life's mission. You will cause the people to see the truth in such channels that today you could not dream of. 

You will master all the problems of your course of education without studying or memorizing. As your lessons come up before you, the other pupils will be memorizing the things they read word for word so they can repeat them to the satisfaction of their examiners, you will be sitting, not memorizing, not reading the books from back to back but you will go through them only by scanning. As you find a principle, you will sit and let your own mind find the answer to the problem. You will not write for examination the things you have memorized; but will analyze the principle such as the principal of the college has never had put to him. Your answers will be short and to the point; and yet will leave no room for doubt. You will go through these things so easily that your strength will wax stronger instead of weaker. You will become calmer instead of growing nervous. You will realize that the man above you is only above you in position-only in the eyes of the world. To you he will look just like a natural man working for the food he needs to eat, just as any other laborer does. You will see man just as he should be seen. There are all kinds of pathways for man to follow in life; yet the pathways all spell Labor or Starvation. So you have but to choose whether you take the path of natural labor or mind labor. Each one draws from the strength

of the body and each one is ready to lay down at night so they can collect enough energy to go through the tomorrow. You, too, will have to take a path but the path in the future will not have the same motive power. Money is the motive power of the masses; but to you, as you enter the Path of 

Wisdom, your motive power will change from money to the help you can be in advancing the thought of the masses to a higher level of life. Do not look that way for I know what I am saying. I know this seems so big to you that you are thinking, 'can this be;' but it will be, as you can not be other than you are a master-mind." "It does seem too big for me; but when I hear you talk, I know I have heard the same talk before and it is so natural; yet so wonderful that it fills me with wonder if it can be true. I feel satisfied it is; but how I am going to get through college without studying or memorizing is more than I can understand." "I see your line of thought; but I must contradict you. I do not think, I either know or I do not know. What I do not know, I never speak of. 

What I know, I speak truly. When I tell you you will go through college, you will see the true and the real and also the foolish sides of Education, you will look ahead to your future life; yet you are going to be a lawyer. As you go through the Educational channels, go after the principle of everything you will need. The things you see you will not need, why should you waste your time even glancing over them? I say, 'waste not one minute over the things you do not need to know, leave that part to the others. Why drink three glasses of

THE AWAKENING 

285 water, if one glass quenches your thirst? Why walk a mile, if you can get what you want by walking onethird of a mile? Why carry anything you do not need? Never over-load your mind with the thoughts of others. You know, if you do, your mind will get so full of other men's thoughts that you will have no room for the things you should know of your self. 

You are only going on eighteen years, I want you to know and know this statement is true: 'no man should no more than you, no matter what it may be.'

The principle shall be open to you to see as well as the other man. You can know as much as all men. I do not mean the small things of life; but the great things which are controlled by a principle. Live by principle and you will be then a principle of yourself. 

There is no height you cannot reach. You have the

Soul-mind, you have a beautiful material brain, let the Soul-mind take care of both and you will master anything that comes into your path. Here is a secret worthwhile knowing, and it is this: You can never accomplish anything by becoming nervous and excited, nor meet an enemy by getting as worked up as he; so when these things come into your life, instead of getting excited just settle down and you will in this way be able to meet any issue in life. Do not participate in anything as you go through college that would burn up your energy. Do not do any routine of exercises. Do these only when you want to do them, and only as long as you want to. 

Never mind the Law laid down by the others—A Law cannot be made to fit into the life of every individual. We, each one of us, should understand when we want to do these things and when we do not, why do them?-They then become labor in the

disguise of exercise. Keep your body in tune with your Soul-mind and you will see and understand the blending of the greatest thing we call music. When the Soul-mind and the body blend together, it puts the sweetest music into insignificance. You will feel such harmony as you never dreamed. I would say unto you, 'walk the Path of common sense as you see it.' You will have a beautiful strong healthy body and they will invite you to do this and do that to try to hold up the tradition of the college; but let the others keep up the material tradition and when you leave you will have done more for the college that keeps up the tradition of the material. 

You will have lifted up the tradition of mind culture higher than it has ever been before and your name will live on for years-not for the game you played but for the new advanced thoughts you brought into the college with you and left them as a credit to the college for the ones who follow behind you and will benefit by the things you taught even the Principal of the Educational Institution. 

As I speak to thee so shall it be. That is enough for today. Do not try to memorize anything I have said to you; but as the days go on each sentence will come back to you in mental picture. As you enter college next season and sit to take your lessons, you will never forget to look for the principle and after you have found it, and, surely you will, the other reading will appear useless waste of time. Listen to the Silent Voice and it will lead you into the right 

Path. It is the dinner hour, we will return." "Will you see me again tonight? The morning has gone so quickly, I had something to ask you; but I have been so interested in these almost unbe-

THE AWAKENING 

287 lievable things you have told me, I forgot to ask you what my father said I should." "If it is anything material, keep it to yourself; but if it is to clear up something that is on your mind then I will listen to it-not today but tomorrow morning. I will see you after dinner and we will take in something different. We will see what they have by way of music or plays and will choose the one that will interest you most. Stop that taxi, we must eat.” "Oh, I do not care about eating," says the boy, "you have fed me a meal that means more than any food could. I care not for food of the material. I am full of food of Wisdom. What a difference!" "You must bear in mind that we must eat to live as well as live to feed on Wisdom. Food for the material is essential and food for the Soul-mind is essential when the brain is strong enough to receive and hold. A weak body means a weak mind. A weak mind cannot receive great thought; so eat, my son, yes, eat enough but not too much. Drink enough but not too much. Try to be big enough no matter what is put upon the table. You know there are many people who enjoy a meal but see so much that they keep on eating. It just takes one bite too many to spoil all the enjoyment of the whole meal. I would say, 'stop at the enjoyment of the meal; then the thoughts of the cook are real and truthful. Here we are, I will see you again after dinner tonight. Good-bye until then."

CHAPTER IV THE REVELATION of the PRINCIPLE 

Let us look at the boy as he sits in his room all afternoon. Let us hear the Silent Voice as his Soulmind comes into action: "He said not to memorize. 

When he said these words to me, I could not see how we could remember without memorizing; yet he talked three hours to me. I know I could not memorize as fast as he talked; yet here I am seeing in mental picture his whole conversation of this morning. How can this be? Will I be able to see the principle of all things as he said I would? He surely would not tell me things if it were not true. 

I am doubting but can you blame me? Such possibilities, I never dreamed of. He relieved my head of its pressure, when I thought I would lose my mind. He talked three hours and my head is just as cool and easy as if I had not had a thought." 

As the afternoon speeds on, he is, for the first time, having a taste of the Path of Wisdom. He looks at his watch and sees he will have to hurry if he is going to dress for dinner. He smiles and says, "If life for me passes as swiftly as this day, how will 

I ever reach the goal he has put before me?"

He hurries to the dining-room, and sees the strange man already eating. He joins him with a smile as he says, "This afternoon has fled so quickly I could hardly believe my watch. For the first time, I am late."

"Sit down, I ordered your meal to be served as soon as you get here;" and as he speaks the waiter brings the young man's meal-a man's meal—a steak and french fried potatoes. The boy looks at it with a smile and says, "How did you know what 

I wanted?" "I know your brain has been working hard and this is the coal to the fire of the human system. I knew you would need a little more coal so there you are. I see there is a great actor in town and the play is 'The Merchant of Venice,' a wonderful lesson in Soul-mind advancement. I want you to see it and tomorrow you will tell me your views of it; then I will let you see the great principle in this just as the writer saw it-for he was a man with an advanced Soul-mind. In every play he wrote, he brings his great victory out by a mental picture; so I want you to see where the mental picture comes in. You see there is a great principle behind the scene."

They leave for the play. The stranger, as they sit and look at the play, does not say a word; but lets his pupil, in the Path of Wisdom, see for himself. 

As they leave, the strange man asks, "Did you enjoy the play?" "Yes, Sir, I surely did. What a play! What a character! What a noble victory!" "It was well done; but some day you will bring a more wonderful picture to the world. You will also use a principle as yet you do not understand.

As time goes on and your eyes are opened and you are called upon to use your Soul-mind, you will see it will not fail you, if you follow its message. We are at the hotel. I will sit here awhile. It is late so

you go to your room; and remember, no sitting up, you must retire at once. As you lay down forget the play you saw, forget the day and rest, that you may be able to be ready for more tomorrow." 

The next morning they meet again after breakfast and take their car to the same place as yesterday. They sit down.

The young man is so full of anticipation, he can control himself no longer. He looks at the strange man and says, "Is life not just one wonderful revelation after another? How much can we get out of life, if we just know how to live?" "Yes, Son, that is the secret of all-knowing how to live. I am leading you to the path that will show you how that word can be fulfilled to its capacity; when all things that man can know will be yours.

You will and can know as much as any man; and since you can know as much as any man that puts you into the knowledge of all men. As your mind expands, you will find an area so expansive that you will sometimes, when you talk to others and they talk to you, wonder where the limit of your mind is.

Again, I say, 'there is no limit to the mind of man if he once realizes that the human mind is just the servant of the Soul-mind.' As the Soul-mind is so shall the human or material mind be. You will see life differently, you will see people differently and you will see man as man. This will take the barrier from between men, no matter what position, and let you see the man without the position; and according to the real so will you estimate the bigness of the man you are looking at. When you meet what once seemed to be your superior you will see them on your level. You will be able to meet any man in any

position and know as much about the principle of his position as he. This will put you into the Path of Wisdom. Some day you will startle the world with your unlimitless power. You will show to them just as we sat last night and saw the story of 'The 

Merchant of Venice.' How many saw the wonderful true picture as it should be seen? What is the principle behind the great mind? It brought forth in mental vision a most wonderful lesson in Soulmind action. I will not go into the depths of this wonderful story but show two true characters, one a devout friend and the other living only for worldly gain, greed and selfishness-a lost hope, yet ready to give his life for his word of honor, the other ready to take that life for greed and animosity.

Then as the scene was drawn to a close toward a dastardly crime, how a master-mind came forth and saved the situation for the young man was determined to give and the old jew was ready to take. No one seemed to be able to see a way out of it. I can see the advanced Soul-mind of this young girl as she sits and goes into the principle of the case. At first she, too, could see no way out of it; but as she sits and ponders there is a picture being brought forward by the Soul-mind. She reads the agreement again and again and can see nothing she can do but wait. A picture and a question is being formulated in her mind. She reads the agreement again and smiles-she has been shown a picture and what a wonderful picture. As she sees the hand with the knife ready to take its pound of flesh, she sees it must be a pound-neither more nor less. She sees the picture as he began to cut into the breast, the stream of blood that must flow; then again she

reads the agreement and as she does another picture is being brought to her and she smiles, this time with a smile of victory, for the agreement does not call for the blood. What a victory! What a revelation! What a picture! So all she has to do is to get in such a position in the play so she can bring her principle forth. She does get in that position and as we look around the people in the court and watch their faces-everyone is sad for no one can see a way out of this brutal demand. But we see her seeing all preparations being made for the taking of the pound of flesh. She smiles within herself. 

Here is where the Soul-mind of Wisdom comes to the top and gains its victory: Everything is ready, the judge is ready to give his permission according to Law; but wait she demands silence, she has something to say and as she does,-let us watch the faces of the people, both of those who would have to take part and the audience. She starts out with a voice of strength and says, 'yes, you have the right to your pound of flesh and you may get your knife ready to take it, but remember, your contract calls for one pound of flesh, neither more nor less. If you take an ounce more or an ounce less, then you will pay the penalty. If in the taking, you draw one drop of blood in the process, you will have to pay with your life.' While she says these words, let us look at the face of the Lawyer, at the face of all his friends, at the face of his enemies. What a change; and then look back once more to she who uttered these words and you will see the difference between the Soul-mind advanced and the Soul-mind unadvanced. Only she could have brought such an ordeal to a finish. As she did, my young man, even as you

see this beautiful picture, so shall you someday bring such a picture before the world in truth and not in story as our great Shakespeare did. The Author had a most wonderful Soul-mind; and, as he wrote his play, he put a great and beautiful picture of advanced Soul-mind forth.” 

What a lesson in life; but how do you see the principle in all things so clearly? It seems no effort to you to understand and explain almost everything that is brought before you; and you explain it in such a manner that you leave no room for doubt or argument. Will I be able some day to konw as much as you?" "You can if you will; but you will always have to be on the alert for as you walk through life at every step someone will want to put a band around your head or you let them put it there but as soon as you are alone then take it off and throw it away. 

People will make all kinds of plans for you, listen to them, they mean well; but after you have listened to them, smile upon them and say 'yes, your plan sounds good and the motive that prompts you is real but after all it is me and not you. Since it is I, why should I not make my own arrangements to suit myself? If they do not plan out it will still be I and only I will be the victim.' You will get sound advice from others, once in a while, if you meet a greater mind than your own. There are advanced minds and master-minds. An advanced mind is a person who knows that life is real and lives the reality of life. 

The master-mind is one who has traveled on the Path of Wisdom and Truth for many years and has become a master of principle and in himself knows that he knows so little he realizes just how small

he is in this vast Creation; yet he understands all 

Creative Principles. There is not a thing he comes in contact with that he cannot look beyond the vision of the masses and see its truth. Any man can see on the surface; but the master-mind penetrates beyond the surface. Anyone can hear the noices of the material; but it takes a man of Wisdom to hear the voice of his Soul-mind as it speaks to him in 

Silence. Only the voice of silence can bring the greater thoughts of life forward. As you go back home, I do not say, make yourself a recluse.-No, mix with the masses, hear them talk, listen to the things they say, analyze their meanings and if you hear anything worthwhile, remember it. After many years, I still have fresh in my memory great sayings that I heard people say in my youth. I once heard a man say, 'and ye shall walk in the sparks of your own fire'-I never forgot those words and many times have they been brought back to me in life. Again, another said, 'Cast thy bread upon the water and it shall be seen after many days.' 

How true I have found that to be. Another said, simply this: 'a lad here.' What wonderful food for thought there are in these few words. Wonderful words, wonderful sayings, wonderful meanings, if you see the true principle behind those three quotations! As you quote them, you must see the principle behind each, for yourself. I am not trying to convey my true picture to you for no man can write his thoughts and mental vision so clear that the other person can see and understand as he does; but it leads that man to see as he should see for himself. I am not telling you these things that you might see as I see, not understand as I understand

for I am against forced thought. Anything forced does not give the same results as the things that run smoothly of its own pleasure. This applies both to man and material. Feed an engine oil and it will do its work smoothly and efficiently; but feed it sand and it will revel and create friction and undue friction retards its progress. That also applies to man. Give man his favorite motive power and he will go through life contented; but reduce his motive power and you decrease his production power. Just as the depression came upon our country. I was asked by a man of Education: 'What brought on the depression?'-I answered him in a few words that seemed to satisfy him. I will tell you, my young man, just as I told him. The reason for the depression is simply this: The producers decreased the buying power of the consumer; then how could the consumer buy the production of the producer ?" "Sir, that is something to think about." "No, not something to think about, but something to make you see the cause of the depression, through the principle, cause and effect. The two greatest words in the language of any industrial country." "You tell me that in time I will be able to see the true principle in everything. When you say everything, just what do you mean?" "I mean just what I say, the sun, the moon, the stars, the shadow, the rain, the strata of the air above us, the gasses by which we live, the reason why everything we have upon the Earth is here and their mission for the benefit of man's mind. You will know everything about everything. There will not be anything you cannot fathom. Creation itself

will reveal its true principle. You will see man in his true self and how some men use their wonderful mind in what they call 'specializing' in one thing.

We have lots of these men in the world, some specialize in one thing, some another. After you realize how big your mind can be when you can see the principle of ten thousand things and you see the other man condensing his wonderful brain down to a pin point of the things he could know if he were big enough to expand his mind instead of contracting it, you will wonder why a man can be satisfied to be a Specialist in one thing only. I myself cannot understand this; but you know as we think so we are. 

If a man thinks one issue is big enough for him that is him and if I want to know as much as all men and I find my mind is big enough-that is me. Somehow I would rather know a little about many things than know much of one thing; so as you go through life I want you to constantly keep before you these words: There is no limit to the mind of man; and swear within yourself that you are going to go the limit, if it be possible. To reach that goal, you find you will not have to think nor will you have to study nor concentrate, all you have to do is relax your mind, I mean your conscious mind and then your Soul-mind will take possession of your material mind and you will be surprised how much you will know in a short time. It is not necessary to read any book word for word to find the principle. In some books the principle is condensed into a few pages, the other part is just the leading up and the retracting. This may be necessary in a book but not in true life. If you want to reach a certain point, do not travel miles to get there, if you can go di-

rect. The same applies if you are asked a question, do not make a long story of it, get to the point at once and make your point clear so it cannot call for an argument; then you are not wasting time in idle words. Make your life positive in every respect.

If it is a question you cannot see through, then do not hesitate to say, 'I cannot answer it but, I would like you to explain the answer to me.' For example, if anyone asks you a question thus: What are the two greatest powers known?-Answer, Vacuum or drawing power and Force. These are the only two momentums known. By Vacuum you can draw, by 

Force you can press forward." "How plain you make everything for me to see and understand. What a wonderful principle you reveal to me. Am I big enough to retain all these things you have made clear to me this morning?" "You can never forget for as you walk through life they will come up before you at every turn you make. When put in the mind in mental picture those pictures can never be lost for the things that come before you bring them forth just as plain as the day you first saw them. It is getting toward lunch so we better go back to the hotel. After supper, I will see you again and give you a talk of the only way for you to get where you should be in the line of Educational Wisdom. The forced Education will disappear from your life and Wisdom will predominate. This afternoon, relax your body, let your mind alone and it will serve you faithfully. I will see you tonight." "I do thank you for the interest you are taking in me. I cannot yet understand." 

The afternoon passes with our young man sitting

in his room reviewing the talk of the morning. One thought after another comes to him. He feels as if he has to give vent to his thoughts and what else can he say, but, "wonderful and how vast; yet how easy to understand. I will go on. I will know all there is to know. I will do my duty to both this wonderful man and myself. I will not let the routine of life be my master, I will master routine." 

What a goal! Will he reach the goal he has set before him? We will see. 

The afternoon speeds on. Time seems nothing.

He dresses for dinner with a more vigorous feeling than he has ever experienced before, a new life indeed! He goes to the dining room with his mind. full, yet, seemingly, not full for there isn't any tightness. Can it be possible that he still has room for more of this wonderful thought that has been his privilege to listen to? "What more can he teach me than he has already?"

He sits down and his strange friend welcomes him again with the words, "Eat what you want. I did not choose for you tonight."

When the meal is over and they retire to a quiet room, the strange man asks, "How is your mind, is it still ready for more?" "Yes Sir, I feel as though I had never studied in my life; yet my mind has been busy all afternoon." "Did you try to force your mind?" "No, it was more of a building up process than a using up process. I can hardly believe it can be true." "The truth is hard to believe, sometimes; but as time goes on, and you let your mind work for you instead of making it work for you, you will find that

it will never get fatigued but as it gives up its thoughts it will be relaxed. There is a wonderful principle even in this; if you let it work because it wants to, it will find pleasure in the working; but if you make it work because you want to, it will get weary and tired by being driven against its will. 

It is all in understanding life in its true principle. 

The reason there are so many, what we call 'great minds' have nervous break-downs they do not know the secret of accumulation and they have to force it upon themselves. Someday, I hope the people who lead our young minds will realize there are minds of different capacity and they will deal out Education accordingly. Now you are going to be a lawyer. you know as far as you have gone in the routine of 

Education you were forced to use up lots of your brain energy for things and principles you will never need in your life as a Lawyer. Do you not think you could have used your mind to greater advantage if the useless part had been eliminated from your studies? When you go back again, I want to give you a line to follow, not the line of least resistance. but a line whereby you will give in your answers to the questions, and you get your own original answers. Go to the principle only, and if you find you will need this principle sometime in your life then master the principle; but if you see you will never need the principle in another given question then do not waste your time on it. Answer it, telling what you feel about it, go to the point in every issue. 

Be not afraid to put your own thoughts in writing to be read by any man; for everything we have, all the knowledge we have, was started from one mind what was not afraid to express itself. Realize

that you should know as much as any man and that your thought, if original, will convey to the other that you claim to give your opinion on any subject. 

Again remember, although, you should respect your teacher, yet know within yourself you are man just as he. You can know all he knows, if you read the same books he reads, yes, in any groove of life from the highest position to the lowest. Remember, you could fill in any of these positions if you were inclined to follow the same footpath that he has traveled; but since you can and will realize that you can do this make a goal high enough that there will be but few men could follow in your footpath. 

Get away from the masses, get away from the ordinary and stay away from routine or groove in life. 

Be not satisfied with only one narrow path in life. 

Make your life as wide as the east is from the west, the north is from the south, and claim to have the privilege of even climbing to the top of any mountain. Keep your freedom of thought and do not let anyone lead you into any channel that would build a fence on either side of 

I would say, you. 'let your mind be free to work for you at all times.' It will not fail you. When you are called upon to speak on any subject in the course of your lessons, do not prepare your discourse before hand but when you are called upon stand up and speak and what you speak will come of itself and the people will marvel at the truth of the analysis of your questions. From year to year you will have more confidence in yourself, and express yourself more freely. You may be questioned by many, but you will never have to seek for an answer, your Soul-mind can answer any question that may come from another man's mind; for is not

your mind as big as his? I say, 'yes, it is if you give it a free true deal.' Never seek the line of least resistance. When you travel, you will see almost everyone reading, trying to get the other persons thoughts so they may speak them; but you will, if you do not follow the Path of least resistance, soon find there is a book you can read at all times and anywhere and that is the Book of Creation. When you read it, you will find it tells you truth and nothing but truth. Do not waste your time, read the 

Creator's book for every leaf is full of Wisdom; and the pictures it paints for you are beautiful and cannot be imitated nor recreated by man for man is part of the book of Creation. As you travel through the channels of Education, you will be told many things that will not hold water; but in your position as pupil, you will have to hold back your momentary mental vision that will come before you. They will keep till some future day when you will be a pupil no more but a man for yourself. Then you can speak just as a man should speak. It is then the world will see you as your true self and not as the other man is." "You tell me such wonderful things and show me how great I can be. I hope I will not disappoint you, after all you have taught me and done for me. I cannot understand why you have so much interest in me." "No, today you cannot; but I am not talking of the youth of today, I am talking of the man of tomorrow. That brings another lesson forward, do not try to be as big as the big man of yesterday; but make up your mind that you will be as big as any man of tomorrow. Do not copy from the man

of the past, watch the great man of today; but aspire to be as great as he of tomorrow. Who would want to ride in the old automobile of yesterday, if he could have a limousine of today? Why do we want to always change the old for the new in all things material; yet try to hold on to the old things of the past? Surely we have men today who know more about all Creation than the men who lived a thousand years ago. We want to retard the mind in the mental; but leap forward in leaps and bounds in the material. Do you not think, my dear young man, that the mental mind should at least keep ahead of the material? We stick to old laws, we stick to old principles of lives, we stick to old teachings and are told that the old time thoughts are good enough for us. Are they? To me, no.

I want to advance with the Evolution of all things. 

Surely, there must be an Evolution of the mind as well as the material. If there is not, then there is a mistake in Creation somewhere. We are either right or wrong. We are either advancing or retarding for nothing stands still. If the minds of men today accept the teachings of the men of yesterday; then we are accepting the line of least resistance and that means no advancement but a falling back. 

The mind of man today should be supreme today; and the mind of tomorrow should reign supreme tomorrow. Therefore, young man, listen to the voice of progress of thought and progress will be your reward. Seek to have the best and latest in everything. Let the antique go to the antique; but I say unto you, 'be modern, analyze the principle of every modern thought; and if it meets your requirements, hold it until a more modern thought comes along.

Never let anything come into your life that you could not readily part from. Make no ties that would retard your progress. Seek, yes, constantly, seek to know more of this great book of Creation and you have a pathway of many lives and many years.' Tomorrow I am leaving for another part of the country; so I would have you know before we part, I will meet you again. Let me see how you will use the principle I have given you. I would suggest that you stay another week. Sit alone as much as possible. Let your mind work of itself. When it has revealed its great capacity of thought, even as young as you are, it will let you know how much more it will serve you as your material mind matures. You see a Soul-mind such as you have will never feed you too much for it just knows how much the material can stand. I will bid you Good Night and Goodbye for a time." "I thank you for what you have done for me and for what you say you are going to do. How can I ever repay you?" "The only thing that will repay me is: Be the man in the future I see you should be and I will be repaid only by the bigness of your future life. 

Good Night."

CHAPTER V HE LEFT HOME HOW, AND Returned HOME WHAT? 

The young man feels the absence of his friend the next day and being left alone his mind soon begins to reopen. The talks he had heard the last few days, the whole conversation, as he sits alone seems to repeat itself. He marvels at this, for he has not tried to remember; yet he has not lost one thought. This is a wonderful revelation to him and it encourages him.

He writes a letter to his parents telling them of his health and that he has decided to stay one week longer. He knows by that time he will have hold of himself.

When night comes, he goes to see a play. This time it is "Hamlet," and as he sits alone watching the play and enjoying it, the thought comes to him, "I wonder if I can of myself, see the principle behind this play." He watches it closely and becomes more interested. He hears the words of Hamlet in his distress making his speech. He sees the principle or the action of the Soul-mind come into play. 

At the end of the speech, he watches Hamlet so keenly that he seems to see the change of expression come over the young man's mind. He sees a new light dawn in the eyes of the once disappointed expression. He wonders what the picture could be; but he is not long in this suspense for soon he sees.

the issue. As Hamlet reveals to the actors his play that he has formed in his mental vision, he sees the truth of the plot is to bring before the King a play that is the repetition of the play that he has acted in reality.

Again he sees the revelation of the Soul-mind; and as the climax of the play comes, he sees and understands. Then again the Soul-mind makes itself clear to him. "Then we have a Soul-mind," he says to himself. For the first time, the Silent Voice says to him, "Yes, and as your Soul-mind is so shall your life be. Give it justice, let it work of its own free will; and it will reveal all principles, but drive it and it will not do justice to thee. So I would say, 'listen, make this thy daily motto: Look to see and understand. Listen to hear and the Voice shall speak to thee. Remember I am good company and always willing to talk for thee.'" 

He can not understand at first, he thinks he is talking to himself; but, no, he never has had such thoughts before. It must be himself. Does he realize he is entering the Path of Wisdom? No, not yet; but soon he will realize this truth. His Soulmind will then start on the upward grade. 

The first day passes so quickly that as he retires for the night, all he can say is, "What a day! Lonesome?—No, I will never be lonesome anymore." 

I wonder? 

Next morning. The same routine.

Breakfast. As he sits at breakfast, these thoughts come to him: "Eat breakfast? Why should I, when 

I do not feel hungry?" 

In a moment he is up from the table. "No, I will not eat because it is breakfast time, I will wait

until I want to eat. I will take a walk instead." As he starts forth his mind seems to say, "Routine, yes, for the masses; but not for you." 

His eyes brighten up and he begins to breathe more air into his lungs. He feels a new vigor taking possession of him. He feels like singing; but the song is within. His heart beats to the music of the inward song and he walks in unison with heart and song. How can this be? He thinks, why the change in his life? The voice soon says, "You have won your first victory."

He speaks aloud and looks around to see if anyone is near enough to hear him. The voice again says, "Fear not to speak thy thoughts aloud; for they are the true thoughts of a manly mind." After he walks for some distance, he feels like he wants breakfast; so he enters a small place by the roadside and sits down, orders a breakfast his appetite calls for and enjoys this simple meal. He feels as if he is satisfied. 

The day speeds on, yes, he finds he has no time to waste. Every moment of the day is filled to capacity.

Each day the same walk is taken, and he goes over the thoughts he has heard; and wishes to meet his strange friend, but, no. 

The week speeds on; and soon he will return home again. The boy who has left his home only a few days ago a nervous wreck; returns a perfect manly young man with a goal in view that no other young man in his home town has. Will his parents see the change? Will the people who knew him see the change? What will the Doctor think now after he has told his parents what he expected

would happen? He is wondering all these things as he prepares for his journey home. A new Creation, a new life, a new young man! 

As he travels homeward in the train he gets his first lesson from the book of Creation. He has traveled before, yes, over the same line; but he has never seen things as he sees now. As he sits alone and gazes from the window, his Master-mind begins to reveal itself. He passes the hills and the mountains and looks at the valleys. He watches the winding of the rivers and sees the sun changing the panoramic scenery and the people hurrying along the roads in their machines. He has seen all this before with the conscious mind; but as he gazes upon the passing scenes today, his mind is busy giving him his first lesson on Creation from the book of Creation. He drinks this in and feels a new Era has been given him. He feels that after all life may be worth the while. How different the journey home to the day when he was leaving home, with a mind over-worked and apparently ceasing to function. How miserable that trip had been; but what a contrast. His mind is now clear. He feels master of himself. He is just born anew. That is the only word he can think of to express himself. He sits and wonders if he can ever fulfill the life this strange friend has laid. before him. He looks upon the scenery and his heart throbs for the first time in his life. He feels a filling up, a sort of swelling within. 

He feels as if he must give vent to his feelings and he just has to say the words as they come to him: "What a Creation! What a beautiful world! What a wonderful enlightenment! How can I ever express myself?"

In his elevated mood, he does not realize the train is pulling into the station of his home town; but as the train stops he sees his parents there to meet him. It seems his father is different, his mother is different, their welcome is different. Why? 

Everything is different. Why? Because he now looks out of the eyes of Wisdom. He sees beyond the surface. Will he be disappointed in his parents or will they be able to understand him? We will see as days go on.

His father shakes his hand, and gets the handclasp of a man in return. He feels this and looks at the boy of a few days who was down, but says nothing. His mother gives him her motherly kiss; but she, too, notices that the kiss returned is not the kiss of the boy whom she left a few days ago, but the kiss of a man. She, too, looks at his beaming, manly expression; then looks at the father but says nothing. The same father, mother and son rode to the station a little over two weeks ago in a mood of distress; but today not distress. Mystery? Yes, he is a mystery to his parents, they a mystery to him. Why?

When they get home, the mother says, "We will eat. Everything the Doctor ordered on your diet is here for you.' 

He says nothing but sits down to the table. The father and mother talk and eat. He talks but does not eat. His mother notices this and asks the reason. "I am not hungry," says the boy, "I ate at midday on the train."

When the meal is over and they all retired to the

sitting room, the father asks the boy if he has the bill from the person who has made him well. "I have the bill; but I am the only one who can settle it so I will keep it with me until I can repay him." "What was the price he charged you. It must be quite a sum since you say you want to wait until you can pay it yourself.' 

"Yes, the price is big but I feel big enough to pay it some day. The price was this: 'you can only repay me by being the man in the future as I see you should be. There is no limit to your mind. Let me see you as near the limit as anyone; then you will have paid me in full for what I have done for you.' 999 "Son, he surely has set you a goal in life; but did he tell you how to get there?" "He put me on the path I have to travel." "What is the principle of this path he set you on ?" "It is the path he calls 'Wisdom.' It is the path he calls 'Truth.' It is the book of Creation he says

I have to read. He says this will reveal the true principle behind everything and it will reveal all mysteries and open up a channel of thought that will never run dry. The more you encourage it the more beautiful it will unfurl itself. He tells me as 

I go through my course of study to study as little as possible and to memorize nothing. To read only the principle then let my higher mind open up the principle for me in its own way. I can know as much as any man in every line of life all I have to do is seek for the principle. He also says when 

I go to college not to waste time on the things I will

not need as a lawyer. If I want to be a lawyer then go into the principle only of the laws. Why take in any side issues until I have mastered law? If I then want to take up anything else, I will have the same path to follow the principle. Father, I can see now why my brain gave way as it did under the strain. I was not using my brain with common sense, I was crowding it with other people's thoughts. I can see just why it tightens up the brain. Try to fill the brain from the outside and it will fill up quickly; but let the brain be fed from its own thought then it will dissolve them and put them back to the inner chambers for future use. 

The mind of man, if used for and by himself, cannot feel pressure. It is only the material part that can be overloaded. From now on, my mind is my own, my opinions are my own, my thoughts are my own, I will be my own power, analyze the principle of everything. If, when I become a Lawyer, I cannot solve a case for myself, I will not take the case on. No, not if I even have to consult you, my father. If I cannot see the principle, no matter what it may be, I will refuse the case; but if I see the true principle, I will carry the case to victory." "Son, you have changed." "I have had my eyes opened to true life, I have had my hearing made more keen and my mental capacity has been increased. I can see more clearly as it should be. I think I would like to go to your office with you every day, until I have to go to college again. Why should we have to go through a routine and be forced to continue this routine because some man had to take that much time to figure it out, and be measured by all other brains with

his own so he set the period of time we must gonot according to our ability but scheduled by the measurement of some man's brain-why should this be?" "Son, you are surprising me. I knew you were above the average young man in brains; but I never thought you had looked into these things so deep and seriously. When and where did you begin to see these things?" "Only since I have been taught to see the principle and to look below the surface into the interior of all things. I have been given a password to use through life and it will stay with me always.' "Then what is this password?" "It is: 'seek Wisdom and all things shall be revealed unto thee.' Can you see the greatness of this? Can you see the truth in it? Do you know this strange man knows everything. No matter what question you ask, he answers without hesitation and so to the point that it leaves no room for contradiction. He says I can know so much and even more than he does. It seems impossible; yet he says it is possible, if I listen enough and understand as I should. He will help me until I am big enough to stand alone." "Do be careful, son, and do not use your mind too much. You know it is only a little over two weeks since you were a nervous wreck." "Mother, I was, but, I am not and I never will again. I have been taught how to relax not contract when my brain is in use. I have been taught to give and receive without an effort. It is so easy, it is hard to understand; yet it is true. I was not hungry at meal time, now I feel like eating, where can I

find something worthwhile? Have you any cold meat? Could I have a nice sandwich and a strong cup of tea?" "Son, you must not eat between meals nor should you go against your Doctor's diet." 

He smiles and says, "I am not going against my

Doctor's diet for he said, 'beefsteak, potatoes and tea for long life and lots of energy;' and he surely knows what he is talking about." 19 "What Doctor are you talking about, Son?" "Why, the only one who made me well and ordered me to eat man's food and be a man. Hurry, Mother, I am hungry or do I have to get it myself?" "No, I will get it; but it makes me nervous. You are not the same. How changed you are!" "Would you rather have me as I was or as I am? 

Tell me." "I like you as you are best. What do you say, Father? We lost our boy, but we have in his place, a man. 

"No, Mother, only the making of one; but wait and see, I have a big bill to pay. It will take me a lifetime to pay it; but I will never stop until I dofor I would not be under an obligation to anyone." 

What a life ahead! Will he fulfill the trust? 

We will see as day by day he lives on. 

His first day at home, he asks his father if he can go to his office. Since he is going to be a Lawyer, why should he not spend his leisure time in looking into the Principle of the Laws of the country. His father gives his consent but warns him that he shall not study so hard. 

He answers his father thus: "My days of study are over for really, you know, Father, study is not

study in the truth of the word. You should use the word memorizing and I have been taught to not forget, not to memorize. You see it will be no strain on me anymore." "Son, you talk deeply. I cannot understand how anyone can know anything without study; but I will watch you. Maybe I, too, can learn something from you. You have told me so many things already that seem strange yet sound like common sense that I can not disapprove your philosophy. Do not hesitate to talk to me at any time. Give me your views as you see them. Know I am your father but I want to be a companion to you also. If I can help you in any way, I will be only too glad to do so.

You have the liberty to come into my office at any time, read of the books that will help you; and if there is anything you would like me to explain, I will be here to do so." 

So our young Master-mind starts out to fathom the principle of Law just as he sees it and not as someone else before saw it. He will have only his own interpretation.

Each day he goes for a few hours, looks up a principle or two then takes a walk into the country somewhere. He soon finds that his mind is willing to work for him without an effort and the principles he has read in the morning are just as clear to him in the afternoon. He does not forget so time goes on and soon he has to go to college again. This time he has to leave home. He is rather glad for he will be able to do more by his own free will and he finds it hard to keep this in the presence of his mother. She is of the material world only and hopes to see her son a big man some day-not of himself

but in the eyes of the masses. Each year when he returns home, she will see his new strength of mind; and then she may, perhaps, see him as he is. 

He leaves home and soon is in his work with all the strength of mind; but this time it is not forced -it is just as he wants it. He finds the principle so easy and solves the subjects put to him with such ease that his teachers marvel at him; but say nothing. They soon see that once they were the teacher now they have changed places in truth with this brilliant young man. They think he is something out of the ordinary. He seems not to study. He makes no notes, he does not write his addresses. 

He gets up before them at any time without the least preparation; and no matter what subject, he makes the principle so clear and easy that they can only sit and listen and marvel at this Master-mind. 

Soon he is the pride of the institution. When they suggest athletics, he smiles and says, "No, it is too hard work. Let the others have it if they want it, I came to the institution to use my brain-not my brutal strength."

They leave him alone to his own way of living. 

He goes once in a while to see them play football; but as he watches the principle behind every move, he soon leaves for he sees more of the smallness of man than he wants to. He says he would rather put the institution on top in mental rather than body strength. He sees no sense in only the 'survival of the fittest' to stand the knocks.

The year goes on. He carries everything before him; yet to those who see him he is always relaxed, no excitement, no worry of the problems to come. 

The professors feel as if he is their superior, but

try to hide it from the students. At the end of the season, he is first on the list. He is the leading light of the institution; yet he does not seem to realize what he has done to put him so high on the honor role. 

The classes for the season are over. He is glad to get home once more. He is just his natural self and has a personality that draws everyone to him. 

He is friendly to everyone; yet does not approach anyone. When anyone comes to him, he is the essence of kindnesss to them. His parents are beginning to see their son as others see him, and they, too, change toward him. He never speaks of the great things he has done but is always trying to say how little he knows to what he should know. 

The morning after his arrival home, he leaves early and does not return until late in the afternoon. 

He is then hungry and asks that supper may be served. 

They sit down to eat and his father asks him where he has spent the day. He smiles and says, "Dad, you see I am over nineteen and up to now you have had to keep me and pay all my expenses; so I thought I would go out and find a job this morning. I could then be self-supporting. I got a job in the factory. It will be a lesson to me and at the same time I will earn my own living. I want to belong to the wheel of industry for a short time; so if ever the time comes when I have to talk of the principle of Labor and Capital I will know what I am talking about. I will soon master the work I have to do, then as soon as I become efficient, I will ask for another job. I want to see how many dif ferent things I can do in the factory during this va-

cation. I think I can master at least three jobs a month-so I will know how to do twelve different men's work as efficient as they do it. Next year I will take some other position and so on until I have not only one trade and one profession but many. 

You see, my strange friend said, there was no limit so I have a long way to go; and I do believe that a man should not talk on any subject, unless he has mastered it. Do you not think this is right?" "Your principle is right; but what will the people say when they find out you are laboring in some factory?" "Do you mind public opinion, Dad? I do not.

It means nothing to me. I am out to know as much of life as I can before I am tied to a groove for life. You know I heard you say 'once a lawyer, always a lawyer.' If you cannot get enough clients then you can starve-not me. I am going to be prepared for any emergency I may find. Give me a wide area in all things. I want liberty, what do you say, Dad?" "You win, Son; but I could have given you something to do in my office." "No, not enough excitement, not enough to see and watch. I want to understand life from many angles, to watch the wheel of industry. There are lessons of life you can only see by becoming one of the cogs. 99 "Be careful, Son, that you do not fit in so easily and comfortably that you may want to stay." "No, I want to mix with the masses but I will never become one of them. It is just another principle I want to fathom."

His mother sits and says nothing. She can not

believe her son is doing such a thing. What will her neighbors think? These are her thoughts; but she, for the first time, is afraid of her boy. He has grown away from her. He is good to her; but,well, she will just have to stay at home and keep silent. What a mind for the mother of such a son. 

As the weeks go by, he continues to work and each week he is put on a different machine. He enjoys this labor. Why? Again, the great secret is revealed. Because he "wanted to," he did not "have to." Each night he goes home hungry and enjoys the meals he is able to pay for-for pay he does. 

His mother and father rebel against this at first; but when he says, "if you do not take pay for my board, I will go where I can pay," they accept his offer that they might keep him at home.

After supper and a bath he walks to a peak of a hill a few miles from home, sits there and watches the sunset and the moon when it is visible; and goes over the pictures of what he sees during the day. He very seldom has companionship as he says he can not tolerate their conversation. They can not understand him so he is more contented with his Creator and the Book of Creation. He sits at home one night with a large sheet of paper before him and on it he has many pathways. Each pathway represents a groove of life as he sees it. He has a pathway that is steep and hard and with many obstacles. He calls this the "path of the laboring class." As they enter this path you see youth, middle age and aged, all making their way to the places alloted to them. This is their world: To work in the morning, standing before a machine all day, looking at the same wheel turning day after

day; until their eyes get weary, their bodies tired and as their day's work comes to a close, you can see they are fatigued. The Silent Voice says to him, "Yes, they have to give beyond their strength so they might get something to eat to sustain life. A routine? Yes. Because they 'want to?' No, because they 'have to' day by day until you can see them leave with tears in their eyes. When you ask them 'why their tears,' they will tell you this story: 'I have worked every day of my life for this company, at the same machine, doing the same work; but today the boss said, I was too old, they were going to put a younger man in my place. I am going home without a job." "Do you not think you have worked enough? "Yes Sir, but we are old, we have nothing. My wife and I have so many children! but they have all left us and they cannot help us for they are in the same position I was in in my younger days. 

They, too, have all they can do.' "Do you not get a pension? "It is too small to live on; but that is not all, Sir, when you get old and have worked as long as I have and have not been able to save anything-then are told you are too old-only then will you know how I feel tonight, and how my wife will feel when 

I tell her. Why do we have to live too long?' 99 

He gazes at the other pathway and sees another picture. This pathway leads into a department store. He also sees life in its sad picture as he gazes upon the young motherhood standing there all day to try to provide for their families—just another footpath hemmed in by circumstances of life from the cradle to the grave. "Sad picture," he says, as

he looks upon them, "but too true." His eyes are being opened and he sees and understands life in all its sadness and sorrow for the ones who are forced into these paths. 

Then again he sees another path. This path is a little wider than the previous. It is the path of the business man. He, too, is on a path. He, has to serve the public. He, must enter this path and follow on until he gets too old; then he has to leave room for the young. He may have been successful; but does he like to give up the path? No, for he knows since he is getting too old for his business, he is soon to pass on. Yes, path after path so narrow you cannot step one step from it lest you fall. These paths we call "life." Are they? When will the people see these pictures and try to prevent their sad endings. Why can we not have someone big enough and able enough to put something into the lives of the people so as they near their journey's end they can at least say, "Yes, I gave the strength of my body but I have advanced my Soul-mind, and I see, not the end but the beginning of a new life." 

As he looks at path after path, he sees pictures of all the different paths of industry; but, waithe is looking more intently upon a new picture which he has unconsciously drawn: The masses are traveling on, pushing, crushing and jostling one another and they all seem in a hurry to get somewhere. 

So he tarries long enough to ask one of these people, "Why such a hurry? Why such turmoil? Why do they tramp one another down in their haste?" 

The questions is answered: "Sir, I know not where we are going; but since I saw the multitude moving in this direction, I, too, became interested

in their destiny and I am pushing and being pushed. 

What I am going to or where, I know not. Why should I care, I am with the multitude." "Oh! I see your journey's end and it is a passing on; but why hurry, why live and die without a thought of advancement, why go through life and yet never live? Are you satisfied with the multitude?" "Yes Sir, this is all I know so I will not tarry longer. I want to be in the hustle and bustle once more; but at the entrance of this wide path there seems to be a road leading off." As he gazes upon this picture, he sees that as soon as you enter the path it begins to widen out. As he gazes further into the path, he sees a sign and it says, "the Path of Wisdom, the Path to Advancement, the Path where there is no fence to keep you in." 

No, Wisdom means advancement, it means a clearer vision, it means a more beautiful world, its pictures are beautiful to look upon. The voice of the Path of Wisdom that speaks to you encourages you to go further and further into the treasure chest of greatness. As you walk upon this path, you seem to understand that all things are controlled by a principle. As you travel on, the principle of all things reveal themselves unto you and as you go you can read the signs before you. There is so much more to know that you do not know that you may seek, yes, ever seek, but you will never reach the massiveness of the word Wisdom. Seek Wisdom and more Wisdom, for did not the Creator say 'seek Wisdom and all things shall be revealed unto thee?'"

As he gazes upon this wonderful revelation, he

feels someone behind him. It is his father. He looks at the sketches and asks the meaning of each. 

As his son tells him the things he sees before him, his father stands and marvels; then says, "Son, you see true pictures and explain them wisely; but these pictures have gone on for years and years and as far as I can see they will have to go on for thousands of years more."

His son then speaks and says, "No, these things must be changed. We must strive to advance the 

Soul-mind of the people, both rich and poor that they may all see that money or poverty is not the goal set before us in life; but to fulfill the trust the 

Creator put in us and try to teach truth and bring truth before the people so we can lift the Soulmind into higher thoughts-thoughts that will let us see that all should live. We have a store house that is filled to capacity for all. Why should we not just labor for our needs in the flesh and turn our leisure time into the uplifting of man's thoughts? 

This can and must be done. Oh! Dad, if I could only get the coming young to see life as I see it, how soon we could make this world a better place for all. 

After all, this world is beautiful but we capture the beauty for the few and let it be marred by the many. 

Do you not think if the people knew the truth of Creation, they would be able to see life, yes, life for there is no death-only life and more life? Why should we retard the progress of man, the Creator's great gift destroyed by man himself that he might have power over the many? Why should any one man say to the thousands, 'you shall eat or you shall starve just as I say?' Is this thing going to go on or are we going to have enough Soul-minds ad-

vanced to stand for the truth and declare the truth to the people? Why preach fear into them, why talk of the end of the world, why speak of punishment after death, why work upon the weakness of our human brothers of the human race? I say let us have the truth, let us tell the truth, let us tell the people there is no death; but only a path to another life; and as we live this time so shall we have the advantage or disadvantage as we choose. If I could speak just as I see, if I could tell truth just as I know and if I could be rightly understood, they would see I am not taking anything from them but trying to give them something so great and so noble that they would be living a life worthwhile, with a goal ahead so far that it will take many lives to reach it." "Son, what has come over you that makes you talk thus? It is noble and beautiful as you put it; but can you prove your philosophy to the masses?" "I realize, it will be a big man's job; but if it is possible, it is my duty, yes, it is part of the payment of the bill I owe to the strange friend of mine -a friend, indeed, and a friend in need." "Son, you have my good wishes; but I am afraid

I will have to pass on on the level and come back to start a new life. I see the sense of that point of your talk and it sounds more reasonable than any other doctrine I have ever heard preached. I am proud of you." "Dad, those are the most pleasing words I ever heard in my life. I see, you, too, are a big man but too far-steeped in the material to advance in this life. Since you do not censure me, I am pleased more than I can tell. Soon I will be leaving you for

another term. I am pleased to go. I saw many things last year that will be useful to me in the future. I know I will see more this year. I have enough money of my own to put me through so, financially, I will be a man for myself for the first time in my life. I hope some day to repay you, Dad, for what you have done for me. Even the first part of my life, which I will always call the mistake part of my life, I thank you for. That was a lesson, indeed. I will soon be twenty, I wish I were twentythree so I could have my certificate as a full-fledged lawyer; for I still have my first ambition in my mind." "Then you still wish to be a Doctor? Well, Son, if you can go into the principle of the medical world as easily as you did the Law, you can be both lawyer and doctor. You are going to have a wonderful field of knowledge if you go on." "I am determined."

Just then the mother enters the room and the conversation is changed. She says, "Your birthday will soon be here. We have never had a birthday party for you. Why can we not have two in one, your birthday and farewell party at the same time?" "So that is what has been worrying you the past week? Mother, I am only a worry to you, am I not? 

I never do what you would have me do, never say what you would have me say; but this time, I say, 'go on with the party. I am with you.' Do not invite too many of the opposite sex.' 

"I do not see why, you are twenty," says his mother. "I am; but I have a big life ahead. You know,

sometimes, young people fall in love and that spoils their career; but go on, I am brave for once.

This gave the mother untold joy for have not her friends been kinder to her since they know her son has gone into the factory. They speak so wonderfully of him that she is ashamed of the feelings she had at the beginning. She is quietly proud of the boy yet does not show it to him. Why? Because he has excelled all the schemes she has laid for him. 

She wants the praise for his greatness but he has, unconsciously, deprived her of it. She says dinner is ready.

While they eat the young man suggests they have a real party. "Why not have the big room of the hotel and invite all the people in our circle." It would cost quite a sum but as he is the only child and he is big enough to work for his own living, why not spend some of the money they have saved. 

They, too, would enjoy it. He tells his mother how she has to be dressed and how he will walk among the people and show himself at his best. He will make an evening to be a memory in the city as well as a memory to her; but he, smiling, warns her that if he sees anyone making love to him he will instantly leave the party, for he does not want to hurt anyone. This pleases the mother and makes her all the more anxious about the great event that will be in honor of her son.

The night of the party arrives. He is the life of the party. His mother is surprised to see him do everything that anyone else does. He even takes a little drink with the men. Yes, a sport of the natural kind, a man in every respect; but there is no one he sees who falls in love with him, he tells his mother

when she stands proudly beside him while he is telling his friends of the wonderful mother he has. 

The party is a success and the talk of the city for days. 

He bids them Good-bye once more for a few months.

CHAPTER VI ENTERING THE WORLD OF LOVE 

He enters college once more and is met with a general shaking of hands. He is the pride of the school. He settles down to the routine and smiles to himself as he thinks, "A man, yes, and still going to school. Good labor lost. So many big strong healthy young men sitting with paper and pen when they could have done so much in a material way. 

A waste of time," he whispers to himself; but since it is the only path to become a lawyer he will go through with it.

The year before he did not study or memorize but went to the principle and found the real issue of each. When he was called upon to speak, both the students and the teachers knew they were in for a new picture such as they had never heard before. 

Therefore, he was not a student in the eyes of the others but a Master-mind on the Law. He was liked by everyone; yet he did not make companionship with any of them. He wanted to be alone.- 

Alone? No, he had good company when alone.

Had he not a Soul-mind that told him everything in truth, so he was never alone.

The session passes quickly, and as usual he is given all honors.

At the close of the session, the Professor calls upon him to make a speech and before he can he

ENTERING THE WORLD OF LOVE 327 

gives a talk about the wonderful young man before him; then introduces him.

When he arises the handclasping is wonderful. 

Everyone expects something worthwhile as always but he just stands there in his natural composure. 

When the noise dies down, he smiles and says, "I have been ordered by the Professor to make a speech so I must obey orders from my superior. I say this: We have a wonderful Institution with a wonderful Professor at the head of it and long may he live to hold his position. He is worthy of the position and the position is worthy of him." 

Turning to the Professor, he says, "I thank you, for giving me the privilege of telling these people how wonderful you are. I thank you," and sits down.

When the person next to him whispers and says he has put his foot in it, he smiles and says, "Well, he said big things about me and I could not let him have anything on me. You see, I am on the top, why back down at the last moment?"

He leaves for home and they all give him a send-off. He is the favorite with all of them, both students and teachers. They try to show their friendship in their own way. 

He is home once more. 

After the greetings are over he thinks he will take a walk to his old time spot where he seems to receive so much from the Silent Voice. He feels that it is a Sacred Shrine where he has the best of company. As he approaches his place, he sees a lone figure sitting there on the very spot he calls his own. He notices it is a young lady. What can she want there? Had she, too, found what he had or is she trying to get away from something. Well, the

spot is his so he will approach her and find out why she is here. He gets nearer; but she does not move nor did she hear him approach. Just as he is going to make himself known, she starts to speak her thoughts aloud: "How lonesome this world is among the masses. How wonderful it is to get away from it. How can the people be so false and cruel and deceitful? What is wrong with this great Creation? 

Will the people never see the truth? Can this turmoil go on forever? Why have I to live with the masses when I would rather live in silence with my Creator? My Creator, as you looked upon your work and said 'it was good,' did you overlook me that I have to feel so small and can do so little to repay you for your wonderful gift to 

Thy Supreme Creation? Why will the people not see the truth? Why can the people not understand 

Thee? Why do they not see the beauties of Thy gift to them? How can I live to forward the world truth? What can I do to relieve the suffering of others? Speak to me, Thou Silent Voice, and I will hear for I am listening, yes, always listening. I would not miss one word that Thou would speak to me. Time, what is time?"

At this moment he can hold his peace no longer; so he answers her question: "Time means nothing if you use it not unwisely." 

At his voice she is startled and turns to see who her intruder was. They look at each other, tell their names; then he smiles and says, "So you, too, have found the one spot of inspiration in this part of the world? This is my Sacred Shrine where I have come to listen to the Silent Voice for the last four years. I came home today from my year's

ENTERING THE WORLD OF LOVE 329 

study and felt that I, too, must be alone with my Creator for a while after months with the masses.' 99 "Then I better leave since you want to be alone. 

I am glad I found your Sacred Shrine. May I come to it when you are not here?" "Since you, too, are one of the Silent Circle and in touch with the Silent Voice, I think it would be well to meet and talk and listen to one who understands." "I am meeting someone here. He will be here in a few minutes so I will just move a little away from you if you want me to.' 99 "No, I do not. I am interested to know who the lucky man is who has the privilege of meeting you in such a spot as this. I promise you, I will not be jealous."

As he is smiling and telling her this, footsteps are heard approaching. He moves to leave her; but she says, "No, do not go. This is not a love affair, but my Master. He who sees and knows the truth of my life and has opened my eyes and taught me how to listen and shows me the principle of life. 

A Master-mind.”

While she is illustrating the man she expects, he walks up to them and says, "I expected to meet one, but I find two; but two only in the flesh, one only in the Soul-mind."

They both look at each other for the first time in a strange forward look and smile. The strange man then says, "Let us be seated. I have walked far and I am not so young as I used to be." 

They sit down. Do they feel as strangers? No, they feel a coming together of a Circle after many wanderings. They knew each other and understand.

The strange man speaks again, "What a wonderful Creation for those who would walk upon the 

Path of Wisdom. How wonderful it is to get away from the struggling masses and sit where you can breathe easily, listen intently and know that you are in touch with the highest planes possible! How wonderful for me to see two souls that have been blended in the past meet to continue the blending by a more nobler, more real blending when the blending was begun in the beginning. I am not putting you two together, I am just seeing the wonderful work of the Creator being completed in two lives. I do not have to tell you, do you not both feel more satisfied with life at this moment than you have been before in the few short years you have lived. I want you to use common sense, you both have a mission in life; so travel alone for awhile. Keep each on your path; but the two paths will become one in due season. 

As sure as you have met tonight so sure will you live this one journey to the end. You are as one; so my dear young people step not aside from the path you have before you for any foolish whim-then you will have no regrets, no reflections of a life wasted. Keep the path of truth before you. Watch the circle of light that will be a friend to you in all your trials of life. You are going to have many troubles, many trials, you will make many enemies, not through your greatness, but through the smallness of others. As these trials come to you and try to retard your progress, know that the light of the Circle is with you. When you feel you cannot go on, when you have gone to the limit of your endurance and about ready to give up; then you will hear a voice from the circle speak and it will say,

ENTERING THE WORLD OF LOVE 331 

fear not for I am with thee, you have fought well and there are many against thee; but thy strength to fight thine own cause brings me to thee and thine enemies shall be subdued. They shall desist from their persecution, they shall fall back from thee and thou shall arise in more strength: For thy Wisdom shall be increased.' Each of you have a great mission in life for a few years apart, then many years together. I am glad to see you both here at the same time. I hope you will meet often and talk of this wonderful thing we call 'life.' Yes, life continued."
