As he finishes talking, the young man tells his new friend that this man is no stranger but his teacher and benefactor. All he has, he owes to him. The three get up as one and the old man looks upward and says, "The Circle of love may appear at times, seemingly, to have broken; but tonight I see it come together in its true form. What a beautiful circle, what a halo of love there is in both of your eyes -the Soul-mind's windows. How wonderful this 

Creation is!" He speaks to his Creator thus: "Thy

Creation is wonderful, and so great and so perfect when seen from the eyes of Wisdom. We see some playing by the wayside. We see some actually laying down upon the path of life. We see some living falsely today. As we penetrate into the future, we see a great awakening. The masses are getting weary, they seem to feel there is something to live for more than they have at present. What a wonderful life there will be upon the Earth when everyone sees the path of truth in its true light and realizes that there is no death, that the circle was of the Creator and cannot be broken. No, not even by

man His supreme Creation. We may retard the progress; but as sure as He saw the finish so will the finish be-not as man would have it but as Thee our great Creator saw it. This is not a life that is led in its childhood into a blind alley and given orders that if it looks to either right or left it will have to take punishment for its daring to turn its head. This is a path that does not compress the mind, it does not blind the eyes of mental vision, it does not compel you to take anything from you, it leads you into the massiveness of this Creation. It unravels all the mysteries. It takes the bandage from your eyes. It tells you it is you that has to make your own path not walk in the foothpath of others. It shows you how to be a man just as you should be not to estimate some other man. It puts you into an individual position and makes you care not what the masses might say or think. It fills you with a flow of thought that never can flow enough to satisfy you. The more it flows, the more beautiful it becomes, the more beautiful the more you realize its truthfulness. When you see the circle of light grow bigger and brighter before you, it illuminates the path ahead, yes, even beyond the passing on of this body we now have and shows us the continuation of the path of advancement and truth. The end of the path is so far away that you will think you can never reach it; yet the path is so beautiful to walk upon that you never got weary in the traveling. It is hard and rough and it may look so big to you that you may feel like sitting down in despair and say, 'what is the use;' then the Silent 

Voice comes to your rescue and says, 'are you going to be a coward after you have struggled this far?

ENTERING THE WORLD OF LOVE 333 

Travel on, the path will be easier and you will gain more strength as you travel.' You see, it is against the common thing of life in the material life, the farther you travel, the weaker you get; but the path of truth, instead of taking your strength adds to it more and more. The farther you travel, the more you see worth traveling for. It pays you as you go along, you do not have to wait until you get to your journey's end. Your reward is given to you always.

When your eyes have been opened, you are always looking for the beautiful, the shadows mean nothing to you any more. You have lived, you have conquered, you receive your reward. As you look at the sun, the moon, the stars, the clouds, the trees, the flowers, the mountains, the valleys, the rivers, the streams and the rain they all tell you their beautiful story. The masses become a different race of people. Their expressions interest you, yes, everything in Creation speaks to you and how wonderful, how interesting life becomes. This truth takes you from the narrow path on which you are walking, it takes you to the desert and shows you the vastness of life's path you have to trod upon. You are taken to the mountain and life is revealed unto you; and as you gaze for miles and miles you know how wonderful life can be if you dwell on the mountain of truth and life all the time. If you have ever sat on top of a mountain at night alone at the setting of the Sun and the rising of the Moon with your bosom swelling up with emotion-you can feel but cannot express your feelings in words. When you feel the tears welling up in your eyes with truthful thansksgiving to the Creator who saw you as part of His Creation; when you think the silence is

so great, you can hear its voice clearly saying, 'yes, drink in your fill, breathe in this purity, see and behold the beauty of the heaven, fill up with the thoughts that can only come when you are away from the hustle and bustle of life-live, yes, live and rejoice in your very life, reap your reward as you go along, weary not in well doing. Tell others of the light you have found, tell others of the treasure awaiting for them, if they will only leave the fog and mystery and step into the great truth of knowing just what we are here for-not to retard or crush the lives of others that we might get to the top in the beautiful! What do the things of the 

Earth matter? If they were wisely distributed, they would mean nothing. It is the Soul-mind food we should go after. Listen to the preacher of truth. 

What is the preacher of truth? The Creator only and He wrote His book by Evolution, by seeing the step by step process of Evolution. So shall we understand the words of truth as I often quote: 'creation cannot lie, only man can do this for he alone has a free will to be truthful or not as his Soul-mind has been trained in the past.' If we could fill the 

Earth with beautiful pictures, have the sounds of beautiful sweet music, hear talks of nothing but higher thoughts; if we could do away with all smallness and in its place put greatness; if we could turn the selfish into the unselfish; if we could turn the receiver into the giver; if we could take the unmanly and change them into the manly; if we could let the world see that worldly gain means nothing; if we could let the oppressor of the poor see himself in the mirror of truthful manhood; then we would be advancing Creation to the place it must

ENTERING THE WORLD OF LOVE 335 

reach. If we could give a true picture of the great master-mind who lived in the past and could tell the truth of His life, His death, His resurrection and the people saw the true principle behind it all; then we would have a better world to live in. If we could take the shadow of life away and put the never failing light there then we could say, 'you, my great

Creator, gave me a trust and I can and will fulfill that trust.' My two young people, I have talked long but as it comes so do I speak. The night is getting late so we will each one to his own path once more; but we will meet again." 

He leaves them.

The two who met as strangers just a few hours ago turn to each other with the words in unison, "Do you understand?" For answer they do the most usual thing for two young people to do, and say, "Strangers? No, only being parted for a while just to make the meeting more sweet." 

They walk home together. To sleep? No, to drift back into the past and then live up to the present. A new light, a new life, a new joy swelling within them. A fire of love that can never be quenched. No, not till the end of time. What a gift! Will they value it?

We will meet them again in the near future together.

CHAPTER VII  

Next morning we see our young man up early. 

We can see a determined look in his eyes, something he has been looking into in the sleepless night he spent after the meeting the night before. 

At breakfast, his father asks him where he is going to spend the day, he answers, "Seeking a job. 

I am going to work this year again; but not in the factory." "What then do you aim to do?" "I am going to be a traveling salesman. I will not be home much. I am almost twenty-one and 

I have not seen much of this country nor met many people of different characters. This will give me a new field of thought." "Have you a position yet?" "No, but I will get one today." "You will?" "Well, Dad, I am out to get what I want, and 

I want this place, so I am sure of it." "I hope you do. It will give you the privilege of meeting a new type of man for there is no one like a traveling salesman. You will know what it feels like to be, as it were, thrown out. Son, if you can make it a go then I will think you are a bigger man than your dad. I tried that once-but what a life! 

I hope you will do better than I did.” "I know I will."

As he leaves his father says, "Good Luck." 

He turns and says, "No such thing," and away he goes with a smile and his natural self. 

As he enters the outer office of the sales department of the biggest firm in the city, he takes out a card, writes on the back of it, "I have a proposition to put before you, not for my benefit, but for yours; so do not keep me waiting or you may miss the great opportunity of your life in salesmanship."

As he sends his card in to the inner office, another man enters the office where he sits, and sits down. He greets him with a "Good Morning. I have not seen you here before." "How could you? This is my first time here for a position. They are after me to give me one." "Did they send for you?" "No, I sent my card in." "That means nothing. I have sent my card in every day for a month, and I am going to send it in today again." "The same card?" "Why, yes.' "Try a new card, it may change your luck.” "Trying to be funny?" "No, only trying my hand at luck." "I must say you have the gift. Have you studied salesmanship?" "No, I study nothing." "Do you not have a course and a school diploma ?" "No." "Then they will not listen to you. Have you any references?" "Not from anyone but myself."

The secretary calls his name. The other man smiles as he says, "Good Luck." 

He enters the office and sees a man sitting behind a desk. He looks at him for a moment then takes a seat on the outer side of the desk. The man at the desk is reading something and does not look up. The young man reads a sign "no smoking.'

He sits a little while, the other man still reading the letter before him; so the young man settles down naturally in his chair, takes a cigar out of his pocket, lights it, crosses his legs and smiles through the cloud of smoke. 

At the smell of the cigar, the other man looks up sharply and says, "Can't you read? That sign says, 'no smoking. 999 "No Sir, I did not bother to read it. I knew you would read it for me. I never try to stop the other man from letting me know how much he does know. 

Now I know you can read. I was beginning to think you could not read. It takes you so long to read one side of a letter." "Young man, you are smart." "My father always said so. Are you ready to hear my proposition or am I to waste my time watching you wasting your time? You know life is short at its longest, so we should not waste any of our time." "So you think I was wasting time?" "No, I know you were killing time; but why you should do this, I do not understand." "All right, let us have your proposition and tell me of the great opportunity you have for me." "I want a situation of salesman and I can tell you that I will never fail to make a sale where anyone

wants to buy. I will waste no time nor will I kill time. I want to belong to the largest sales department you have. Now your opportunity is to give this job to me. I have given you the first chance so there is your golden opportunity. Are you going to take it or leave it? Do you want me or do you not? I am not asking for a big salary. I do not need it. I thought I could work for you for twentyfive dollars per week, a company car and expenses, and a liberty as large as possible. I also wish to have the hardest men you know to deal with. I want to meet men who will say yes or no and mean it. Have you an opening for me?" "What did you say your name is?" "There is nothing in a name; but you have it on the card." 

He reads his name then looks at him, smiles and says, "Well, I would be a fool if I missed such an opportunity as you have offered me. You will be put on the salesman's staff. I will pay you the wages you want and the car will be ready for you tomorrow at eight o'clock sharp. I will give you a list of big houses of business with big men to meet just as you wish. I will hold the privilege of calling you in at any time, if you do not make good the first month. I think one month on the road will satisfy you in the life of salesmanship." "Do you think so? Wait and see."

Next morning, he is there at the appointed time and is given the list of places he has to go. He leaves for his first time to meet what the world call "big men." I wonder, will he see their greatness in his life of Wisdom as the masses see? As he travels that day to the first business house, he has to

call upon, does he, as other salesmen, formulate a speech or line up a point of argument? No, he is drinking in the beauties of nature. He is reading the book of Creation, he is receiving such an over-flow of thoughts through the Silent Voice that he is surprised at the things he sees and understood.

He nears the city of his first visit and begins to wonder what kind of man this can be about whom his employer had said, "If you can land the sale, it will mean so much to us, and will prove your ability as a salesman." He told him he had tried man after man, yes, old salesmen; but the man would not listen to them. He was what they described as a "lion" and roared as such. Can he find a means of subduing this lion of finance? Do not men tame the lion in the natural state?

When he enters the city, he remembers a college friend who lives in this city. He will look him up. He finds his friend's telephone number, calls him and is invited to call upon them as soon as he can. He loses no time, finds the home, is received by his friend and is soon one of the family-for his naturalness makes him at home anywhere. They have a pleasant time talking of their college days of last year and their aspiration of the future. His friend is surprised to find that he is working for his own education. 

When he asks him what line of business he is in, he tells him and mentions the name of the first man he is going to see. 

His friend smiles and says, "Why, you have taken on a man's job; but you will find you are up against a bigger problem than you ever had at school. This man is known as the lion of the business world. He stamps in his cage and orders the

people who want to talk to him out of his office. 

You surely have lots of grit. I always knew you had, but the last speech you gave at college proved it more and more to me. I have to smile to myself many times when I think how you pulled it on the Professor." "Never mind. Tell me more about this great man. I am to meet him this afternoon." "He is all right at home. His home is three houses from us; and he has the sweetest little wife. She handles him though. We are friends. She is a beautiful painter. Would you like to see her and look at her paintings? I know you like art of any kind, if I remember right." "What a wonderful idea. First, I meet the wife of this man, so big. Yes, I will go.' 99 His friend takes him to see the home and homelife of a man with a reputation.

They are received by a pleasant little lady who invites them in. The introduction; then our young master-mind settles down into his natural self. The paintings are beautiful and as he gazes upon one after another, he wonders how a woman can accomplish so much beauty and live with a man such as her husband is supposed to be. There is one picture that meets his gaze and he looks long and thoughtfully at it; and as he does so, he sees-what?-a picture produced by a mental vision of the past. The longer he looks upon it the more familiar it becomes until he can hold himself no longer. 

He turns to the lady and says, "Where did you get this picture you copied?" He notices a beaming smile spreading over her face.

She says, "This picture was never painted before

by the human hand. It is the reproduction of a mental picture I have had ever since I could remember what mental pictures meant. I carried it day by day, yes, year by year, untill I felt sure I could do justice to it. Do you not think it a most beautiful natural picture of an old time scene just as we might not be able to forget, no matter how many times we live. A picture that is so deeply imprinted in the 

Soul-mind that it will never fade away. Time cannot erase it from my mental vision. I had a strange man, soon after I finished this picture, visit me, through some friends of mine. They said he was a man of Wisdom. He was very interesting. When he saw this picture, just as you, he gazed long upon it but did not ask me where I copied it from. He said, 'I see now a picture of the past in the natural put into the unnatural; but even at that, it is worthy of the painter.' He told me other things that seemed to become a natural vision of the past, and as he talked that evening, he revealed the truth of life continued. He made it so easy to see, so plain to understand; yet he did not ask you for your opinion. 

Who he could be I could never tell-but what a night that was for me. Why am I talking thus to you, you do not understand, you are too young and have not met this man of truth. When I saw you gazing, I saw the same expression on your face as I saw on his-that, seemingly, looking back into the past. Can you?" "I am supposed to. The strange man you met once is my Master-mind, I have no doubt." "Can this be possible?" "It seems so. Sorry I cannot stay longer; but I have business to attend to this afternoon. How

glad I am to have met you, and to think that you are also one of the Path of Wisdom. The circle is wonderful, don't you think?" "Yes, it is; but we are so much misunderstood." "Today; but not tomorrow. The tomorrow will bring light and true understanding, we have no fear; but duty demands my time. How much I have enjoyed seeing your painting; and how much I have enjoyed your company and conversation, I can never tell. Words are not big enough." "How long are you going to be in town? I was thinking if you are going to be here a few days, why not come to dinner tonight. I know my dear husband would like to meet a man like you. He always tells me how monotonous life is. Every man he meets is afraid of him and how he hates a coward. I would like him to meet you.' 99 "All right, I will come for dinner and meet this husband of yours. I have heard of him but never saw or met him. What time do you usually have dinner?" "I have everything ready at six o'clock and he always gets home at five forty-five. You see, I have a regular routine and he just has to fall in." "I see. I will be here at five fifty-five. I know we will have a pleasant evening." 

As they leave, his young friend turns to him and says, "You are just as big as I always thought you were. How have you reached this principle you always bring in in your conversation? What is this past? What did you see when you looked at that painting? How can you be so natural and understand everyone you meet?" "You do ask the questions; but I have no time

to answer them now. I have to meet the Lion in his den. Won't you ride up town with me, since you are just killing time as you say you are?"

They reach the office where he has to meet his first man in business. He enters the outer office. 

There are a few men sitting around; but they have nothing to say. He takes a seat, looking around at each man. There is not a smile on the face of any of them. He takes a cigar, lights it and is about to smoke for a while when one of the other men speaks and says, "Young man, if you want to stay in here, you better not smoke. He," pointing to the inner office door, "would surely not stand for it. I would advise you not to."

The inner office door is opened to leave someone out and as it is the smoke just seemed to flow in with a vengeance. The lion leaves his cage, cigar in his mouth and comes out into the outer office. "Who is smoking in here?" looking at the young man.

The young man smiles and turns to the man beside him and says, "Tell him who is smoking. Can he not see very good?" 

The lion shoots eyes of rage at the young man and says, "He does not have to tell me, I can see.' "Then why ask such a foolish question?" "I will see you later," and walks into his inner office. 

The other men in the office say, "There you have made him not fit to be interviewed today. He will be on the war path for sure."

Another man is called who goes in for only a few minutes. As he leaves, he turns to the young man and says, "If I were you, I would go home, Son." "Not me."

The outer office is soon cleared with only our young Master-mind left-still smoking.

His name is called. As he enters the den, he smiles, takes a seat, pulls at his cigar, looks at the man behind the desk; but never speaks. "Well, how long is it going to take you to put this proposition you tell me about on this card of yours. Get to the point quickly, I have no time to waste on you." "You may as well waste it on me as waste it on yourself." "What do you mean?" "Only what I say; but sit quiet a while until I see." "See what?" "Just how big or how little the man I have to talk to is. No, do not speak. There now, you may talk, if you have something to say; and that seems almost impossible." He can see the anger rising at every word he says; but being a Master-mind and a natural youth, the unnatural does not have any effect on him. 

The big man says, "What can I do for you? 

What have you to sell? You are just wasting your time, I would not buy anything from the firm I see you represent." "Go on, I am estimating the capacity of the man's mind I have to deal with. I think I see the size of yours. 99 "How big is it?" "Not so big, not big enough to know what it is in need of or how to treat a man like a man." "How dare you talk to me." "Why should I not talk to you? You are not

deaf by any means, are you? I have a merchandise you need, and I am here to let you have it, if you are satisfied to pay the price. You cannot get the same any cheaper anywhere, so you may as well get at least part of it from the firm I represent. No, do not go into a rage anymore, it is hard on the constitution; and, remember, if you keep this up day by day, some day you will fall, yes, fall and then where will be your greatness? You cannot force anything beyond its limit without a climax. You know, even a lion in reality can be tamed down to calmness by a little sugar. Maybe that is what you need. Do you mind," handing him a cube. Take it, it is good for you." "Young man, you insult me." "Well, how many men have you insulted in your life time? This is my first, how do you like it?" "Who are you? I thought you came here to sell." "No, I did not come to sell, I came to see if you wanted to buy the things you might need. Do you need anything in my line? If you do, you can have it just for the ordering. If you are not in need of anything, then you would be a fool, indeed, if you bought. You see, I am not asking you to buy anything from me, I am willing to fill in your wants for you; but I have not much more time to give you this afternoon. Could you have your secretary find out what you need? Have her write them down, I will call again tomorrow morning." "All right, young man, but I will not see you before one o'clock tomorrow." "I will be here at nine, I have other calls to make tomorrow."

They both arise to go and each one looks at his watch and says, "Five-thirty already?"

As he enters the outer office, the lion looks after him and wonders if he has met what he has been looking for, a man. "He must be. How I would like to have a son like that." He closes the office and hurries home to dress for dinner. When he arrives home, he meets his wife, all smiles and gentleness as she says, "Five minutes late and just when

I wanted you to be on time. I met the most wonderful man this afternoon. Young So and So brought him to see my paintings and he was very much interested. He speaks so clearly and to the point. I asked him to dinner that he might meet you, John. Yes, help me with these. Now carry that to the table. 

He will be here in a few minutes. There he comes. 

I am all excited. We had such a wonderful conversation this afternoon. No, I will let him in.”

As the young man enters and the two men meet, they look at each other. Again, the young Mastermind shows his superiority for apparently he does not recognize the man of the afternoon. They are introduced by the wife and shake hands-as true a shake as the big man had ever experienced. While they sit at the table, the hostess is the main conversationalist. She is full of the thoughts of the afternoon and is anticipating more. She does not eat much. She is full of memories. She relates to her husband the story of the painting. As time goes on, he becomes interested. When they finish the meal, our young man drinks his glass of water. The hostess says to her husband, "Bring the young man another glass of water and do not forget to put ice in it. Yes, and bring me my glass of milk." Smiling,

she says to the young man, "You see, he likes to wait on me. He does so many things for me."

He returns with the glass of water and milk so ordered. He casts a glance toward the young man that only he can understand; then the young man smiles and says to the hostess, "You surely have your husband trained. Does he wash the dishes for you too? I imagine he does." "He always helps, don't you, John?" "The young man leans back in his chair and lights up a cigar and hands one to his host. He takes it, then turns to his wife and says, "May I?" "Yes, John, tonight, you may." Turning to the young man, she says, "I do not allow smoking in my home. I do not like it; but, tonight, I will try to bear with it." "No, if you do not like smoking, I will not smoke." He receives another look from his host, who then lit his cigar and left the table for a more comfortable seat in the sitting room. When the two men are left alone, neither has anything to say for a few moments. "You surely do have a wonderful wife," said the young man. "Do you think so?" "Yes, and I think she has a kind, gentle and obedient husband. She is calling you." "I know, I have to help wash those infernal dishes. Why can we not use paper dishes and throw them away after meals?" "You better go, she is calling you again." "I will get it back on you, you young upstart, wait until tomorrow."

As husband and wife come in and sit down again,

she is full of anticipation and he expects to have an evening of boredom. "How did you come to meet the strange man,' asks the lady?

He relates the story and then tells them he is going to be a lawyer for a while but a call from a Silent Voice keeps telling him he must be a Doctor. 

He tells how he found the principle and used it in his studies and how easy it is to see the principle in all things, "Even between the individual. This principle keeps you in your natural self and, therefore, shows up the unnatural in the other person. Don't you think," turning to his host, "the natural will always win?" "What do you mean by the natural? That seems to be a favorite word of yours. Is there such a thing as a natural man?" "There are a few and these men live by a principle of naturalness. What I mean by being a natural man is to know that there is no great men; and that position does not make the man. This can be seen in every day life. How a man has one side for home life and another for the public. If you watch closely, in almost every case where a man has an unscrupulous nature before the public, you will find him docile at home. He may rule his people by the iron hand; but his wife rules him by her tongue and position. If you want to see a man, see him in both places; then you will see him as he should be seen. We have a lot of men who have to be straightforward in their position and say what they mean; but why say it in a boisterous way when you could say it in a manly way? Why try to give others the impression that you are a man when your very words

and manner belie you? A man can do his duty more efficiently by manliness than otherwise; and we should never forget that the other man is trying to be a man and a success in his line just as you are in yours." "Say, young man, what is this—a lecture or a nail driving contest? Do you think you can tell me anything? I who have lived so many more years than you. 99 “No Sir, I do not try to tell you anything, I just say what I have to say. If the other man squeals then I know I am driving the nail home; but I am not talking for the individual, I am talking in a plain conversaton." "Go on, tell us more. I know my wife is enjoying it; and when you please her, it suits me. I may as well tell my wife we met this afternoon and that you win. I see my mistake. I have been trying to find a man who would come back at me for years. 

I found only one, beside you. He was not a salesman I just do not remember how it came about; but I know he made me take back-water almost before he spoke. He, too, talked of this principle. 

It must be a wonderful thing to know life as he knew it. If you go on as you are, I can see where you are going to be a wonderful man some day. You say the right thing at the right time. I remember this man meeting one of our biggest men in town who held a very high position and was self conscious of it. He knew he was looked up to by every one he met; but when he met this man, as usual, he tried to show how important he was. He tried to talk important to the stranger and it got hold of him. 

Position came into the conversation. The stranger

said, 'there are no great men. Position means nothing in the line of manhood for men in positions are the same always, each one to his position, each position has its master. Let us look at position: Take a big organization for example. There is the owner who sits upon his throne. He is the big wheel; then if you watch him closely, you will see he holds a ring in his hand. On that ring there are quite a few lesser rings and from these rings there are imaginary thongs leading from him to the highest position in his organization. If you trace them to the end, you will find them attached to the men one step below him; and as he feels inclined he pulls one of these rings and the man at the other end turns around and is told what he must do whether it is a manly act or not. If he refuses, there is some other man to take his place. You can carry that picture from position to position right down to the masses and every one is subject to the pull above him; so as you see the principle, again, it shows itself. It is clear to see that the masses only can create the position, not the organization as we are led to believe. You see, the masses control everything in the line of industry. 

As long as they buy the product, the producer can go on and that keeps us all in our position; but let the masses cease to buy the production of any industry, then the industry is no more. The men, no matter what position they hold in time of prosperity, become as one-men without a position. That goes on in every line of life. The world's depression was brought about by the producer reducing the wages of the masses to such an extent that the buying power of the masses ceased. Then how could the producer sell his produce? If we could see life in

its true principle at all times, there would be no depression. It is the greed of the few who in their greed for power and wealth bring on times such as the world has gone through the last few years.

When will man see you cannot take the wages from the masses and still accumulate? There is no common sense principle to it. Money is the motive power of a Nation; so let the Nation see that the wealth is kept in circulation and not confiscated by the few: For after all money is just a means of exchange, and once put away it becomes useless. Only in circulation, can it fulfill its usefulness. This holds good in every line of life. What is needed to keep anything in motion must be used as such and not laid away in its uselessness. Would a wise man buy a ton of coal and keep it in the cellar and let his house grow cold? What a different world we would have, if each one was interested in what he could give his neighbor, instead of how much he can take from him. Today we have too much take and not enough give. It is so much more wonderful to give than it is to receive; yet there are so few who seek to have the pleasure of giving, if it is just a kind word, a smile of encouragement or a helping hand in the line of need. When will the people understand the true principle of living and getting the most out of life?' "My Master-mind told me there is no limit to the mind of man either for the higher or lower. 

Why cannot man see the higher is more wonderful than the lower? How easy it is to be a man, if you want to. How easy it is to be efficient in all things if you find the true principle and the true principle is: Do as much good as you can and as little harm

to others as you can. Give everyone a chance to be a man as you want to be, and never forget that each one who comes into our lives has someone to live for just as we have. Then let us not make their pathway any harder than we can help. You see, you brought up a very interesting subject. I could talk on but maybe I will weary you and the time is getting late; so I must go to my hotel. I surely have enjoyed the evening with you."

The hostess says, "Will you come again tomorrow? I have enjoyed the evening so much and I am sure my husband has too; but I want you to myself tomorrow afternoon. Will you come?" "That all depends on how successful I am in my business tomorrow; but, I think I will be able to with your husband's consent."

Our young man leaves the lion and the lamb in one person.

The next morning he goes to the office at nine sharp and sends his card in and is soon admitted to the inner office. When business is over, the man of yesterday sits up in his chair and asks this question: "Why are you a salesman?" "Because I want to be. I am going to be a lawyer, therefore, I must go through college. It takes money to do this so I want to earn my expenses during the college vacation to put me through next year. As

I was traveling home from college on the train, I met a bright looking man who seemed to be all business. We started a conversation and he told me the story of his trip. He belonged to the same city as 

I so I was interested in him. He traveled for a certain firm and was one of their advanced salesmen who was making good. They gave him a new route

with a goal ahead. If he could land a certain order, which meant so much to his firm, he would be given a salaried position. He told me it was a bright light in his life. He had a nice wife and two sweet children and he, in his present position, could not be home enough to enjoy the pleasures of home. When his firm put this up to him, you can imagine how, for days, he had an imaginary audience with this man he had to meet who had his future happiness in his hands. What anticipation, what building up, how he would set out for this position that had so much in store for him, if he proved successful. He reached the city, all excited, full of what he would say; yet with an inward fear for he had heard the man was named as, not a man, but, a lion. He arrived and sat in the outer office, saw other salesman and the longer he sat and waited the more fear he had; but at last his time came. As he entered the man's office, the reception was anything but manly. He said the man snarled at him-'what can I do for you? Who do you represent?' As soon as he told him, he barked again, 'I haven't any time to listen to you. There is nothing your firm produces that 

I would buy.' He tried to get in with a word or two; but no, he turned his back on him and he had to leave. Man, what a man! By this time I was seeing a mental picture such as I had never seen before. 

I made up my mind, I was going to be a traveling salesman; and that was just a few days ago. Here 

I am. Did you get everything ready for me?" "Here it is." "Well, if anyone starts to tell me anything about you again, I will surely tell them a different story:

For I have seen a man under the disguise of a lion

bark. I will not take any more of your time. I will go; but remember, every salesman who comes into your office is a man and do not forget, by giving, you may give, unconsciously, a life, peace and joy to some wife and children. Good-bye. I am pleased to have had the pleasure of meeting you.' 99 

Shaking hands, the older says, "I am pleased to shake hands with a man." 

The younger with a smile says, "I wish I were able to return the compliment," and steps out of the office. "Big men," he whispers to himself, as he walks out into the street. 

Does he feel a victory? No, he has an inward satisfaction and further proof that the natural man can always conquer the unnatural. 

He spends the afternoon with the lady of yesterday, then they part knowing more of the life of the path of wisdom than when they met.

As he leaves the city to travel once more on the highway, he sees life in everything. Is he getting ready for the next big man he has to meet? No, that will take care of itself. 

He drinks in the beauties of Creation. "Yes, surely this is life and to know there is nothing in 

Creation that you cannot understand and see the principle behind, while others are scanning the surface of this Earth's beauty and are, seemingly, satisfied. How much more are they satisfied when they can see beyond-beyond, how far? There isn't any limit."

His car speeds on and so does his wonderful Soul-mind speak in its wonderful silent way. It shows him everything in its true principle. The day passes and night comes on. Once more he seeks

a place to stay for the night. Not in the city but in the country so he might sit alone and be with the voice he understands. "Night to the advanced mind is as new wine. It brings all the finer feelings to the front, when you are away from the hustle and bustle of everyday life. What a path to walk upon! Why don't the masses see? Is it the fault of the masses? No, it is the fault of the few who seek to keep the masses in darkness and ignorance of the truth of life that they may keep them subdued to the point of servitude. They care not how they retard the progress of the human race, as long as they get all the ease and comfort out of life that is possible. They teach you to submit now and gain your reward later while they are reaping their reward here. They care not for the reward later, if they have it here. Why worry about it in the future? Yes, I can see more clearly every day, the camouflage of life. Why can we not have real life? It must come.

Man must see life as it really is; and as sure as I live, I will, no matter what the cost, try to give light and truth to those whom I meet as I travel on. My deal yesterday was a financial victory; but if I put a little seed in the man who has so many lives under his care and have changed him in the eyes of the masses from someone to fear into someone to respect, my day's work was complete." These thoughts come running through his mind and he forgets about time. "What is time anyway when you are in touch with the silent voce? Time means nothing. It is what you learn during this, we call, 'time' that counts." 

---

MEASURING MAN 

357 

CHAPTER VIII MEASURING MAN 

The next morning as he prepares for his day's work, he feels the issue will not be so big for he had already met the biggest problem on this route; so he starts out and, seemingly, has success in every house that needs his merchandise. 

Night comes.

He again retires to his room and as he settles down and reviews the day, thoughts begin to come. 

The first thought is: "Why do we have to travel to seek orders? Why waste time and money? Is it possible that these men whom I have met today know so little of the needs of their firm they are supposed to serve that they have to have men call on them to tell them what they need? This is so unnecessary; yet it has become a wonderful wheel in the life of industry. What was the incentive in the first place? Competition, that is all. One firm trying to close the other down." He smiles at this picture and says, "Oh, well, I am in it for what it is worth, not financially, but to see the principle behind everything. Some principles are clean, some are otherwise; yet we have to see both and understand both before we can speak with authority for or against either, and it surely is a lesson worthwhile."

Weeks go on and he has to report to the office, he is told by his employer that he is a wonderful find.

He sends him on a new route the following month, then again on another route at his request. He says he has conquered the other man and he wants to meet as many men as possible in big positions in the industrial world. This puzzles his employer but he has no say what he wants he must have for they could not afford to lose so useful a man. The employee rules for once; yet, unconsciously. The older man says, "What a mind, what a man! We must not lose him."

At the end of the fourth month, he is called in the office and told that his success has been brought before the board of directors and they desire to see him. "They are going to offer you a promotion and be sure you take it. It will put you in line to rise to the highest place in this organization. They expect you at seven tonight. Be sure you are on time."

At seven he is there and is admitted to the room of the board of directors. He is introduced but left standing. The chairman of the board rises to welcome the young man and as he does So, the young man turns to one of the directors and says, "How long have you been sitting there, better get up and stretch your legs. I can listen to the men more attentively if I sit." He takes the chair, the chairman waiting for time to state what is on his mind. He looks at the young man and sees almost six feet of solid manhood. His eyes sparkle with a clean, pure life. The longer he looks at him, the harder it is for him to read the speech that has been prepared for him. As he is about to talk, the man next to our young master-mind offers him a cigarette. He smiles, draws a cigar from his pocket and says, "No, I grew up. "9

MEASURING MAN 

359 "You did?" "Yes." "Can't a man smoke a cigarette?" "How can he smoke a boy's smoke or I might say, a lady's smoke and be a man? Can a woman smoke a cigar? How can you be a man and imitate a lady?"

This brings a laugh to the board; then one of them hands him a glass of liquor. He looks at it and says, "No, I need not the artificial, I have enough of the natural."

By this time the chairman has to take his seat for he is tired standing. When the conversation ends and he has taken one more glass of artificial courage, he rises to his feet and says, "Young man, your unusual ability as a salesman has brought you before us that we might congratulate you on your unusual success. You have brought prosperity to our organization. We see a future ahead of you, if you will accept the position we are about to offer you in appreciation for what you have already done for us. 

You have been the means of us being able to give our workmen more time." "And the company bigger profits," chimes in the young man. "Why, yes," says the chairman. "That is what we employed you for." "If my memory does not fail me, I think I employed myself. Is that not so," turning to the man whom he saw the first morning? "Yes, you did. Sure you did."

The chairman than says, "In all the years in my business life and as a chairman of this board, I have

not had so much interruption. Young man, you are not here to dictate, you are here to listen." "Then get to the point and you will encourage no contradiction." "We are here to offer you a permanent situation." 

He notices the smile on the man's face and feels conscious of another blunder. Just as he is going to proceed, the young man says, "Not permanent. 

There is nothing permanent so why offer the impossible?"

The members of the board are on edge. Their leader is being corrected before their very eyes; and by a young man. What will be the outcome?

They are all waiting in anticipation for as long as they have known this man he would have no contradiction. "Well then, we are prepared to offer you a situation with this organization as long as it goes or as long as you live. How does that suit you? Can you find a loop-hole in that statement?" "Yes, I can." "Where?" "You said, "as long as the organization goes or as long as I live.' You, by your statement, may be causing a drag in your industry, if I should get sick and could not do my duty." "Young man, you are too far beyond me. I now see why you were such a success. You will not leave any room for doubt and stick to the point. We need you, will you consent to become one of us?"

He looks around the table at the men sitting there with their glasses of liquor and their cigarettes and smiles at the chairman and says, "No, the fall would be too far; but to make myself clear, if you, the

MEASURING MAN 

361 board, are interested and would like to hear the reason why I decline your offer, which to the ordinary man would be a great honor, although I see nothing to it, I will tell you. I would like to state my reason, so when we part you will all understand. 

I do appreciate the offer you have given me and if it were not that I have my life planned out in a very different channel to what you offer me, I would be glad to accept the position you have in mind. I am not a salesman, nor do I intend to be. My father is a lawyer and my mother the wife of a lawyer, so they naturally wanted their only son to be a lawyer. 

Ever since I can remember, a voice has been calling me and telling me I must be a Doctor or do some work in the art of healing. Suffering Humanity arouses my curiosity more than anything else. I am going through college to be a lawyer. Two more years then I will fill my father and mother's ambition. In the meantime, I am finding my own life in my own way, I am letting a still small voice lead me. What it says, that I do. It was the listening to one of your most brilliant salesmen tell his story of disappointment that led me to take the position I have had in your firm the last few months. Last year I mastered eight different trades in a factory in this city. This year I have had the privilege of meeting what the material world calls 'big men.'

I have faced them all in my natural way and as the natural always gains over the unnatural, you all know the results I got. So much did I prove my efficiency that you now offer me a position which I know to you is big; but to me-not big. Do you know what you are offering me?-an office, maybe twelve by sixteen, furnished with a desk, a chair, a

spittoon, an ash tray, some paper written by some other man, a staff of men to order and lift up or call down according to the results they get for their labor. Six days a week, this must go on, yes, ever on, every day the same routine, the office, the calling down or the lifting up, subject to my superior's orders. If he says, 'take So & So off the payroll,' whether it be for partial reason or inefficiency, I will have no say. I will have to obey the order, whether it be right or wrong. You might say, 'you will have a salary.' That part I do not believe in. 

If I did not earn my salary, I would be dishonest. 

If I over-earned it, I would be dishonest to myself. 

I would rather be paid according to the work I do. 

You may say, 'you are open for advancement even unto the board of directors.' As I look around this table and look at you men with your cigarettes in your hand and your glasses of liquor which help give you a little courage to go on with the business, would I want to take any of your positions? No, decidedly not. I want to have natural courage. I want to know what I am talking about. I want to understand the principle; so, gentlemen, I am sorry 

I have to say 'no' to your wonderful offer. I would advise that the salesman who tried and failed, not because of his inefficiency but the unmanliness of the other man, be called and offered this position. He is worthy of it. He needs it for his wife and children. He is worthy of all you can do for him. He will give his life for the firm. Let the firm give life worthwhile to him. Since I have kept this gentleman standing long enough, I might say to him, 'now you can have your seat.' Another little suggestion before I go, never invite anyone before thẹ

MEASURING MAN 

363 board without providing a seat for him, then always give him a few minutes rest to gaze around the table so he can estimate the size of the men he has to listen to. This will give him an even break. 

When he turns to the men around him to find the size of them, offer him a cigarette. If he refuses because he smokes a man's smoke, do not try to bring him down to the level of a youth or I might say today of a smoking woman. Since I have nothing more to say, I will leave you."

The chairman of the board goes with him to the door and says, "I am proud to meet a man.' "Thank you. I wish I could return the compliment," and leaves with a merry laugh.

On his way home, he feels that he must visit his sacred spot on the hill tonight. "I wonder if she will be there. It is four months tonight. I wonder?" 

When he arrives home, he is greeted by his mother and father. The mother says, "Son, we do not see much of you." "No, Mother, you want your son to be a man and the only way is to meet the world face to face and read between the lines as you go along." "I am proud of you," says the father. "You surely have the business world talking about you in this town. Did you accept the position offered you?" "No, Dad, it is not big enough. I want a man's job not a make-believe one. I will never draw a salary that I do not honestly earn; and how can a man be honest when he has to push the other man?" "I am afraid some day you will change your mind in money matters. You may have a big mind; but you also need an income." "Dad, money seems the easiest thing to get.

Produce the goods the people need and money is the last consideration. You may have money but give me wisdom, power and a love for suffering humanity and let the life of money take its course. 99 

His father is apparently surprised but satisfied for he says, "You know more about life than I. I have no fear of your future."

He leaves to go to his shrine where he hopes to meet someone whom he has thought about the last few days; yet he consoles himself, if he is disappointed in the one he will not be disappointed in the other for he knows the Silent Voice will not or can not have disappointed him. So with a light heart and a smiling soul, he wends his way up to the hill top. He tries to penetrate the darkness, so eager is he to see. As he draws nearer he sees the outline of a lone figure sitting on the very spot where he had seen the picture four months before. He tiptoes toward her. She is unconscious of his approach is talking to herself. He hears her saying, "Surely, he will be here tonight. Yes, I know he will for I feel him so near to me.' 

These words put him in a peculiar position; but he steals back from her silently then walks toward her with a firm step so she may hear his approach. 

When she hears him, she turns, rises from her seat and comes toward him. They shake hands. She says, "I felt you would come tonight? This is the first time I have been able to get here since I saw you last." "This is my first time also; but I, too, felt I had to come tonight.' 99 "So here we are. Let us sit down. Do you want me to stay?"

365 "Do you want me to stay?" he says. "I surely do." 

They sit down and realize they are unconsciously, holding each other's hand. 

He says, "How natural it is. Why have you not been here since last we met?" "I would have to tell you a sad story. I would rather talk about the great truth of life and listen to what you have been doing in the last four months.

I have heard lots of wonderful things about you and I am anxious to hear your greatness from yourself." "Do you not know a man of Wisdom sees nothing great he does, neither does he see greatness in anyone but his Creator and His son who laid the Path of Truth bare before us in the world of the material. In the eyes of the material, there may be things and men who appear to them as great but when you travel the path of wisdom, when you understand the principle of this unseen great power, then how can we see anything great in the material?

Creation had not provided the material? Man's mind is wonderful. When you see the uses to which he has put the material things of Creation into action, we must give credit to the Soul-mind of man. 

When you see them used, not for the benefit of the masses but for the benefit of only the few, when we see such unequal distribution, then it makes us wonder why these things are. As we penetrate the beginning of this unequal principle, those chosen few try to tell us that they have a right to this great portion and the masses are not worthy of their hire only as they see them, then our thoughts naturally

travel to the great Creator and we are yet to wonder if He did create the world for the few and not the many. If He did fill the storehouse with everything wonderful that the few chosen might live in luxury while the many wallow in distress and poverty. As we see this picture, it surely makes us think; but as we follow the path of wisdom it is only then we can see the true principle of Creation. He did not choose a select few, He did give the things of the Earth that man might have at least food to eat and clothes to wear and a shelter worthwhile to live in. The inequality has been brought about by men who are afraid of pain and punishment or, in other words, we have sought the line of least resistance, we submit to lose our manhood so we can have comfort and luxury by the selling of ourselves to others. Why man can cater to another man because he holds what we call 'position' so he might smile on us is more than I can understand. Why cater for a smile? I never want a smile from the other man if I have to force it. That is not a smile, it is a joke; and life is too serious to have jokes like these. I surely have seen man or what the masses call men in the last four months. What a revelation! I, sometimes, wonder if the world will ever get out of this mess of camouflage it has gotten in. I think the world has manufactured more of the make-believe than it knew before. It has such a hold, it will take hundreds of years for the masses to see through the fog this make-believe has put them in. You may think I am talking too much; but I just have to give vent to my feelings tonight. Why do they encourage advancement in everything material and condemn everything that would advance the higher mind of man? If we do not know more of the Earth today than the men of two thousand years ago then where is our advancement?

Nothing in Creation stands still. It is either going up or down, it is either active or dead, there is no in between. This is true in everything, we are either good or bad, sick or well, therefore, if we go by the teaching of our forefathers, we are not going forward but going back to the time in which they lived.

Since we would not go back to that material life such as they had, why do we still live in the same mystery, the same fog, the same teachings in which they lived. We watch them the next day back among the masses. They look at them and each asks the question within themselves: "Is it possible this is the same picture we have seen for years in the past?

We get so mixed with so many paths that to choose the right one is too much for us. Every way we go, we are greeted with so much unnaturalness that we feel like giving up and sit down and wait. Of course, I would say, let us give our thoughts to others so we will not be reduced to one point of thought; but let us not condemn any one thought until we have read them or heard them from the foundation of the thought. If we condemn a man without a thorough knowledge of his thoughts then we are judging unwisely. Never pick up or read a book with a biased attitude. If you do, you are false to yourself and unjust to the man who wrote it. It will not display your greatness but will show your smallness. Can we advance the thinking world by holding them down? Can I not think as much as you? Can you not think as much as me? Only by the exchange of thought can we bring thoughts to the front worthwhile. Let us leave the fear out of life and act as a man should-naturally. Let us tell the masses that the environment of life should not retard the progress of their Soul-mind thoughts.

Can man tell us what the Creator meant us to do.

If he has gone so far down that he cannot help himself; then it is the duty of the other to try to lift him up not by creating a fear, but by showing him how big he might be if he wanted to be. If a man has to be persuaded to be good, then he is not good. It is only when he wants to be that he can be.” 

As these thoughts flow through the young man's mind, it gives him another vision of real life. I say, no, he would not be using common sense. Since the Creator finished this world in His mental vision of Creation, then started Evolution as a painter and Evolution brought man forth to finish His Creation; and because man retarded the progress as He saw it should go on, would He rise up in anger towards Evolution and man and destroy this beautiful picture? Did 

He expect man to see as He saw? How could man see as He saw when it was He who brought man forth?" The young man is surprised at this new revelation; yet he sees another lesson in life and

377 dom," are his thoughts when he reaches home that night after spending a day with the material yet receiving constantly from the Soul-mind. 99 When his mother looks at him, she says, "Son, I do not understand you; but I am proud of you. I say 'wander' because there is no set purpose in the life of the masses. I see everyone trying to take as much as possible from his fellow-man. I see people pretending friendship where there is no friendship. I see people offering things to other people and, if they take it, it brings them sorrow. Why do the people live such lives? It is a wide path, it leads into expansion and I want to expand-not compress. The path of Wisdom is vast and to cover only a small portion of it will take me a lifetime; but I must leave you, I must go out again."

I think he is a Creation of himself, but what a man
